RULE 2: If the integer source operand is not in the valid input set, then executing FCVT.D.W raises the same exception (e.g., invalid‑conversion exception) and writes the same destination floating‑point register value and fflags as an FCVT.int.D (or FCVT.int.S) instruction would for that operand.
RULE 3: FCVT.D.W must produce an exact conversion result regardless of the rounding mode specified in the rm field.
RULE 4: The rounding mode field (rm) has no effect on the result of FCVT.D.W.
RULE 5: If FCVT.D.W is executed, then it never raises an illegal‑instruction or other privilege‑level exception regardless of the current privilege mode; only the standard input‑range exception (invalid‑conversion, setting mcause accordingly) may be raised when the source integer value is out of the representable range for double‑precision.

INSTRUCTION: FCVT.D.WU
FORMAT: fcvt.d.wu fd, rs1, rm
RULE 1: FCVT.D.WU reads an unsigned integer value from integer register rs1 (32-bit on RV32, 32- or 64-bit on RV64) and writes the converted double-precision floating-point value to floating-point register rd.
RULE 2: FCVT.D.WU converts the unsigned integer operand to a double-precision floating-point value and the conversion always yields an exact result, ignoring the rounding mode specified by the rm field.
RULE 3: The set of input values for which FCVT.D.WU produces a defined result and the architectural response to inputs outside that set are identical to those defined for the FCVT.int.S instruction.
RULE 4: FCVT.D.WU produces a double-precision floating-point result and no sign-extension of the value is performed.

INSTRUCTION: FCVT.H.D
FORMAT: fcvt.h.d fd, fs1, rm
RULE 1: The FCVT.H.D instruction MUST be decoded when opcode=OP-FP, funct5=5, fmt=H, rs2=D, with any values for rs1, rd, and rm.
RULE 2: The FCVT.H.D instruction is only defined when the D extension is implemented; if the D extension is not implemented the encoding is reserved.
RULE 3: The FCVT.H.D instruction MUST convert the read double-precision floating-point value to a half-precision floating-point value using the rounding mode specified by the rm field.
RULE 4: The FCVT.H.D instruction MUST write the resulting half-precision floating-point value to the destination floating-point register indicated by rd.
RULE 5: The FCVT.H.D instruction MUST select its rounding mode according to the standard floating-point rounding-mode field rm.
RULE 6: If the processor does not implement both the Zfhmin and D extensions, then executing an FCVT.H.D instruction raises an illegal‑instruction exception (mcause set to the illegal‑instruction code).

INSTRUCTION: FCVT.H.L
FORMAT: fcvt.h.l fd, rs1, rm
RULE 1: If XLEN ≠ 64 and an FCVT.H.L instruction is executed, then the core raises an illegal‑instruction exception (mcause = 2).
RULE 2: FCVT.H.L converts the signed 64-bit integer value in the integer source register rs1 to a half-precision floating-point value and writes that result to the floating-point destination register rd.
RULE 3: FCVT.H.L uses the rounding mode specified by the 3-bit rm field to round the exact conversion result to the nearest representable half-precision value.
RULE 4: FCVT.H.L exhibits the same rounding, overflow, underflow, inexact, and invalid-operation exception behavior as the single-precision integer-to-floating-point conversion instructions.
RULE 5: FCVT.H.L is encoded as an OP-FP instruction with funct5 = FCVT.H.int, fmt = H, rs2 = L, rs1 = the integer source register, rm = the rounding-mode field, rd = the floating-point destination register, and opcode = OP-FP; any deviation from this encoding yields a reserved encoding.

INSTRUCTION: FCVT.H.LU
FORMAT: fcvt.h.lu fd, rs1, rm
RULE 1: FCVT.H.LU must be executed only on RV64 implementations; executing it on an RV32 implementation causes an illegal instruction exception.
RULE 2: For FCVT.H.LU the rs2 field must be encoded as L[U] to select conversion from an unsigned 64-bit integer source.
RULE 3: FCVT.H.LU uses the rm field to specify the rounding mode that is applied during the conversion from integer to half-precision floating-point.
RULE 4: FCVT.H.LU writes the half-precision floating-point result into the floating-point register specified by rd.
RULE 5: The half-precision result written by FCVT.H.LU is the value of the unsigned integer operand from rs1 converted according to the rounding mode indicated by rm.
RULE 6: FCVT.H.LU requires the fmt field to be set to H, indicating that the conversion result is in half-precision format.

INSTRUCTION: FCVT.H.Q
FORMAT: fcvt.h.q fd, fs1, rm
RULE 1: The rm field of the FCVT.H.Q encoding selects the rounding mode used for the conversion.
RULE 2: The conversion performed by FCVT.H.Q must follow the same architectural rules as the double-precision floating-point-to-floating-point conversion instructions, applied between half-precision and quad-precision formats.

INSTRUCTION: FCVT.H.S
FORMAT: fcvt.h.s fd, fs1, rm
RULE 1: If the Zfhmin standard extension is implemented, the FCVT.H.S instruction MUST be implemented.
RULE 2: FCVT.H.S is only available when the Zfinx extension is implemented, because the Zhinxmin extension depends upon the Zfinx extension.

INSTRUCTION: FCVT.H.W
FORMAT: fcvt.h.w fd, rs1, rm
RULE 1: FCVT.H.W MUST convert the 32-bit signed integer value read from integer register rs1 into a half-precision floating-point number and write the resulting value to floating-point register rd.
RULE 2: FCVT.H.W MUST use the rm field of the instruction to select the rounding mode applied during the integer-to-floating-point conversion.

INSTRUCTION: FCVT.H.WU
FORMAT: fcvt.h.wu fd, rs1, rm
RULE 1: FCVT.H.WU converts the 32-bit unsigned integer value in the integer source register rs1 to a half-precision floating-point value and writes the result to the floating-point destination register rd.
RULE 2: FCVT.H.WU interprets the source integer register value as an unsigned integer, ignoring any sign extension.
RULE 3: FCVT.H.WU applies the rounding mode specified in the rm field to the conversion according to the IEEE-754 rounding rules for floating-point conversions.
RULE 4: In RV64, the unsigned 64-bit integer conversion is provided by the separate instruction FCVT.H.LU; FCVT.H.WU still converts only a 32-bit unsigned integer.
RULE 5: If FCVT.H.WU is executed and a condition that would cause a standard floating‑point conversion exception occurs (e.g., the F‑extension is disabled, the instruction is not implemented, or an invalid rounding mode is used), then the same exception is raised (such as an illegal‑instruction exception or the appropriate floating‑point exception with the corresponding mcause); otherwise, FCVT.H.WU raises no additional exceptions beyond those standard conversion exceptions.

INSTRUCTION: FCVT.L.D
FORMAT: fcvt.l.d rd, fs1, rm
RULE 1: FCVT.L.D converts the double-precision floating-point value in floating-point register rs1 to a signed 64-bit integer and writes the result to integer register rd.
RULE 2: FCVT.L.D rounds the conversion result according to the rounding mode specified in the rm field of the instruction.
RULE 3: FCVT.L.D must apply the same range of valid inputs and the same handling of invalid inputs as defined for the single-precision floating-point-to-integer conversion instruction.

INSTRUCTION: FCVT.L.H
FORMAT: fcvt.l.h rd, fs1, rm
RULE 1: FCVT.L.H MUST convert the half-precision floating-point value held in the source register to a signed 64-bit integer and write the resulting integer to the destination register.
RULE 2: FCVT.L.H uses the rounding mode specified in the rm field to round the source half-precision floating-point value before performing the conversion to a signed 64-bit integer.
RULE 3: FCVT.L.H is defined only for RV64 implementations and is not a valid instruction on RV32 systems.

INSTRUCTION: FCVT.L.Q
FORMAT: fcvt.l.q rd, fs1, rm
RULE 1: FCVT.L.Q converts a quad-precision floating-point number in the source floating-point register to a signed 64-bit integer placed in the destination integer register, using the current rounding mode.
RULE 2: FCVT.L.Q follows the same conversion semantics as the double-precision-to-integer conversion instructions, including overflow, NaN, and rounding behavior defined by the current rounding mode.
RULE 3: FCVT.L.Q is defined only for RV64 implementations; its encoding is reserved on RV32 implementations.

INSTRUCTION: FCVT.L.S
FORMAT: fcvt.l.s rd, fs1, rm
RULE 1: FCVT.L.S converts the floating-point number in source register rs1 to a signed 64-bit integer placed in destination integer register rd.
RULE 2: FCVT.L.S must round the source floating-point value according to the rounding-mode field rm.
RULE 3: FCVT.L.S must set the Invalid exception flag when the rounded result is not representable in the signed 64-bit integer format.
RULE 4: When the Invalid flag is set, FCVT.L.S must clip the result to the nearest representable signed 64-bit integer value.
RULE 5: FCVT.L.S must produce –2^63 as the result for any input whose rounded value is less than –2^63, including out-of-range negative inputs.
RULE 6: FCVT.L.S must produce 2^63-1 as the result for any input whose rounded value exceeds 2^63-1, including out-of-range positive inputs.
RULE 7: FCVT.L.S must produce –2^63 as the result when the input is negative infinity (–∞).
RULE 8: FCVT.L.S must produce 2^63-1 as the result when the input is positive infinity (+∞).
RULE 9: FCVT.L.S must produce 2^63-1 as the result when the input is a NaN.
RULE 10: FCVT.L.S must set the Inexact exception flag if the rounded result differs from the original operand value and the Invalid flag is not set.

INSTRUCTION: FCVT.LU.D
FORMAT: fcvt.lu.d rd, fs1, rm
RULE 1: FCVT.LU.D must convert the double-precision floating-point value held in floating-point register rs1 to an unsigned integer value and write the result to integer register rd.
RULE 2: FCVT.LU.D is defined only on implementations with XLEN = 64 and is not defined for RV32 (RV64-only).
RULE 3: FCVT.LU.D must treat as valid exactly the double-precision floating-point inputs that are valid for the FCVT.int.S instruction.
RULE 4: For any double-precision floating-point input that is invalid for FCVT.int.S, FCVT.LU.D must produce the same architectural result as FCVT.int.S (e.g., raise the same exception).
RULE 5: FCVT.LU.D must round the conversion result according to the rounding mode specified in the rm field of the instruction.

INSTRUCTION: FCVT.LU.H
FORMAT: fcvt.lu.h rd, fs1, rm
RULE 1: FCVT.LU.H MUST convert a half-precision floating-point source operand to an unsigned 64-bit integer result.
RULE 2: FCVT.LU.H is defined only for RV64 implementations and is not defined for RV32.
RULE 3: In the encoding of FCVT.LU.H the rm field supplies the rounding mode used for the conversion.

INSTRUCTION: FCVT.LU.Q
FORMAT: fcvt.lu.q rd, fs1, rm
RULE 1: FCVT.LU.Q converts a quad-precision floating-point number to an unsigned integer value.

INSTRUCTION: FCVT.LU.S
FORMAT: fcvt.lu.s rd, fs1, rm
RULE 1: FCVT.LU.S MUST convert the single-precision floating-point value in floating-point register rs1 to an unsigned 64-bit integer written to integer register rd.
RULE 2: FCVT.LU.S MUST be defined only for RV64 implementations and does not exist in RV32.
RULE 3: FCVT.LU.S MUST round the source floating-point value according to the rounding mode specified in the rm field of the instruction.
RULE 4: If the rounded value produced by FCVT.LU.S is not representable in the unsigned 64-bit integer format, FCVT.LU.S MUST clip the result to the nearest representable unsigned 64-bit value and MUST set the invalid exception flag.
RULE 5: When the rounded input to FCVT.LU.S is the minimum valid input (after rounding), FCVT.LU.S MUST produce the result 0.
RULE 6: When the rounded input to FCVT.LU.S is the maximum valid input (after rounding), FCVT.LU.S MUST produce the result 2^64−1.
RULE 7: For any out-of-range negative input to FCVT.LU.S, the instruction MUST produce the result 0.
RULE 8: When the input to FCVT.LU.S is negative infinity, FCVT.LU.S MUST produce the result 0.
RULE 9: For any out-of-range positive input to FCVT.LU.S, the instruction MUST produce the result 2^64−1.
RULE 10: When the input to FCVT.LU.S is positive infinity, FCVT.LU.S MUST produce the result 2^64−1.
RULE 11: When the input to FCVT.LU.S is NaN, FCVT.LU.S MUST produce the result 2^64−1.
RULE 12: FCVT.LU.S MUST set the inexact exception flag if the rounded result differs from the operand value and the invalid exception flag is not set.
RULE 13: FCVT.LU.S does NOT perform sign-extension of a 32-bit result because it directly produces a full 64-bit integer result.

INSTRUCTION: FCVT.Q.D
FORMAT: fcvt.q.d fd, fs1, rm
RULE 1: FCVT.Q.D must convert the double-precision floating-point value contained in the source floating-point register rs1 to a quad-precision floating-point value and write the converted result to the destination floating-point register rd.
RULE 2: FCVT.Q.D must produce the same architectural result as other double-precision floating-point-to-floating-point conversion instructions, including the handling of NaNs, infinities, signed zeros, and the raising of floating-point exception flags in accordance with the IEEE 754 standard.

INSTRUCTION: FCVT.Q.H
FORMAT: fcvt.q.h fd, fs1, rm
RULE 1: The rm field of FCVT.Q.H selects the rounding mode applied during the conversion.
RULE 2: The rd field of FCVT.Q.H specifies the destination floating-point register that receives the quad-precision result.

INSTRUCTION: FCVT.Q.L
FORMAT: fcvt.q.l fd, rs1, rm
RULE 1: FCVT.Q.L must convert the 64-bit signed integer value in integer register rs1 to a quad-precision floating-point value and write the result to floating-point register rd.
RULE 2: FCVT.Q.L must be executed only on an RV64 implementation; on an implementation that does not support the RV64 base ISA, execution of FCVT.Q.L must raise an illegal-instruction exception.
RULE 3: FCVT.Q.L must produce an exact conversion result and must ignore the rounding-mode field (rm) so that the rounding mode does not affect the result.

INSTRUCTION: FCVT.Q.LU
FORMAT: fcvt.q.lu fd, rs1, rm
RULE 1: FCVT.Q.LU converts a 64-bit unsigned integer source operand to a quad-precision floating-point result.
RULE 2: FCVT.Q.LU is defined only for RV64 implementations; its encoding is reserved on RV32 or on implementations that do not support RV64.
RULE 3: FCVT.Q.LU always produces an exact conversion result and the current floating-point rounding mode does not affect its operation.

INSTRUCTION: FCVT.Q.W
FORMAT: fcvt.q.w fd, rs1, rm
RULE 1: FCVT.Q.W converts a 32-bit signed integer operand to a quad-precision floating-point result.
RULE 2: FCVT.Q.W performs its conversion using the same architectural rules for rounding, overflow, and special cases as the existing integer-to-double-precision conversion instructions.

INSTRUCTION: FCVT.Q.WU
FORMAT: fcvt.q.wu fd, rs1, rm
RULE 1: FCVT.Q.WU is not an RV64-only instruction and therefore must be present in any RV32 or RV64 implementation that includes the floating-point extension.
RULE 2: FCVT.Q.WU obeys the rounding mode specified in the rm field of the instruction and may raise the same floating-point exception flags (invalid operation, overflow, underflow, inexact) as the analogous double-precision conversion instructions.
RULE 3: FCVT.Q.WU is not specified to always produce an exact result and therefore its result may be rounded according to the current rounding mode.

INSTRUCTION: FCVT.S.BF16
FORMAT: fcvt.s.bf16 fd, fs1, rm
RULE 1: FCVT.S.BF16 must convert a BF16 source operand to an IEEE-754 single-precision (FP32) result placed in the destination floating-point register.
RULE 2: FCVT.S.BF16 converts a BF16 value to an FP32 value.
RULE 3: FCVT.S.BF16 must perform an exact conversion of the BF16 input to the FP32 result.
RULE 4: When the BF16 input to FCVT.S.BF16 is a normal number or infinity, FCVT.S.BF16 shifts the BF16 encoded bits left by 16 positions and writes zeros into the least-significant 16 bits of the FP32 result.
RULE 5: FCVT.S.BF16 NaN-boxes the FP32 result by setting the most-significant FLEN-32 bits of the destination register to 1.
RULE 6: FCVT.S.BF16 raises the invalid exception for inputs that are NaNs.
RULE 7: FCVT.S.BF16 converts the BF16 value held in the source floating-point register to an FP32 (single-precision) value and writes the converted FP32 result to the destination floating-point register.
RULE 8: FCVT.S.BF16 produces an FP32 value as its result and stores that result in a floating-point register.

INSTRUCTION: FCVT.S.D
FORMAT: fcvt.s.d fd, fs1, rm
RULE 1: FCVT.S.D converts a double-precision floating-point value in a source floating-point register to a single-precision floating-point value that is written to a destination floating-point register.
RULE 2: FCVT.S.D rounds the conversion result according to the rounding mode specified in the RM field.

INSTRUCTION: FCVT.S.H
FORMAT: fcvt.s.h fd, fs1, rm
RULE 1: FCVT.S.H reads the half-precision floating-point value from source register rs1 (src) and writes the resulting single-precision floating-point value to destination register rd (dest).
RULE 2: FCVT.S.H applies the rounding mode indicated by the rm field to the half-precision to single-precision conversion.

INSTRUCTION: FCVT.S.LU
FORMAT: fcvt.s.lu fd, rs1, rm
RULE 1: FCVT.S.LU converts the unsigned integer value held in integer register rs1 to a single-precision floating-point value that is written to floating-point register rd.
RULE 2: FCVT.S.LU rounds its conversion result according to the rounding mode specified in the rm field, using the same rounding rules as all other floating-point to integer and integer to floating-point conversion instructions.
RULE 3: If the rounded conversion result cannot be represented in the single-precision destination format, FCVT.S.LU clips the result to the nearest representable value and sets the invalid-operation exception flag.
RULE 4: FCVT.S.LU sets the inexact-operation exception flag when the rounded conversion result differs from the original unsigned integer operand value and the invalid-operation exception flag is not set.

INSTRUCTION: FCVT.S.Q
FORMAT: fcvt.s.q fd, fs1, rm
RULE 1: FCVT.S.Q converts a quad-precision floating-point number to a single-precision floating-point number.

INSTRUCTION: FCVT.S.WU
FORMAT: fcvt.s.wu fd, rs1, rm
RULE 1: FCVT.S.WU MUST convert the unsigned integer value in integer register rs1 to a single-precision floating-point value written to floating-point register rd.
RULE 2: FCVT.S.WU MUST round the conversion result according to the rounding mode specified in the rm field.
RULE 3: FCVT.S.WU MUST clip the rounded result to the nearest representable single-precision value and set the Invalid exception flag when the rounded result is not representable in the destination format.
RULE 4: FCVT.S.WU MUST set the Inexact exception flag when the rounded result differs from the operand value and the Invalid exception flag is not set.

INSTRUCTION: FCVT.W.D
FORMAT: fcvt.w.d rd, fs1, rm
RULE 1: FCVT.W.D converts the double-precision floating-point value in floating-point register rs1 to a signed 32-bit integer and writes the result to integer register rd.
RULE 2: FCVT.W.D rounds the conversion result according to the rounding mode specified in the rm field of the instruction.
RULE 3: When executed on an RV64 implementation, FCVT.W.D sign-extends the 32-bit integer result to produce a 64-bit value written to integer register rd.
RULE 4: FCVT.W.D must raise the floating-point exception flags that correspond to converting its double-precision source operand to a signed 32-bit integer, and the set of flags raised is identical to those that would be raised for FCVTMOD.W.D with the same operand.

INSTRUCTION: FCVT.W.H
FORMAT: fcvt.w.h rd, fs1, rm
RULE 1: FCVT.W.H must convert the half-precision floating-point value in source floating-point register rs1 to a signed 32-bit integer and write the resulting integer into integer register rd.
RULE 2: FCVT.W.H must apply the rounding mode indicated by the rm field when rounding the source value during the conversion.

INSTRUCTION: FCVT.W.Q
FORMAT: fcvt.w.q rd, fs1, rm
RULE 1: FCVT.W.Q converts a quad-precision floating-point number to a signed 32-bit integer.

INSTRUCTION: FCVT.W.S
FORMAT: fcvt.w.s rd, fs1, rm
RULE 1: FCVT.W.S converts the floating-point value in source register rs1 to a signed 32-bit integer placed in destination integer register rd.
RULE 2: When XLEN is greater than 32, FCVT.W.S sign-extends the 32-bit conversion result to fill the full XLEN bits of rd.
RULE 3: FCVT.W.S rounds the floating-point operand according to the rounding mode specified in the rm field of the instruction.
RULE 4: If the rounded result of FCVT.W.S is not representable in the signed 32-bit destination format, FCVT.W.S clips the result to the nearest representable value and sets the invalid exception flag.
RULE 5: For FCVT.W.S, any input whose rounded value is less than –2³¹ is considered out-of-range negative and the produced integer result is –2³¹.
RULE 6: For FCVT.W.S, an input value of negative infinity –∞ yields the integer result –2³¹.
RULE 7: For FCVT.W.S, an input value of positive infinity +∞ or a NaN produces the integer result 2³¹–1.
RULE 8: When FCVT.W.S produces a result that differs from the original operand after rounding and the invalid exception flag is not set, FCVT.W.S sets the inexact exception flag.
RULE 9: If the rounded result of FCVT.W.S lies within the inclusive range –2³¹ to 2³¹–1, FCVT.W.S writes that exact integer value to rd without clipping.

INSTRUCTION: FCVT.WU.D
FORMAT: fcvt.wu.d rd, fs1, rm
RULE 1: FCVT.WU.D converts the double-precision floating-point value in floating-point register rs1 to an unsigned 32-bit integer and writes the result to integer register rd.
RULE 2: FCVT.WU.D rounds the source floating-point value according to the rounding mode specified in the rm field before performing the conversion.
RULE 3: In RV64 implementations, FCVT.WU.D sign-extends the 32-bit unsigned integer result to 64 bits when writing to integer register rd.
RULE 4: FCVT.WU.D accepts as valid inputs exactly the same range of double-precision floating-point values defined for FCVT.int.S, and any input outside that range is handled identically to FCVT.int.S.

INSTRUCTION: FCVT.WU.H
FORMAT: fcvt.wu.h rd, fs1, rm
RULE 1: FCVT.WU.H converts the half-precision floating-point value held in the source floating-point register (rs1) to an unsigned 32-bit integer and writes the result to the destination integer register (rd).
RULE 2: FCVT.WU.H applies the rounding mode specified by the rounding-mode field (rm) when performing the conversion from half-precision to unsigned integer.

INSTRUCTION: FCVT.WU.S
FORMAT: fcvt.wu.s rd, fs1, rm
RULE 1: FCVT.WU.S converts the floating-point value in floating-point register rs1 to an unsigned 32-bit integer and writes the result to integer register rd.
RULE 2: FCVT.WU.S rounds the conversion result according to the rounding mode specified in the rm field.
RULE 3: If the rounded result of FCVT.WU.S is not representable in the unsigned 32-bit destination format, FCVT.WU.S clips the result to the nearest representable value and sets the invalid exception flag.
RULE 4: FCVT.WU.S sets the inexact exception flag when the rounded result differs from the original operand value and the invalid exception flag is not set.
RULE 5: For FCVT.WU.S the minimum valid input value after rounding is 0.
RULE 6: For FCVT.WU.S the maximum valid input value after rounding is 2^32 - 1.
RULE 7: When FCVT.WU.S receives a negative input that is out of range, it produces the output 0.
RULE 8: When FCVT.WU.S receives negative infinity as input, it produces the output 0.
RULE 9: When FCVT.WU.S receives positive infinity or a NaN as input, it produces the output 2^32 - 1.

INSTRUCTION: FCVTMOD.W.D
FORMAT: fcvtmod.w.d rd, fs1, rm
RULE 1: FCVTMOD.W.D must round the conversion toward zero regardless of any rounding-mode field value.
RULE 2: FCVTMOD.W.D takes the lower 32 bits of the rounded, unbounded two's-complement result, sign-extends them to XLEN bits, and writes the value to integer register rd.
RULE 3: FCVTMOD.W.D converts a source operand of positive or negative infinity to an integer result of zero.
RULE 4: FCVTMOD.W.D converts a source operand that is NaN to an integer result of zero.
RULE 5: FCVTMOD.W.D raises floating-point exception flags exactly as FCVT.W.D would raise for the same input operand.
RULE 6: FCVTMOD.W.D encoding requires the rs2 field to be 8 and the rm field to be 1 (RTZ), and any other rm value makes the encoding reserved.

INSTRUCTION: FDIV.Q
FORMAT: fdiv.q fd, fs1, fs2, rm
RULE 1: FDIV.Q MUST perform a floating-point division of the quad-precision value in source register rs1 by the quad-precision value in source register rs2, using the rounding mode specified by its RM field, and write the quad-precision result to destination register rd.

INSTRUCTION: FDIV.S
FORMAT: fdiv.s fd, fs1, fs2, rm
RULE 1: FDIV.S writes the result of the operation to the floating-point register identified by the rd field.
RULE 2: FDIV.S performs a single-precision floating-point division of the value in rs1 by the value in rs2.
RULE 3: FDIV.S may use the rm field to select the rounding mode applied to the division result.
RULE 4: When FDIV.S uses the rm field, the selected rounding mode governs how the division result is rounded in accordance with the rounding-mode semantics of the F extension.

INSTRUCTION: FENCE
FORMAT: fence pred, succ
RULE 1: The rs1 and rd fields of a FENCE are reserved for finer-grain fences in future extensions, and base implementations shall ignore these fields while standard software shall set them to zero.
RULE 2: Configurations where the fm field or the predecessor or successor sets are reserved shall be treated by base implementations as a normal FENCE with fm = 0000, and standard software shall use only non-reserved configurations.

INSTRUCTION: FENCE.I
FORMAT: fence.i
RULE 1: The funct12, rs1, and rd fields of a FENCE.I encoding are reserved; base implementations shall ignore these fields.

INSTRUCTION: FEQ.S
FORMAT: feq.s rd, fs1, fs2
RULE 1: FEQ.S must compare the values in the two specified single-precision floating-point source registers for equality.
RULE 2: FEQ.S must write the integer value 1 to the integer destination register rd when the two compared operands are equal, and must write the integer value 0 to rd when they are not equal.
RULE 3: FEQ.S must set the floating-point invalid-operation exception flag only if at least one of its source operands is a signaling NaN.
RULE 4: FEQ.S must write the integer value 0 to rd when either source operand is NaN, regardless of whether the NaN is signaling or quiet.

INSTRUCTION: FLD
FORMAT: fld fd, imm12(rs1)
RULE 1: FLD MUST load a double-precision floating-point value from the memory location specified by its effective address into the floating-point destination register rd.
RULE 2: If the effective address of an FLD is not naturally aligned or the implementation’s XLEN is less than 64, then the load is not required to be atomic (no exception is raised and the operation may be performed as a non‑atomic memory sequence).
RULE 3: FLD transfers the bits from memory to the destination register without modification, thereby preserving the payload of non-canonical NaNs.
RULE 4: FLD loads an 8-byte double-precision floating-point value from the memory address computed as its effective address into the floating-point register rd'.
RULE 5: FLD must read a memory element of exactly 64 bits (Membits = 64).
RULE 6: FLD must write the loaded value into a floating-point register element whose size is FLEN.
RULE 7: FLD must have pointer masking applied to its effective address when pointer masking is enabled for the privilege mode that performs the FLD access.
RULE 8: If FLD accesses a misaligned address that exceeds the implementation’s supported misaligned‑access limits, then it raises a load‑address‑misaligned exception (mcause = 5) and mtval contains the faulting address.
RULE 9: If a FLD is executed with an address whose low three bits are not zero, then no exception is raised; instead the hardware reads the two aligned 8‑byte words at (address & ~7) and (address & ~7 + 8), combines their bytes according to the pointer‑masking transformation, and the destination floating‑point register contains exactly that assembled 64‑bit value.
RULE 10: FLD is not exempt from pointer masking by the CSR read/write exemption; pointer masking applies to FLD even though CSR accesses are exempt.
RULE 11: If software stores a tagged address into a CSR and later uses that CSR value as the address for an FLD operation, the FLD address must be subject to pointer masking only if pointer masking is enabled for the privilege mode performing the FLD.
RULE 12: FLD must observe the pointer-masking transformation of its address according to the current privilege-mode settings, including the effects of MPRV, SPVP, and MXR.

INSTRUCTION: FLE.S
FORMAT: fle.s rd, fs1, fs2
RULE 1: FLE.S MUST write the integer value 1 to rd if the single-precision floating-point value in rs1 is less than or equal to the value in rs2, and MUST write the integer value 0 to rd otherwise.
RULE 2: FLE.S MUST set the invalid-operation exception flag if either rs1 or rs2 contains a NaN.
RULE 3: FLE.S MUST write the integer value 0 to rd if either rs1 or rs2 contains a NaN, irrespective of the comparison result.
RULE 4: When the FLE.S instruction is executed with a quiet NaN operand, it must set the invalid operation exception flag.

INSTRUCTION: FLEQ.D
FORMAT: fleq.d rd, fs1, fs2
RULE 1: If the D extension is implemented, the FLEQ.D instruction MUST operate on double-precision floating-point operands.
RULE 2: The FLEQ.D instruction MUST produce the same comparison result as the FLE.D instruction for all operand values.
RULE 3: If either source operand of FLEQ.D is a quiet NaN, the invalid-operation exception flag MUST NOT be set.

INSTRUCTION: FLEQ.H
FORMAT: fleq.h rd, fs1, fs2
RULE 1: The FLEQ.H instruction performs a half-precision floating-point less-than-or-equal comparison whose result is defined identically to the result of the FLE.H instruction.
RULE 2: When a quiet NaN is supplied as either source operand to the FLEQ.H instruction, the invalid-operation exception flag must not be set.

INSTRUCTION: FLEQ.Q
FORMAT: fleq.q rd, fs1, fs2
RULE 1: FLEQ.Q must produce the same Boolean comparison result as the FLE.S instruction for the same pair of operands.
RULE 2: FLEQ.Q must not set the invalid operation exception flag when either source operand is a quiet NaN.

INSTRUCTION: FLEQ.S
FORMAT: fleq.s rd, fs1, fs2
RULE 1: For any pair of floating-point operands that are not quiet NaNs, the FLEQ.S instruction MUST produce the same comparison result and raise the same exception flags as the FLE.S instruction.
RULE 2: When either source operand of the FLEQ.S instruction is a quiet NaN, the instruction MUST NOT set the invalid operation exception flag.

INSTRUCTION: FLH
FORMAT: flh fd, imm12(rs1)
RULE 1: FLH must transfer the loaded 16-bit value without altering any of its bits, and must preserve the payload bits of non-canonical NaNs present in the memory source.
RULE 2: FLH must NaN-box the value written to the destination floating-point register, placing the loaded halfword into the low bits and setting the remaining upper bits according to the NaN-boxing rule.
RULE 3: FLH must read a 16-bit half-precision floating-point element from the effective memory address.
RULE 4: FLH must place the value read from memory into the destination floating-point register, converting the 16-bit half-precision representation to the register's FLEN format.
RULE 5: If a transformed FLH instruction used for a trap has any non‑zero bit in the immediate offset fields (bits 31:25, 24:20, or 11:7), then the processor raises an illegal‑instruction exception (mcause = 2).

INSTRUCTION: FLI.D
FORMAT: fli.d fd, rs1
RULE 1: If the D extension is implemented, executing the FLI.D instruction loads a double-precision floating-point value into the destination floating-point register rd.
RULE 2: The double-precision value loaded by FLI.D for entry 1 (the minimum positive normal value) is numerically different from the value loaded by the corresponding single-precision entry of FLI.S.
RULE 3: The FLI.D instruction never sets any floating-point exception flags.

INSTRUCTION: FLI.H
FORMAT: fli.h fd, rs1
RULE 1: The FLI.H instruction is defined only if the Zfh or Zvfh extension is implemented.
RULE 2: Execution of FLI.H uses the rs1 field to index a table of 32 half-precision constants and writes the selected constant into floating-point register rd.
RULE 3: If the value read from the FLI.H constant table entry 1 is not the IEEE‑754 half‑precision minimum positive normal (bit pattern 0x0400) or is equal to the value read from the FLI.S constant table entry 1, then the two reads return identical values instead of distinct ones.
RULE 4: If an FLI.H instruction reads constant‑table entry 29, then the destination floating‑point register contains the IEEE‑754 half‑precision positive‑infinity value (sign 0, exponent all 1, fraction 0).
RULE 5: If a program loads the constant from FLI.H entry 29, then the destination register receives the half‑precision positive‑infinity value (bit pattern 0x7C00), identical to the value loaded from entry 30.
RULE 6: For FLI.H, the constant produced by entry 1 is numerically greater than the constants produced by entries 2 and 3 because 2^-16 and 2^-15 are subnormal in half-precision.
RULE 7: Execution of FLI.H never sets any floating-point exception flags.

INSTRUCTION: FLI.Q
FORMAT: fli.q fd, rs1
RULE 1: Execution of FLI.Q loads a quad-precision floating-point value into the destination floating-point register rd, performing the analogous operation to FLI.S but with quad precision.
RULE 2: For FLI.Q, immediate-value entry 1 represents the minimum positive normal quad-precision value, which is numerically different from the entry 1 value of other formats.
RULE 3: Execution of FLI.Q never sets any floating-point exception flags.

INSTRUCTION: FLI.S
FORMAT: fli.s fd, rs1
RULE 1: When the FLI.S instruction is executed with rs1 = 0, the floating-point register rd is written with the single-precision value –1.0 (sign = 1, exponent = 01111111, significand = all zeros).
RULE 2: When the FLI.S instruction is executed with rs1 = 1, the floating-point register rd is written with the smallest positive normal single-precision value (sign = 0, exponent = 00000001, significand = all zeros).
RULE 3: When the FLI.S instruction is executed with rs1 = 2, the floating-point register rd is written with the single-precision value 1.0 × 2⁻¹⁶ (sign = 0, exponent = 01101111, significand = all zeros).
RULE 4: When the FLI.S instruction is executed with rs1 = 3, the floating-point register rd is written with the single-precision value 1.0 × 2⁻¹⁵ (sign = 0, exponent = 01110000, significand = all zeros).
RULE 5: When the FLI.S instruction is executed with rs1 = 4, the floating-point register rd is written with the single-precision value 1.0 × 2⁻⁸ (sign = 0, exponent = 01110111, significand = all zeros).
RULE 6: When the FLI.S instruction is executed with rs1 = 5, the floating-point register rd is written with the single-precision value 1.0 × 2⁻⁷ (sign = 0, exponent = 01111000, significand = all zeros).
RULE 7: When the FLI.S instruction is executed with rs1 = 6, the floating-point register rd is written with the single-precision value 0.0625 (2⁻⁴) (sign = 0, exponent = 01111011, significand = all zeros).
RULE 8: When the FLI.S instruction is executed with rs1 = 7, the floating-point register rd is written with the single-precision value 0.125 (2⁻³) (sign = 0, exponent = 01111100, significand = all zeros).
RULE 9: When the FLI.S instruction is executed with rs1 = 8, the floating-point register rd is written with the single-precision value 0.25 (sign = 0, exponent = 01111101, significand = all zeros).
RULE 10: When the FLI.S instruction is executed with rs1 = 9, the floating-point register rd is written with the single-precision value 0.3125 (sign = 0, exponent = 01111101, significand = 010…000, where only the most-significant two bits of the fraction are non-zero).
RULE 11: When the FLI.S instruction is executed with rs1 = 10, the floating-point register rd is written with the single-precision value 0.375 (sign = 0, exponent = 01111101, significand = 100…000, where only the most-significant two bits of the fraction are non-zero).
RULE 12: When the FLI.S instruction is executed with rs1 = 11, the floating-point register rd is written with the single-precision value 0.4375 (sign = 0, exponent = 01111101, significand = 110…000, where only the most-significant two bits of the fraction are non-zero).
RULE 13: When the FLI.S instruction is executed with rs1 = 12, the floating-point register rd is written with the single-precision value 0.5 (sign = 0, exponent = 01111110, significand = all zeros).
RULE 14: When the FLI.S instruction is executed with rs1 = 13, the floating-point register rd is written with the single-precision value 0.625 (sign = 0, exponent = 01111110, significand = 010…000, where only the most-significant two bits of the fraction are non-zero).
RULE 15: When the FLI.S instruction is executed with rs1 = 14, the floating-point register rd is written with the single-precision value 0.75 (sign = 0, exponent = 01111110, significand = 100…000, where only the most-significant two bits of the fraction are non-zero).
RULE 16: When the FLI.S instruction is executed with rs1 = 15, the floating-point register rd is written with the single-precision value 0.875 (sign = 0, exponent = 01111110, significand = 110…000, where only the most-significant two bits of the fraction are non-zero).
RULE 17: When the FLI.S instruction is executed with rs1 = 16, the floating-point register rd is written with the single-precision value 1.0 (sign = 0, exponent = 01111111, significand = all zeros).
RULE 18: When the FLI.S instruction is executed with rs1 = 17, the floating-point register rd is written with the single-precision value 1.25 (sign = 0, exponent = 01111111, significand = 010…000, where only the most-significant two bits of the fraction are non-zero).
RULE 19: When the FLI.S instruction is executed with rs1 = 18, the floating-point register rd is written with the single-precision value 1.5 (sign = 0, exponent = 01111111, significand = 100…000, where only the most-significant two bits of the fraction are non-zero).
RULE 20: When the FLI.S instruction is executed with rs1 = 19, the floating-point register rd is written with the single-precision value 1.75 (sign = 0, exponent = 01111111, significand = 110…000, where only the most-significant two bits of the fraction are non-zero).
RULE 21: When the FLI.S instruction is executed with rs1 = 20, the floating-point register rd is written with the single-precision value 2.0 (sign = 0, exponent = 10000000, significand = all zeros).
RULE 22: When the FLI.S instruction is executed with rs1 = 21, the floating-point register rd is written with the single-precision value 2.5 (sign = 0, exponent = 10000000, significand = 010…000, where only the most-significant two bits of the fraction are non-zero).
RULE 23: When the FLI.S instruction is executed with rs1 = 22, the floating-point register rd is written with the single-precision value 3.0 (sign = 0, exponent = 10000000, significand = 100…000, where only the most-significant two bits of the fraction are non-zero).
RULE 24: When the FLI.S instruction is executed with rs1 = 23, the floating-point register rd is written with the single-precision value 4.0 (sign = 0, exponent = 10000001, significand = all zeros).
RULE 25: When the FLI.S instruction is executed with rs1 = 24, the floating-point register rd is written with the single-precision value 8.0 (sign = 0, exponent = 10000010, significand = all zeros).
RULE 26: When the FLI.S instruction is executed with rs1 = 25, the floating-point register rd is written with the single-precision value 16.0 (sign = 0, exponent = 10000011, significand = all zeros).
RULE 27: When the FLI.S instruction is executed with rs1 = 26, the floating-point register rd is written with the single-precision value 128.0 (2⁷) (sign = 0, exponent = 10000110, significand = all zeros).
RULE 28: When the FLI.S instruction is executed with rs1 = 27, the floating-point register rd is written with the single-precision value 256.0 (2⁸) (sign = 0, exponent = 10000111, significand = all zeros).
RULE 29: When the FLI.S instruction is executed with rs1 = 28, the floating-point register rd is written with the single-precision value 2¹⁵ (sign = 0, exponent = 10001110, significand = all zeros).
RULE 30: When the FLI.S instruction is executed with rs1 = 29, the floating-point register rd is written with the single-precision value 2¹⁶ (sign = 0, exponent = 10001111, significand = all zeros).
RULE 31: When the FLI.S instruction is executed with rs1 = 30, the floating-point register rd is written with the positive infinity single-precision value (sign = 0, exponent = 11111111, significand = all zeros).
RULE 32: When the FLI.S instruction is executed with rs1 = 31, the floating-point register rd is written with the canonical NaN single-precision value (sign = 0, exponent = 11111111, significand = 100…000, where only the most-significant bit of the fraction is non-zero).
RULE 33: For rs1 values 8 through 22, the floating-point value loaded by FLI.S has only the two most-significant bits of the significand possibly non-zero and all other significand bits are zero.
RULE 34: Execution of the FLI.S instruction never sets any floating-point exception flags.

INSTRUCTION: FLQ
FORMAT: flq fd, rs1, imm12
RULE 1: FLQ must transfer the 128-bit floating-point value without modifying any of its bits, and must preserve the payloads of non-canonical NaNs present in the source.
RULE 2: FLQ places the loaded 128-bit value into a floating-point register whose width is FLEN bits.
RULE 3: FLQ must be encoded with the mew bit cleared to 0, and any encoding that sets the mew bit to 1 for a 128-bit load is reserved.
RULE 4: If a transformed FLQ instruction’s funct3, rd, or opcode field differs from the corresponding fields of the trapping FLQ instruction, then the processor raises an illegal‑instruction exception.
RULE 5: When MXR is in effect in the effective privilege mode for a FLQ execution, pointer masking MUST NOT be applied to the address used by FLQ.
RULE 6: If a misaligned FLQ load or store requires one or more aligned sub‑accesses (after applying pointer‑masking) that would raise an exception (e.g., page‑fault, access‑fault, or illegal‑instruction), then the entire FLQ operation raises the same exception (mcause set to the fault code) and the destination register is not updated.

INSTRUCTION: FLT.S
FORMAT: flt.s rd, fs1, fs2
RULE 1: FLT.S compares the single-precision floating-point values in registers rs1 and rs2 using the less-than relation.
RULE 2: FLT.S writes the integer value 1 to rd when the comparison rs1 < rs2 is true, and writes the integer value 0 to rd when the comparison is false.
RULE 3: FLT.S sets the floating-point invalid operation exception flag if either operand is a NaN.
RULE 4: FLT.S produces a result of 0 (writes 0 to rd) when either operand is a NaN.
RULE 5: If FLT.S is executed, the hardware treats the 32‑bit values in rs1 and rs2 as IEEE‑754 binary32 numbers; consequently the destination register rd is set to 1 when the numeric value of rs1 is less than that of rs2 according to IEEE‑754 comparison rules (including NaN handling), and set to 0 otherwise.

INSTRUCTION: FLTQ.D
FORMAT: fltq.d rd, fs1, fs2
RULE 1: FLTQ.D produces the same comparison result as the FLT.D instruction for all operand values.
RULE 2: When a quiet NaN is an input to FLTQ.D, the invalid-operation exception flag must not be set.
RULE 3: For inputs that are not quiet NaNs, FLTQ.D must set the invalid-operation exception flag in the same manner as FLT.D.

INSTRUCTION: FLTQ.H
FORMAT: fltq.h rd, fs1, fs2
RULE 1: FLTQ.H treats its source registers as half-precision floating-point numbers.
RULE 2: FLTQ.H produces the same integer comparison result as the FLT.H instruction for the given half-precision operands.
RULE 3: FLTQ.H does not set the invalid-operation exception flag when either source operand is a quiet NaN.
RULE 4: For all operand cases other than quiet NaNs, FLTQ.H sets exception flags in the same way as FLT.H.

INSTRUCTION: FLTQ.Q
FORMAT: fltq.q fd, fs1, fs2
RULE 1: FLTQ.Q must not set the invalid-operation exception flag when any source operand is a quiet NaN.

INSTRUCTION: FLTQ.S
FORMAT: fltq.s rd, fs1, fs2
RULE 1: FLTQ.S performs the same floating-point less-than comparison as FLT.S and must not set the invalid-operation exception flag when either source operand is a quiet NaN.

INSTRUCTION: FLW
FORMAT: flw fd, imm12(rs1)
RULE 1: FLW MUST load a single-precision floating-point value from the memory location whose effective address is computed and place the value into floating-point register rd.
RULE 2: FLW MUST compute its effective address by adding a sign-extended 12-bit byte offset to the base address contained in register rs1.
RULE 3: If the effective address of an FLW instruction is not naturally aligned, then the load is not required to be atomic, so the destination floating‑point register may contain a value that does not correspond to a single coherent memory word (e.g., a mixture of old and new data).
RULE 4: FLW MUST preserve all bits of the transferred single-precision value, including the payloads of non-canonical NaNs.
RULE 5: The FLW instruction loads a single-precision floating-point value from memory into the destination floating-point register rd′.
RULE 6: The FLW instruction is semantically equivalent to the uncompressed instruction flw rd′, offset(rs1′).
RULE 7: FLW MUST ignore the mew bit (inst[28]) during execution.
RULE 8: FLW MUST access memory with an element size of 32 bits.
RULE 9: FLW MUST write the loaded value into a floating-point register whose size is FLEN bits.
RULE 10: If a synchronous exception is taken for a cause other than a load address‑misaligned, load‑access‑fault, or load‑page‑fault exception, then the trap‑instruction register (mtinst) must not contain a transformed FLW encoding.
RULE 11: FLW is an explicit floating-point memory-access instruction and its effective address MUST be processed by the architectural pointer-masking transformation.
RULE 12: Because pointer masking applies to all explicit memory accesses generated by CPU instructions, including those from the floating-point unit, FLW accesses MUST be subject to pointer masking.
RULE 13: Misaligned FLW accesses MUST be supported and are subject to the same limitations that apply to misaligned accesses when pointer masking is not present.
RULE 14: The architectural effect of a misaligned FLW access MUST be identical to applying the pointer-masking transformation to each aligned sub-access that composes the misaligned operation.

INSTRUCTION: FMAX.S
FORMAT: fmax.s fd, fs1, fs2
RULE 1: FMAX.S must write to destination register rd the operand that is larger in value between the single-precision floating-point registers rs1 and rs2.
RULE 2: For FMAX.S, when the two operands are signed zeros, the positive zero (+0.0) is considered larger than negative zero (-0.0) and therefore must be written to rd.
RULE 3: If both rs1 and rs2 are NaNs, FMAX.S must produce the canonical NaN as the result written to rd.
RULE 4: If exactly one of rs1 or rs2 is a NaN, FMAX.S must write the non-NaN operand to rd.
RULE 5: When any operand supplied to FMAX.S is a signaling NaN, the invalid operation exception flag must be set even if the result written to rd is not a NaN.
RULE 6: FMAX.S must compute the maximum of its two single-precision floating-point source operands according to the IEEE 754-2019 maximum operation.
RULE 7: FMAX.S must handle NaN operands using the IEEE 754-2019 maximum operation semantics, i.e., it does not apply the special canonical-NaN result used by FMAXM.S.

INSTRUCTION: FMAXM.D
FORMAT: fmaxm.d fd, fs1, fs2
RULE 1: FMAXM.D computes the maximum of its two double-precision operands in the same manner as FMAX.D, but if either operand is a NaN the result is the canonical NaN.

INSTRUCTION: FMAXM.H
FORMAT: fmaxm.h fd, fs1, fs2
RULE 1: If either operand of the FMAXM.H instruction is a NaN, the result produced by FMAXM.H is the canonical NaN.
RULE 2: If neither operand of the FMAXM.H instruction is a NaN, the result of FMAXM.H is the greater of the two half-precision values as defined by the IEEE 754-2019 maximum operation.

INSTRUCTION: FMAXM.Q
FORMAT: fmaxm.q fd, fs1, fs2
RULE 1: When both operands of FMAXM.Q are not NaN, the result must be identical to the result of the FMAX.Q instruction applied to the same operands.
RULE 2: If either operand of FMAXM.Q is a NaN, the result must be the canonical NaN.
RULE 3: The FMAXM.Q instruction must implement the IEEE 754-2019 maximum operation for quad-precision operands.

INSTRUCTION: FMAXM.S
FORMAT: fmaxm.s fd, fs1, fs2
RULE 1: FMAXM.S MUST compute the IEEE 754-2019 maximum of its two single-precision floating-point operands when neither operand is a NaN.
RULE 2: If either operand of FMAXM.S is a NaN, FMAXM.S MUST produce the canonical NaN as the result.
RULE 3: For non-NaN operands, FMAXM.S returns the operand with the greater numeric value according to the IEEE 754-2019 ordering, including the defined handling of signed zeros and infinities.

INSTRUCTION: FMIN.Q
FORMAT: fmin.q fd, fs1, fs2
RULE 1: If the FMIN.Q instruction is executed with two quad‑precision source registers, then the destination register contains the quad‑precision minimum of the two source operands.

INSTRUCTION: FMIN.S
FORMAT: fmin.s fd, fs1, fs2
RULE 1: FMIN.S writes the smaller of the values in source registers rs1 and rs2 to destination register rd.
RULE 2: For FMIN.S, the value –0.0 is considered less than the value +0.0.
RULE 3: If both rs1 and rs2 are NaNs, FMIN.S produces the canonical NaN as the result.
RULE 4: If exactly one of rs1 or rs2 is a NaN, FMIN.S returns the non-NaN operand as the result.
RULE 5: Signaling NaN inputs to FMIN.S set the invalid-operation exception flag even when the result is not NaN.
RULE 6: FMIN.S MUST compute the IEEE 754-2019 minimum of its two single-precision floating-point source operands.
RULE 7: When the two source operands of FMIN.S are both numeric values and are unequal, FMIN.S MUST return the operand that is numerically smaller according to IEEE 754 ordering.
RULE 8: When the two source operands of FMIN.S are numerically equal, FMIN.S MUST return the operand that preserves the sign of zero (returning -0.0 if either operand is -0.0, otherwise +0.0).
RULE 9: If one source operand of FMIN.S is a NaN and the other operand is a numeric value, FMIN.S MUST return the NaN operand as specified by the IEEE 754-2019 minimum operation.
RULE 10: If both source operands of FMIN.S are NaNs, FMIN.S MUST return a NaN according to the IEEE 754-2019 minimum operation and MUST NOT replace it with the canonical NaN.

INSTRUCTION: FMINM.D
FORMAT: fminm.d fd, fs1, fs2
RULE 1: FMINM.D implements the IEEE 754-2019 minimum operation on its two double-precision floating-point source operands.
RULE 2: If either source operand of FMINM.D is a NaN, the result produced by FMINM.D is the canonical NaN.

INSTRUCTION: FMINM.Q
FORMAT: fminm.q fd, fs1, fs2
RULE 1: The FMINM.Q instruction shall compute the IEEE 754-2019 minimum of its two quad-precision source operands.
RULE 2: When either source operand of the FMINM.Q instruction is a NaN, the result shall be the canonical NaN.
RULE 3: When neither source operand of the FMINM.Q instruction is a NaN, the result shall be the smaller of the two operands according to the IEEE 754-2019 minimum ordering, including the signed-zero handling identical to that of FMIN.S.

INSTRUCTION: FMINM.S
FORMAT: fminm.s fd, fs1, fs2
RULE 1: FMINM.S implements the IEEE 754-2019 minimum operation for single-precision floating-point numbers.
RULE 2: If either operand supplied to FMINM.S is a NaN, the result produced by FMINM.S is the canonical NaN.
RULE 3: When an instruction word matches the encoding of FMIN.S and has bit 13 set to 1, the processor must decode and execute it as the FMINM.S instruction.

INSTRUCTION: FMSUB.S
FORMAT: fmsub.s fd, fs1, fs2, fs3, rm
RULE 1: If FMSUB.S is not encoded using the R4‑type format (i.e., the rs1, rs2, rs3, or rd fields are not placed in the R4‑type positions), then executing the instruction raises an illegal‑instruction exception; when correctly encoded, the hardware reads the floating‑point values from rs1, rs2, and rs3 and writes the fused‑multiply‑subtract result to floating‑point register rd.
RULE 2: FMSUB.S computes the fused operation (rs1 × rs2) – rs3 as a single arithmetic step and writes the rounded result to rd.
RULE 3: FMSUB.S may select the rounding mode applied to its result by encoding the desired mode in its rm field.
RULE 4: FMSUB.S must set the invalid-operation exception flag when one multiplicand is Infinity and the other multiplicand is zero, regardless of the value in rs3, including when rs3 holds a quiet NaN.

INSTRUCTION: FMUL.Q
FORMAT: fmul.q fd, fs1, fs2, rm
RULE 1: FMUL.Q MUST write the quad-precision floating-point product of its operands to the destination register dest.
RULE 2: FMUL.Q MUST apply the rounding mode indicated by the rm field of the instruction encoding when producing its result.
RULE 3: FMUL.Q MUST compute the product of the two quad-precision operands using the same architectural semantics, rounding behavior, and exception handling as the double-precision FMUL instruction, but operating on quad-precision values.

INSTRUCTION: FMUL.S
FORMAT: fmul.s fd, fs1, fs2, rm
RULE 1: FMUL.S performs single-precision floating-point multiplication of the values in registers rs1 and rs2 and writes the exact mathematical product, after rounding, to register rd.
RULE 2: FMUL.S can select the rounding mode for the multiplication operation by using the rm field of the instruction.

INSTRUCTION: FMV.D.X
FORMAT: fmv.d.x fd, rs1
RULE 1: FMV.D.X is only defined for implementations where XLEN ≥ 64; implementations with XLEN < 64 MUST not provide FMV.D.X.
RULE 2: FMV.D.X copies the 64-bit pattern from integer register rs1 into floating-point register rd, treating the bits as an IEEE 754-2008 double-precision encoding.
RULE 3: FMV.D.X MUST preserve all transferred bits unchanged, including the payloads of noncanonical NaNs.

INSTRUCTION: FMV.H.X
FORMAT: fmv.h.x fd, rs1
RULE 1: FMV.H.X must read the lower 16 bits of integer register rs1 and interpret them as a half-precision floating-point value encoded according to the IEEE 754-2008 standard.
RULE 2: FMV.H.X must write to floating-point register rd a NaN-boxed representation of the half-precision value taken from rs1.
RULE 3: FMV.H.X must preserve all bits of the half-precision value during the move, so that the bit pattern placed in the lower 16 bits of rd is identical to the lower 16 bits read from rs1.
RULE 4: FMV.H.X must preserve the payloads of noncanonical NaNs, meaning that if the source half-precision bits encode a noncanonical NaN, the same payload appears in the NaN-boxed result in rd.

INSTRUCTION: FMV.W.X
FORMAT: fmv.w.x fd, rs1
RULE 1: FMV.W.X must copy the lower 32 bits of integer register rs1 into floating-point register rd, interpreting those bits as an IEEE 754-2008 single-precision value.
RULE 2: FMV.W.X must not modify any bits during the transfer, so the bit pattern placed in rd is identical to the source bits from rs1.
RULE 3: FMV.W.X must preserve the payloads of non-canonical NaNs when moving the value from rs1 to rd.
RULE 4: FMV.W.X MUST have an rs2 field value that is not equal to 1.

INSTRUCTION: FMV.X.D
FORMAT: fmv.x.d rd, fs1
RULE 1: FMV.X.D is architecturally defined only for implementations where XLEN ≥ 64; for implementations with XLEN < 64 the encoding is reserved.
RULE 2: FMV.X.D copies the 64-bit IEEE 754-2008 double-precision bit pattern from floating-point source register rs1 into integer destination register rd without alteration.
RULE 3: FMV.X.D does not modify any of the bits being transferred from the floating-point register to the integer register.
RULE 4: FMV.X.D preserves the payloads of noncanonical NaNs when moving the bit pattern from the floating-point register to the integer register.
RULE 5: FMV.X.D is an instruction that is defined only when the Zdinx extension is implemented.
RULE 6: FMV.X.D has the same architectural effect as the D-extension FMV.X.D except that any reference to an f-register operand is replaced by a reference to the integer x-register with the same register number.
RULE 7: FMV.X.D does not generate any floating-point exception because it performs only a register-to-register move of raw bits.
RULE 8: FMV.X.D leaves all floating-point control and status registers unchanged.

INSTRUCTION: FMV.X.H
FORMAT: fmv.x.h rd, fs1
RULE 1: FMV.X.H moves the half-precision value in floating-point register rs1 to integer register rd by placing the 16-bit value in the lower 16 bits of rd and filling the upper XLEN-16 bits of rd with copies of the floating-point number's sign bit.
RULE 2: FMV.X.H must not modify any bits of the transferred half-precision value, therefore the payloads of noncanonical NaNs are preserved unchanged.
RULE 3: If the FMV.X.H instruction is executed on a processor that does not implement the Zfh extension, then it raises an illegal‑instruction exception; otherwise, it copies the low 16 bits of the source floating‑point register into the destination integer register.

INSTRUCTION: FMV.X.W
FORMAT: fmv.x.w rd, fs1
RULE 1: FMV.X.W must copy the 32-bit IEEE-754-2008 encoding of the single-precision value in floating-point register rs1 into the lower 32 bits of integer register rd without alteration.
RULE 2: FMV.X.W must preserve all bits of the transferred value, including the payload bits of non-canonical NaNs.
RULE 3: On RV64 implementations, FMV.X.W must fill the upper 32 bits of integer register rd with copies of the sign bit of the source floating-point value.

INSTRUCTION: FMVH.X.Q
FORMAT: fmvh.x.q rd, fs1
RULE 1: FMVH.X.Q transfers the upper 64 bits of a quad-precision floating-point value, enabling the complete 128-bit value to be moved to a pair of integer registers when used together with the FMV.X.D instruction that transfers the lower 64 bits.

INSTRUCTION: FMVP.D.X
FORMAT: fmvp.d.x fd, rs1, rs2
RULE 1: FMVP.D.X takes bits 31-0 of integer register rs1 as the low-order 32 bits of the double-precision value.
RULE 2: FMVP.D.X takes bits 31-0 of integer register rs2 as the high-order 32 bits (bits 63-32) of the double-precision value.
RULE 3: FMVP.D.X writes the resulting double-precision value to floating-point register rd, overwriting its previous contents.

INSTRUCTION: FMVP.Q.X
FORMAT: fmvp.q.x fd, rs1, rs2
RULE 1: FMVP.Q.X is defined only for RV64 implementations.
RULE 2: If the Q extension is not implemented and an FMVP.Q.X instruction is executed, then the processor raises an illegal‑instruction exception (mcause = illegal instruction).
RULE 3: FMVP.Q.X takes bits 63:0 of the double-precision result from integer register rs1.
RULE 4: FMVP.Q.X assembles the double-precision value using bits 127:64 from rs2 as the most-significant bits and bits 63:0 from rs1 as the least-significant bits and writes the assembled value to floating-point register rd.

INSTRUCTION: FNMADD.S
FORMAT: fnmadd.s fd, fs1, fs2, fs3, rm
RULE 1: FNMADD.S computes the single-precision result rd as the negated product of rs1 and rs2 minus the value in rs3, i.e., rd <- -(rs1 × rs2) - rs3.
RULE 2: The rounding mode applied to the computation performed by FNMADD.S is taken from the rm field according to the encoding defined in Table 24.
RULE 3: FNMADD.S must set the invalid-operation exception flag when the multiplicands are Infinity and zero, even if the addend rs3 is a quiet NaN.

INSTRUCTION: FNMSUB.S
FORMAT: fnmsub.s fd, fs1, fs2, fs3, rm
RULE 1: FNMSUB.S must multiply the floating-point values in rs1 and rs2, negate the product, add the floating-point value in rs3, and write the final result to rd.
RULE 2: FNMSUB.S reads the floating-point registers rs1, rs2, and rs3 as sources and writes the computed result to the floating-point register rd.
RULE 3: FNMSUB.S must round the computed result according to the rounding mode specified in the rm field of the instruction.
RULE 4: FNMSUB.S must set the invalid operation exception flag when the multiplicands are one Infinity and one zero, even if the addend rs3 is a quiet NaN.

INSTRUCTION: FROUND.D
FORMAT: fround.d fd, fs1, rm
RULE 1: The FROUND.D instruction is defined only when the D extension is implemented.
RULE 2: When executed, FROUND.D rounds the double-precision floating-point value in floating-point register rs1 to an integer using the rounding mode specified in the instruction's rm field.
RULE 3: After rounding, FROUND.D writes the resulting integer, encoded as a double-precision floating-point number, to floating-point register rd.
RULE 4: If the source operand in rs1 is +0, −0, +∞, or −∞, FROUND.D copies that value to rd without modification.
RULE 5: If the source operand in rs1 is a signaling NaN, FROUND.D sets the invalid operation exception flag and does not set any other exception flags.
RULE 6: FROUND.D implements the roundToIntegral operations defined by IEEE-754-2019, excluding the roundToIntegralExact variant.

INSTRUCTION: FROUND.H
FORMAT: fround.h fd, fs1, rm
RULE 1: The FROUND.H instruction rounds the half-precision floating-point value in floating-point register rs1 to an integer using the rounding mode indicated by the instruction's rm field, and writes the resulting integer represented as a half-precision floating-point number into floating-point register rd.
RULE 2: When the source operand of a FROUND.H instruction is zero or an infinity, the value is copied to rd unchanged.
RULE 3: If the source operand of a FROUND.H instruction is a signaling NaN, the invalid operation exception flag is set and no other floating-point exception flags are set.

INSTRUCTION: FROUND.Q
FORMAT: fround.q fd, fs1, rm
RULE 1: FROUND.Q rounds the quad-precision floating-point number read from floating-point register rs1 to an integer according to the rounding mode specified in the instruction's rm field.
RULE 2: FROUND.Q writes the integer result, represented as a quad-precision floating-point number, to floating-point register rd.
RULE 3: When the input to FROUND.Q is a zero or an infinite value, FROUND.Q copies that value to rd without modification.
RULE 4: If the input to FROUND.Q is a signaling NaN, FROUND.Q sets only the invalid operation exception flag and does not set any other exception flags.
RULE 5: FROUND.Q implements the IEEE-754-2019 roundToIntegral operation and therefore does not perform the roundToIntegralExact variant.

INSTRUCTION: FROUND.S
FORMAT: fround.s fd, fs1, rm
RULE 1: FROUND.S rounds the single-precision floating-point value in rs1 to an integer according to the rounding mode specified in its rm field.
RULE 2: FROUND.S writes the rounded integer to rd encoded as a single-precision floating-point number.
RULE 3: FROUND.S copies zero or infinite inputs from rs1 to rd without modification.
RULE 4: When FROUND.S receives a signaling NaN in rs1, it sets the invalid operation exception flag and does not set any other exception flags.

INSTRUCTION: FROUNDNX.D
FORMAT: froundnx.d fd, fs1, rm
RULE 1: Execution of the FROUNDNX.D instruction rounds the double-precision value in floating-point register rs1 to an integer using the rounding mode specified in the instruction's rm field.
RULE 2: The integer result produced by the FROUNDNX.D instruction is written to floating-point register rd as a double-precision floating-point representation.
RULE 3: If the source operand rs1 of the FROUNDNX.D instruction is a zero or an infinite value, the same value is copied to destination register rd unchanged.
RULE 4: If the source operand rs1 of the FROUNDNX.D instruction is a signaling NaN, the invalid operation exception flag is set.
RULE 5: If the source operand rs1 of the FROUNDNX.D instruction is not NaN and its value differs from the rounded result, the inexact exception flag is set.
RULE 6: The rounding performed by the FROUNDNX.D instruction implements the IEEE 754-2019 roundToIntegralExact operation.

INSTRUCTION: FROUNDNX.Q
FORMAT: froundnx.q fd, fs1, rm
RULE 1: FROUNDNX.Q performs the IEEE 754-2019 roundToIntegralExact operation on its source operand.
RULE 2: FROUNDNX.Q sets the inexact exception flag when the input operand is not NaN and differs from the rounded result.
RULE 3: When the input operand to FROUNDNX.Q is zero or infinite, FROUNDNX.Q writes the same value to the destination register unchanged.
RULE 4: If the input operand to FROUNDNX.Q is a signaling NaN, FROUNDNX.Q sets the invalid operation exception flag and does not set any other exception flags.

INSTRUCTION: FROUNDNX.S
FORMAT: froundnx.s fd, fs1, rm
RULE 1: FROUNDNX.S rounds the single-precision floating-point value in rs1 to an integral value according to the rounding mode specified in its rm field and writes the integral value, represented as a single-precision floating-point number, to rd.
RULE 2: If the input value in rs1 for FROUNDNX.S is +0 or –0, FROUNDNX.S copies that zero value unchanged to rd.
RULE 3: If the input value in rs1 for FROUNDNX.S is +∞ or –∞, FROUNDNX.S copies that infinite value unchanged to rd.
RULE 4: When the input value in rs1 for FROUNDNX.S is a signaling NaN, FROUNDNX.S sets the invalid operation exception flag and does not set any other exception flags.
RULE 5: For a non-NaN input to FROUNDNX.S, the inexact exception flag is set if the rounded integral result differs from the original input value.
RULE 6: For a non-NaN input to FROUNDNX.S, the inexact exception flag is not set when the rounded integral result is identical to the original input value.
RULE 7: FROUNDNX.S implements the IEEE 754-2019 roundToIntegralExact operation.

INSTRUCTION: FSD
FORMAT: fsd fs2, imm12(rs1)
RULE 1: FSD MUST store a double-precision value from a floating-point register to memory.
RULE 2: If the effective address used by FSD is not naturally aligned or the processor XLEN is less than 64, then the store is not guaranteed to be atomic, and a load of the same address immediately afterward may return a value that mixes old and new data.
RULE 3: FSD does not modify any bits of the double-precision value it stores.
RULE 4: FSD preserves the payload of non-canonical NaNs when storing them.
RULE 5: If the Zfinx extension is not implemented, then executing an FSD instruction raises an illegal‑instruction exception (mcause is set to the illegal‑instruction cause).
RULE 6: The FSD operation performed by C.FSDSP stores a double-precision floating-point value from floating-point register rs2 to memory.
RULE 7: The FSD instruction stores a double-precision floating-point value from floating-point register rs2 to memory.
RULE 8: The FSD instruction is available only in implementations that include the RV32DC or RV64DC extensions.
RULE 9: FSD stores the double-precision floating-point value from source floating-point register rs2′ to the memory address obtained by adding a zero-extended offset, scaled by 8, to the base address held in register rs1′.
RULE 10: The offset used by FSD is first zero-extended before being multiplied by 8 in the effective-address calculation.
RULE 11: The effective address for FSD is computed as rs1′ plus the product of the zero-extended offset and 8.
RULE 12: If any memory operation generated by FSD fails PMP, an instruction, load, or store access-fault exception must be generated for the FSD instruction, while any operations that succeeded may already have produced visible side effects.
RULE 13: Each memory operation resulting from FSD is evaluated against PMP entries using the standard priority and matching logic, where the lowest-numbered entry that matches all bytes of the operation determines success or failure based on its L, R, W, and X bits.
RULE 14: For an FSD memory operation executed in M-mode, the operation must succeed when no PMP entry matches; for the same operation executed in S-mode or U-mode, the operation must fail when no PMP entry matches and at least one PMP entry is implemented.
RULE 15: If the effective address of an FSD instruction lies within a page that lacks write permission, the implementation must raise a store page-fault exception.
RULE 16: When an FSD instruction is decomposed into multiple accesses, a store page-fault exception may be raised after one or more of those accesses have completed, and any portion that completed before the fault becomes architecturally visible.
RULE 17: If a standard, non-compressed store instruction that is an FSD traps, the transformed instruction written to the trap-instruction CSR has the format defined for transformed store instructions.
RULE 18: For any other exception that causes an FSD instruction to trap, the trap-instruction CSR must not contain a transformed FSD encoding.
RULE 19: FSD MUST be treated as an explicit memory-access instruction and therefore MUST be subject to the pointer-masking rules that apply to all explicit memory accesses.
RULE 20: If an FSD instruction accesses a memory address that is not 8‑byte aligned while pointer masking is disabled, then the core raises a store/AMO address‑misaligned exception (mcause = 6).
RULE 21: The behavior of a misaligned FSD instruction MUST be identical to applying the pointer-masking transformation to each aligned sub-access that composes the misaligned memory operation.

INSTRUCTION: FSGNJ.D
FORMAT: fsgnj.d fd, fs1, fs2
RULE 1: A register move of a double-precision value can be accomplished with a single FSGNJ.D instruction.

INSTRUCTION: FSGNJ.H
FORMAT: fsgnj.h fd, fs1, fs2
RULE 1: The FSGNJ.H instruction must execute the half-precision floating-point sign-injection operation in the same manner as the single-precision sign-injection instruction defines for single-precision values.

INSTRUCTION: FSGNJ.Q
FORMAT: fsgnj.q fd, fs1, fs2
RULE 1: FSGNJ.Q must copy the sign bit of the second source floating-point operand to the destination floating-point register while preserving the magnitude of the first source operand.
RULE 2: If FSGNJ.Q rd, rs1, rs2 is executed, then rd contains a quad‑precision floating‑point value whose exponent and fraction bits are identical to rs1 and whose sign bit equals the sign bit of rs2.

INSTRUCTION: FSGNJ.S
FORMAT: fsgnj.s fd, fs1, fs2
RULE 1: FSGNJ.S MUST produce a result whose exponent and fraction bits are copied from rs1 and whose sign bit is copied from rs2, and the resulting floating-point value is written to rd.
RULE 2: FSGNJ.S MUST NOT set any floating-point exception flags.
RULE 3: FSGNJ.S MUST NOT canonicalize NaNs and MUST preserve NaN payload bits unchanged.
RULE 4: If rs1 and rs2 are the same register, FSGNJ.S must produce a result identical to that register, effectively moving the value to rd.

INSTRUCTION: FSGNJN.D
FORMAT: fsgnjn.d fd, fs1, fs2
RULE 1: FSGNJN.D produces a double-precision floating-point result whose magnitude is taken from the first source operand and whose sign bit is the logical negation of the sign bit of the second source operand.
RULE 2: FSGNJN.D applies the same sign-injection semantics defined for the single-precision sign-injection instruction, but operates on double-precision (64-bit) floating-point operands.

INSTRUCTION: FSGNJN.H
FORMAT: fsgnjn.h fd, fs1, fs2
RULE 1: FSGNJN.H must compute a half-precision floating-point result whose magnitude equals the magnitude of the value in the first source register and whose sign bit equals the logical complement of the sign bit of the value in the second source register.
RULE 2: If either source operand of FSGNJN.H is a signaling NaN, the instruction must raise the invalid operation exception.
RULE 3: If either source operand of FSGNJN.H is any NaN (signaling or quiet), the result written by FSGNJN.H must be a canonical half-precision NaN.
RULE 4: FSGNJN.H must not raise any floating-point exception other than the optional invalid operation exception specified for signaling NaNs.
RULE 5: The rounding-mode field of the instruction encoding is ignored by FSGNJN.H and does not affect the produced result.

INSTRUCTION: FSGNJN.S
FORMAT: fsgnjn.s fd, fs1, fs2
RULE 1: FSGNJN.S copies all bits except the sign bit from the first source floating-point register (rs1) into the destination register.
RULE 2: FSGNJN.S sets the sign bit of the destination register to the logical complement of the sign bit of the second source floating-point register (rs2).
RULE 3: Execution of FSGNJN.S does not set any floating-point exception flags.
RULE 4: Execution of FSGNJN.S does not canonicalize NaNs, so the NaN payload bits from rs1 are preserved unchanged in the result.
RULE 5: When the two source registers of FSGNJN.S are the same register, the instruction produces a result equal to that operand with its sign bit inverted, effectively performing a floating-point negation.

INSTRUCTION: FSGNJX.D
FORMAT: fsgnjx.d fd, fs1, fs2
RULE 1: FSGNJX.D must produce a double-precision floating-point result whose sign bit equals the exclusive-OR of the sign bits of the two source registers.
RULE 2: FSGNJX.D must copy all remaining bits (the exponent and fraction fields) from the first source register into the result unchanged.
RULE 3: The handling of NaN inputs for FSGNJX.D must be identical to the handling defined for the analogous single-precision sign-injection instruction.

INSTRUCTION: FSGNJX.H
FORMAT: fsgnjx.h fd, fs1, fs2
RULE 1: FSGNJX.H MUST produce a half-precision result whose exponent and fraction fields are copied from the first source operand and whose sign bit is the exclusive-OR of the sign bits of the first and second source operands.
RULE 2: FSGNJX.H MUST write the half-precision result to the destination floating-point register.

INSTRUCTION: FSGNJX.S
FORMAT: fsgnjx.s fd, fs1, fs2
RULE 1: FSGNJX.S produces a result whose non-sign bits are copied verbatim from the source register rs1.
RULE 2: FSGNJX.S sets the sign bit of its result to the exclusive-OR of the sign bits of the source registers rs1 and rs2.
RULE 3: FSGNJX.S writes the resulting floating-point value into the destination register rd.
RULE 4: FSGNJX.S does not modify any floating-point exception flags.
RULE 5: FSGNJX.S does not canonicalize NaNs, leaving NaN payload bits unchanged.

INSTRUCTION: FSH
FORMAT: fsh fs2, imm12(rs1)
RULE 1: If the effective address used by an FSH instruction is not naturally aligned, then the store is not guaranteed to be executed atomically.
RULE 2: FSH must transfer the half-word value without modifying any bits, and must preserve the payloads of non-canonical NaNs in the transferred data.
RULE 3: FSH must ignore all bits of rs2 except the lower 16 bits, using only those low 16 bits as the value to be stored.
RULE 4: The mew bit (inst[28]) in an FSH instruction is unspecified and does not affect the operation of FSH.
RULE 5: When FSH is executed, it stores a 16-bit half-precision floating-point element to memory.
RULE 6: The memory element size for FSH is 16 bits, so the store accesses exactly one 16-bit element in memory.
RULE 7: The source operand for FSH is a floating-point register of width FLEN, and the half-precision value stored is derived from that register.

INSTRUCTION: FSQ
FORMAT: fsq fs2, imm12(rs1)
RULE 1: FSQ stores a 128-bit floating-point value to the memory location specified by its effective address.
RULE 2: FSQ must preserve all bits of the source floating-point value when storing, including the payloads of non-canonical NaNs.
RULE 3: FSQ uses the width field value binary 100 to select the 128-bit memory element width.
RULE 4: If an FSQ instruction accesses memory with an element size not equal to 128 bits, then the architectural result is undefined (e.g., the destination register may contain any value).
RULE 5: If an FSQ instruction has the mew bit (inst[28]) set to 1, then execution raises an illegal‑instruction exception (mcause is set to the illegal‑instruction code and the faulting PC is recorded in mepc).
RULE 6: If the me w field of the FSQ is set to any value, then the instruction executes without raising an exception and the destination register contains the same result as when the me w field is zero.
RULE 7: FSQ MUST be subject to pointer masking because pointer masking applies to all explicit memory accesses.

INSTRUCTION: FSUB.Q
FORMAT: fsub.q fd, fs1, fs2, rm
RULE 1: FSUB.Q must subtract the quad-precision floating-point value in the source register identified by rs2 from the quad-precision floating-point value in the source register identified by rs1 and produce a quad-precision result.
RULE 2: FSUB.Q must interpret a fmt field value of 11 as selecting the 128-bit quad-precision format for the operation.
RULE 3: FSUB.Q must write the resulting quad-precision value to the floating-point register specified by the rd field.

INSTRUCTION: FSUB.S
FORMAT: fsub.s fd, fs1, fs2, rm
RULE 1: FSUB.S performs a single-precision floating-point subtraction of the value in rs2 from the value in rs1 and writes the resulting single-precision value to rd.
RULE 2: The rounding mode specified by the rm field determines how the exact result of the FSUB.S subtraction is rounded to a representable single-precision value.

INSTRUCTION: FSW
FORMAT: fsw fs2, imm12(rs1)
RULE 1: FSW stores the 32-bit single-precision value held in floating-point register rs2 to the memory location whose effective address is the sum of the contents of integer register rs1 and the 12-bit signed byte offset encoded in the instruction.
RULE 2: FSW must not alter any bits of the value being transferred, and therefore the payload of a non-canonical NaN present in rs2 is preserved unchanged in memory.
RULE 3: The FSW instruction stores a single-precision floating-point value held in floating-point register rs2 to memory.
RULE 4: The immediate offset used by the FSW instruction is zero-extended before being scaled by a factor of 4.
RULE 5: The FSW instruction writes four bytes (a single-precision word) to the memory location whose effective address was computed.
RULE 6: When the compressed instruction C.FSWSP is decoded, it expands to an FSW instruction with the same rs2 and offset(x2) operands, and the resulting FSW instruction has identical architectural behavior to the uncompressed form.
RULE 7: For a non-compressed FSW that traps, the transformed instruction written to mtinst or htinst must preserve the rs2, funct3, and opcode fields of the original FSW.
RULE 8: When an FSW generates any of the synchronous exceptions store/AMO address misaligned, store/AMO access fault, or store/AMO page fault, the implementation must write a transformed standard FSW instruction to the trap-instruction register (mtinst or htinst).
RULE 9: Pointer masking MUST be applied to the effective address of every explicit memory access performed by the FSW instruction.
RULE 10: The FSW instruction supports misaligned stores, subject to the same architectural limitations that apply to misaligned stores when pointer masking is not used.
RULE 11: For a misaligned FSW store, the architectural effect MUST be identical to the result of applying the pointer masking transformation to each constituent aligned byte-wise memory access that would occur without pointer masking.

INSTRUCTION: HFENCE.GVMA
FORMAT: hfence.gvma rs1, rs2
RULE 1: HFENCE.GVMA requires the same permissions and raises the same exceptions as HINVAL.GVMA.
