RULE 10: If the menvcfg.SSE field is 1, execution of the SSAMOSWAP.W instruction MUST raise a virtual-instruction exception.
RULE 11: If satp.MODE (or vsatp.MODE when V=1) is Bare and the effective privilege mode is less than M, execution of SSAMOSWAP.W MUST raise a store/AMO access-fault exception.
RULE 12: If the effective privilege mode is M, execution of SSAMOSWAP.W MUST raise a store/AMO access-fault exception.
RULE 13: SSAMOSWAP.W is one of the only instructions permitted to write to a memory region mapped as a shadow-stack (SS) page, and writes by any other instruction to that region raise a store/AMO access-fault exception.
RULE 14: Stores performed by SSAMOSWAP.W to a shadow-stack page do NOT trigger a store/AMO access-fault exception, which is the fault type that other instructions would raise.
RULE 15: If the memory region accessed by SSAMOSWAP.W is not idempotent, execution of SSAMOSWAP.W MUST raise a store/AMO access-fault exception.
RULE 16: If PMP configuration does not provide read-write permission for the memory accessed by SSAMOSWAP.W, execution of SSAMOSWAP.W MUST raise a store/AMO access-fault exception.
RULE 17: If the PMA attributes of the memory range accessed by SSAMOSWAP.W do not provide AMOSwap level support, execution of SSAMOSWAP.W MUST raise a store/AMO access-fault exception.
RULE 18: If the Zicfiss extension is not implemented and an SSAMOSWAP.W instruction is executed, then the processor raises an illegal‑instruction exception (mcause set to the illegal‑instruction code).
RULE 19: If the address supplied to SSAMOSWAP.W contains bits that should be cleared by pointer masking, then the hardware masks the address and the store updates the memory location at the masked address (address & mask).
RULE 20: The pointer-masked address for SSAMOSWAP.W MUST be computed according to the pointer-masking settings of the effective privilege mode.
RULE 21: The pointer-masking computation for SSAMOSWAP.W MUST incorporate the effects of MPRV, SPVP, and MXR as described for other explicit memory accesses.

INSTRUCTION: SSPOPCHK.X5
FORMAT: sspopchk.x5 sspopchk_x5
RULE 1: SSPOPCHK.X5 MUST use register x5 as its rs1 operand, and using any other register as rs1 causes a software-check exception.
RULE 2: When the CSR xSSE equals 1, SSPOPCHK.X5 performs a load of an XLEN-wide value from the address held in the shadow-stack pointer register ssp, using the same architectural semantics as a normal XLEN load with base ssp.
RULE 3: If the bitwise comparison performed by SSPOPCHK.X5 determines that the loaded value is not equal to the contents of register x5, SSPOPCHK.X5 raises a software-check exception with cause = 18 and xtval set to the string "shadow stack fault (code=3)".
RULE 4: If the bitwise comparison performed by SSPOPCHK.X5 determines that the loaded value is equal to the contents of register x5, SSPOPCHK.X5 increments the shadow-stack pointer register ssp by XLEN/8.
RULE 5: The increment of ssp performed by SSPOPCHK.X5 occurs only if the load from the shadow stack completes without raising any load/store/AMO access-fault exception and no software-check exception is raised.
RULE 6: The software-check exception generated by SSPOPCHK.X5 has lower priority than any load/store/AMO access-fault exception that may be triggered by the same load.
RULE 7: The compressed instruction C.SSPOPCHK x5 expands to SSPOPCHK.X5 and exhibits exactly the same architectural behavior as SSPOPCHK.X5.

INSTRUCTION: SSPUSH.X1
FORMAT: sspush.x1 sspush_x1
RULE 1: The 16-bit C.SSPUSH x1 instruction has the same architectural effect as SSPUSH.X1.
RULE 2: The store performed by SSPUSH.X1 uses the same semantics as a normal XLEN-bit store with an implicit base register of ssp and an implicit offset of –XLEN/8.
RULE 3: SSPUSH.X1 decrements the architectural ssp register by XLEN/8 only after the store to the shadow stack completes successfully.
RULE 4: If the architectural xSSE flag is 0, SSPUSH.X1 does not modify memory and does not change the ssp register.
RULE 5: SSPUSH.X1 writes the XLEN-wide value contained in integer register x1 to the memory address specified by the current shadow-stack pointer (ssp).
RULE 6: After performing the XLEN-wide store, SSPUSH.X1 decrements the shadow-stack pointer (ssp) by XLEN/8.

INSTRUCTION: SSPUSH.X5
FORMAT: sspush.x5 sspush_x5
RULE 1: SSPUSH.X5 MUST push the value contained in register X5 onto the shadow stack.
RULE 2: When SSPUSH.X5 is executed and the architectural xSSE bit is 1, the implementation must store the XLEN-wide value of x5 to the memory address ssp−(XLEN/8).
RULE 3: The store performed by SSPUSH.X5 uses the same architectural semantics as an ordinary XLEN-wide store, with the base address implicitly equal to ssp.
RULE 4: If the store performed by SSPUSH.X5 completes successfully, the implementation must decrement ssp by XLEN/8.
RULE 5: If the store performed by SSPUSH.X5 does not complete successfully, the implementation must not modify ssp.

INSTRUCTION: SSRDP
FORMAT: ssrdp rd
RULE 1: SSRDP reads the current value of the ssp register and writes that value to the integer register specified as the destination operand of the instruction.
RULE 2: If the shadow-stack extension status bit xSSE is 1, executing SSRDP writes the current value of the ssp register into the destination integer register specified by rd.
RULE 3: If the shadow-stack extension status bit xSSE is 0, executing SSRDP writes zero into the destination integer register specified by rd.

INSTRUCTION: SUB
FORMAT: sub rd, rs1, rs2
RULE 1: SUB writes the architectural result of the operation into the register indicated by the rd field.
RULE 2: SUB computes the arithmetic subtraction of the value contained in rs2 from the value contained in rs1.
RULE 3: SUB ignores any overflow that occurs during the subtraction and keeps only the low XLEN bits of the mathematical result.
RULE 4: SUB stores those low XLEN bits into the destination register rd.
RULE 5: The SUB instruction computes the two's-complement XLEN-bit subtraction of the value in source register rs2 from the value in source register rs1 and writes the resulting integer to destination register rd.
RULE 6: When the destination register rd is the same as source register rs1, the SUB instruction replaces the original content of that register with its original value minus the value in rs2, thereby decrementing that register by the amount in rs2.
RULE 7: The SUB instruction MUST compute the value of the first source register minus the value of the second source register and write the XLEN-bit result to the destination register.
RULE 8: The SUB instruction MUST produce its result modulo 2^XLEN, discarding any overflow beyond the XLEN-bit width.
RULE 9: The SUB instruction MUST read the contents of its two source registers and MUST update only the destination register; no other architectural state may be changed.
RULE 10: The SUB instruction MUST NOT raise an exception for arithmetic overflow.
RULE 11: When the second source register holds the value 1, executing SUB on a register MUST decrement that register by one.
RULE 12: If the second source register holds a count of non‑zero bytes, then after executing SUB rd, rs1, rs2 the destination register rd contains the value of rs1 minus that count.

INSTRUCTION: SUBW
FORMAT: subw rd, rs1, rs2
RULE 1: SUBW MUST ignore the upper 32 bits of each source operand when performing the subtraction.
RULE 2: SUBW MUST produce a 32-bit signed subtraction result and sign-extend it to the full XLEN width, so that bits XLEN-1 through 31 of the destination register equal bit 31 of the 32-bit result.
RULE 3: SUBW must subtract the lower 32 bits of the second source register from the lower 32 bits of the first source register, interpreting both operands as signed 32-bit integers.
RULE 4: SUBW must ignore any overflow that results from the signed 32-bit subtraction.
RULE 5: SUBW must write the low 32 bits of the subtraction result, sign-extended to 64 bits, into the destination register.

INSTRUCTION: SW
FORMAT: sw rs2, imm12(rs1)
RULE 1: SW stores a 32-bit value taken from the low bits of register rs2 into memory.
RULE 2: The effective address used by an SW instruction is computed by adding the contents of register rs1 to the sign-extended 12-bit immediate offset.
RULE 3: In a little-endian configuration, an SW instruction writes the least-significant byte of rs2 to the lowest addressed byte and writes the remaining bytes in order of increasing significance.
RULE 4: In a big-endian configuration, an SW instruction writes the most-significant byte of rs2 to the lowest addressed byte and writes the remaining bytes in order of decreasing significance.
RULE 5: When the effective address of an SW instruction is naturally aligned to a 4-byte boundary, the instruction shall not raise an address-misaligned exception.
RULE 6: If an execution environment guarantees that misaligned stores are fully supported, an SW instruction with a misaligned effective address may be performed by hardware, by an invisible trap, or by a combination of hardware and invisible trap, and software shall never experience a contained or fatal address-misaligned trap.
RULE 7: If the execution environment does not guarantee invisible handling of misaligned stores and an SW instruction is issued with a misaligned effective address (address % 4 ≠ 0), then either the store completes and a later load from that address returns the stored word, or a store/AMO address‑misaligned exception is raised (mcause = 6 and mtval contains the faulting address).
RULE 8: If a SW instruction’s effective address is not naturally aligned (not a multiple of 4), then the store is not guaranteed to be atomic and the implementation may raise a store‑address‑misaligned exception (mcause = 0x5).
RULE 9: SW must store the low-order 32 bits of the value in register rs2 to the addressed memory location.
RULE 10: Encodings of the SW instruction that do not set the rl bit are RESERVED.
RULE 11: The SW instruction MUST store the low-order 32 bits of register rs2 to the memory location whose address equals the effective address computed for the instruction.
RULE 12: SW must store the 32-bit value contained in source register rs2′ to the memory location whose effective address is computed for the instruction.
RULE 13: The SW instruction must write a 32-bit word to the memory location whose effective address is the sum of the base register and the sign-extended immediate offset.
RULE 14: Pointer masking does not affect software reads or writes of CSRs, but when SW uses an address obtained from a CSR, the address shall be masked if pointer masking is enabled for the executing privilege mode.

INSTRUCTION: UNZIP
FORMAT: unzip rd, rs1
RULE 1: UNZIP performs a bit-deinterleave operation on the XLEN-bit input value from rs to produce the output value written to rd.
RULE 2: When an UNZIP instruction is executed, for each integer i from 0 to XLEN/2-1 the bit at position i+XLEN/2 of the destination register X(rd) is set to the bit 2·i+1 of the source register X(rs1).
RULE 3: Execution of an UNZIP instruction does not modify the contents of the source register X(rs1).
RULE 4: The UNZIP instruction is the inverse of the ZIP instruction, such that applying UNZIP to a value that was produced by ZIP of the same operand yields the original operand value.
RULE 5: The UNZIP operation assigns, for every index i from 0 to XLEN/2-1, X(rd)[i] = X(rs)[2*i] and X(rd)[i + XLEN/2] = X(rs)[2*i + 1].
RULE 6: For any 32-bit value V, executing UNZIP on a register containing V and then executing ZIP on the resulting register must produce V in the final destination register.
RULE 7: For any 32-bit value V, executing ZIP on a register containing V and then executing UNZIP on the resulting register must produce V in the final destination register.
RULE 8: UNZIP performs a bit-interleave (de-interleave) operation on the bits of the value in source register rs1 and writes the resulting value to destination register rd.

INSTRUCTION: VAADD.VV
FORMAT: vaadd.vv vd, vs2, vs1, vm
RULE 1: VAADD.VV must, for each element i whose index is less than the active vector length and whose mask bit is true, compute the infinite-precision signed sum of vs2[i] and vs1[i], right-shift the sum by one bit, round the shifted value according to the current setting of the vector rounding-mode register vxrm, and write the rounded result to vd[i].
RULE 2: VAADD.VV must perform the addition and averaging in infinite precision before any rounding or truncation to the destination element width.
RULE 3: If the exact sum of the paired source elements cannot be represented in the destination element width, then VAADD.VV rounds the sum to the nearest representable value, stores that value in the destination element, and does not raise any overflow exception.
RULE 4: If VAADD.VV is executed in a privilege mode or with vector configuration that would cause VAADDU.VV to raise an illegal‑instruction or vector exception, then VAADD.VV raises the same exception and mcause is set to the identical cause code.
RULE 5: For any element i whose mask bit is false, VAADD.VV must leave vd[i] unchanged.

INSTRUCTION: VAADDU.VV
FORMAT: vaaddu.vv vd, vs2, vs1, vm
RULE 1: If VAADDU.VV does not compute each element as the infinite‑precision unsigned sum of vs1[i] and vs2[i] (i.e., it truncates the result to the element width), then the corresponding destination vector element contains a value different from the exact sum (it equals (vs1[i] + vs2[i]) mod 2^ELEN).
RULE 2: VAADDU.VV must right-shift the infinite-precision sum of vs2[i] and vs1[i] by one bit before any rounding is applied.
RULE 3: VAADDU.VV must round the right-shifted value for each element i according to the current vxrm rounding mode and must write the rounded result to the destination element vd[i] when the corresponding mask bit vm[i] is 1.
RULE 4: VAADDU.VV must leave destination element vd[i] unchanged for any element i whose mask bit vm[i] is 0.
RULE 5: VAADDU.VV must never produce an overflow; the result is defined for all possible operand values.
RULE 6: VAADDU.VV must perform all arithmetic in infinite precision prior to the right shift and rounding steps.

INSTRUCTION: VAADDU.VX
FORMAT: vaaddu.vx vd, vs2, rs1, vm
RULE 1: VAADDU.VX must read the unsigned integer scalar operand from integer register rs1, read each unsigned integer element of source vector register vs2, write the resulting unsigned integer elements to destination vector register vd, and apply the optional mask vm if it is present.
RULE 2: VAADDU.VX must right-shift the infinite-precision sum for each element i by one bit and then round the shifted value according to the current setting of the vxrm rounding-mode register.
RULE 3: VAADDU.VX must never cause an overflow in any result element, because overflow cannot occur for this instruction.

INSTRUCTION: VADC.VIM
FORMAT: vadc.vim vd, vs2, simm5, v0
RULE 1: VADC.VIM computes for each element i the result vd[i] = vs2[i] + imm + v0.mask[i].
RULE 2: VADC.VIM writes the computed result to every body element of the destination register even when the corresponding mask bit is clear.
RULE 3: VADC.VIM uses the mask bits of the implicit vector register v0 as the carry-in bits for each element addition.

INSTRUCTION: VADC.VVM
FORMAT: vadc.vvm vd, vs2, vs1, v0
RULE 1: For each element i within the current active vector length, VADC.VVM computes vd[i] as the sum of vs2[i], vs1[i], and the corresponding mask bit v0.mask[i].
RULE 2: VADC.VVM is defined only for encodings with the mask bit vm equal to 0, and any encoding with vm equal to 1 is reserved.
RULE 3: VADC.VVM obtains the carry-in for each element from the mask bits of the implicit vector register v0.
RULE 4: VADC.VVM must not use v0 as the destination register; an encoding that specifies v0 for vd is reserved.
RULE 5: VADC.VVM writes the computed result to every element of the destination register vd for the entire active vector length, regardless of any element mask.
RULE 6: VADC.VVM must obey the general vector register source/destination overlap restrictions, and any encoding that violates those restrictions is reserved.

INSTRUCTION: VADC.VXM
FORMAT: vadc.vxm vd, vs2, rs1, v0
RULE 1: VADC.VXM computes vd[i] = vs2[i] + x[rs1] + v0.mask[i].
RULE 2: Despite being encoded as a masked instruction, VADC.VXM produces a result for every element and writes back all elements of the destination vector register regardless of the mask bits.
RULE 3: The encoding of VADC.VXM is reserved when the destination vector register vd is v0.
RULE 4: The carry-in used by VADC.VXM for each element i must be taken from the mask bit v0.mask[i] of the implicit v0 register.
RULE 5: VADC.VXM may write its result to any vector register that satisfies the standard source/destination overlap restrictions.

INSTRUCTION: VADD.VV
FORMAT: vadd.vv vd, vs2, vs1, vm
RULE 1: VADD.VV adds each element of the first source vector register group vs2 to the corresponding element of the second source vector register group vs1 and writes the integer sum to the destination vector register group vd.
RULE 2: When vm is present, VADD.VV performs the element-wise addition only for those element positions where the corresponding mask bit in vm is true, leaving other element positions of vd unchanged.

INSTRUCTION: VADD.VX
FORMAT: vadd.vx vd, vs2, rs1, vm
RULE 1: VADD.VX performs integer addition of the scalar integer in integer register rs1 to each element of source vector register vs2 and writes the resulting elements to destination vector register vd.
RULE 2: When a mask register vm is supplied, VADD.VX updates only those elements of vd whose corresponding mask bit is true and leaves the other elements unchanged.

INSTRUCTION: VAESDF.VS
FORMAT: vaesdf.vs vd, vs2
RULE 1: For VAESDF.VS any SEW value other than 32 is a reserved encoding.
RULE 2: For VAESDF.VS the vd register group overlapping the vs2 scalar element group is a reserved encoding.
RULE 3: VAESDF.VS writes the new round state to the same vd register group as output.
RULE 4: VAESDF.VS performs a final-round AES block-cipher decryption.
RULE 5: VAESDF.VS raises an illegal-instruction exception and retires with RETIRE_FAIL when LMUL × VLEN < EGW.
RULE 6: VAESDF.VS retires with RETIRE_SUCCESS when execution completes without triggering an illegal-instruction exception.

INSTRUCTION: VAESDF.VV
FORMAT: vaesdf.vv vd, vs2
RULE 1: VAESDF.VV raises an illegal-instruction exception and does not retire when LMUL × VLEN is less than the element group width (EGW) of 128 bits.
RULE 2: VAESDF.VV writes the 128-bit result of the XOR back to element group i of the destination register vd, overwriting the original round-state element.

INSTRUCTION: VAESDM.VS
FORMAT: vaesdm.vs vd, vs2
RULE 1: If LMUL * VLEN is less than 128 bits (the element width EGW), executing VAESDM.VS raises an illegal-instruction exception and the instruction retires with failure.
RULE 2: VAESDM.VS writes the 128-bit result of the inverse MixColumns step back to the corresponding element group of vd, producing the new round state.
RULE 3: For each active element group, VAESDM.VS selects scalar element index 0 of vs2 as the round key used in the XOR operation.

INSTRUCTION: VAESDM.VV
FORMAT: vaesdm.vv vd, vs2
RULE 1: If the SEW field of a VAESDM.VV encoding is any value other than 32, the encoding is reserved.
RULE 2: For the VAESDM.VS form only, if the vd register group overlaps the vs2 scalar element group, the encoding of VAESDM.VV is reserved.
RULE 3: VAESDM.VV raises an illegal-instruction exception and causes RETIRE_FAIL when LMUL multiplied by VLEN is less than 128 bits.
RULE 4: VAESDM.VV computes the number of element groups to process as the integer division of VL by 4 and the starting element group index as the integer division of VSTART by 4.
RULE 5: VAESDM.VV applies the AES inverse ShiftRows transformation to the round-state element read from Vd.
RULE 6: VAESDM.VV applies the AES inverse SubBytes transformation to the result of the inverse ShiftRows step.
RULE 7: VAESDM.VV XORs the output of the inverse SubBytes step with the round-key element read from Vs2.
RULE 8: VAESDM.VV applies the AES inverse MixColumns transformation to the XOR result.
RULE 9: VAESDM.VV writes the 128-bit result of the inverse MixColumns step back to the same element group i of Vd, replacing the original round state.

INSTRUCTION: VAESEF.VS
FORMAT: vaesef.vs vd, vs2
RULE 1: VAESEF.VS must be encoded with SEW equal to 32, and any encoding with a different SEW value is defined as a reserved encoding.
RULE 2: VAESEF.VS is a reserved encoding when the destination register group vd overlaps the scalar element group of the source register vs2.
RULE 3: If VAESEF.VS executes, then each 128‑bit element of the destination vector register vd is replaced with the result of the AES final‑round encryption function applied to the corresponding element group.
RULE 4: VAESEF.VS applies the AES SubBytes forward transformation to the 128-bit state element obtained from vd for each element group.
RULE 5: VAESEF.VS applies the AES ShiftRows forward transformation to the result of the SubBytes step for each element group.
RULE 6: VAESEF.VS XORs the shifted-row state with the round key taken from the scalar element group of vs2 (element index 0) for each element group.
RULE 7: VAESEF.VS writes the XOR result back into the corresponding element group of vd, thereby updating the round state.
RULE 8: VAESEF.VS causes an illegal-instruction exception and retirement failure when LMUL × VLEN is less than the effective group width of 128 bits.
RULE 9: When LMUL × VLEN is at least 128 bits, VAESEF.VS computes the number of element groups as vl divided by the element-group size (4) and starts processing at vstart divided by the element-group size.
RULE 10: For each element-group index i from the start index up to (but not including) the computed length, VAESEF.VS uses the i-th element of vd as the state and the element 0 of vs2 as the round key.

INSTRUCTION: VAESEF.VV
FORMAT: vaesef.vv vd, vs2
RULE 1: VAESEF.VV encodings with a SEW field value other than 32 are reserved.
RULE 2: VAESEF.VV writes the resulting new round state to the destination vector register vd.
RULE 3: If the product of LMUL and VLEN is less than the effective group width of 128 bits, VAESEF.VV raises an illegal-instruction exception and does not retire successfully.
RULE 4: If LMUL × VLEN is at least 128 bits, VAESEF.VV processes each element group i from floor(vstart/EGS) to floor(vl/EGS)-1 by reading the 128-bit round-state from vd, reading the corresponding 128-bit round-key from vs2, applying the AES forward SubBytes transformation, then the AES forward ShiftRows transformation, XORing the shifted result with the round key, and writing the 128-bit result back to vd at the same element group index.
RULE 5: If all selected element groups have been processed, then VAESEF.VV retires without raising an exception.

INSTRUCTION: VAESEM.VS
FORMAT: vaesem.vs vd, vs2
RULE 1: VAESEM.VS encoding is reserved when the selected element width (SEW) is any value other than 32.
RULE 2: VAESEM.VS encoding is reserved for the .vs form when the destination register group vd overlaps the scalar element group of the source register vs2.
RULE 3: VAESEM.VS raises an illegal-instruction exception and results in RETIRE_FAIL if LMUL multiplied by VLEN is less than the effective group width of 128 bits.
RULE 4: VAESEM.VS applies the AES SubBytes forward transformation to the round-state value.
RULE 5: VAESEM.VS applies the AES ShiftRows forward transformation to the result of SubBytes.
RULE 6: VAESEM.VS applies the AES MixColumns forward transformation to the result of ShiftRows.
RULE 7: If the VAESEM.VS instruction does not XOR the MixColumns output with the supplied round key, then the destination vector register will contain a value that differs from the mathematically expected new round state.
RULE 8: VAESEM.VS writes the new round state back to the corresponding element group i of vd.

INSTRUCTION: VAESEM.VV
FORMAT: vaesem.vv vd, vs2
RULE 1: VAESEM.VV raises an illegal-instruction exception and retires fail when LMUL multiplied by VLEN is less than 128 bits, because the vector register group cannot hold the required 128-bit element group.
RULE 2: When LMUL multiplied by VLEN is at least 128 bits, VAESEM.VV executes without raising an exception.
RULE 3: VAESEM.VV applies the AES SubBytes forward transformation to each 128-bit round-state element group taken from the input vector register vd.
RULE 4: VAESEM.VV applies the AES ShiftRows forward transformation to the output of the SubBytes step for each round-state element group.
RULE 5: VAESEM.VV applies the AES MixColumns forward transformation to the output of the ShiftRows step for each round-state element group.
RULE 6: For each element group i, VAESEM.VV XORs the result of the MixColumns step with the round-key element taken from element group i of vector register vs2.
RULE 7: VAESEM.VV writes the XOR result back to element group i of the destination vector register vd, replacing the original round-state element.
RULE 8: VAESEM.VV performs the AES middle-round encryption function on each 128-bit element group of the input round state.

INSTRUCTION: VAESKF1.VI
FORMAT: vaeskf1.vi vd, vs2, zimm5
RULE 1: If the selected element width (SEW) is not 32, the encoding for VAESKF1.VI is reserved.
RULE 2: The immediate field uimm[3:0] supplies the round number for VAESKF1.VI, and uimm[4] is ignored.
RULE 3: For VAESKF1.VI, if the round number extracted from uimm[3:0] is 0 or greater than 10, the effective round number is obtained by inverting bit 3 of uimm[3:0], mapping 0 to 8 and values 11-15 to 3-7.
RULE 4: The effective round number for VAESKF1.VI determines the round constant used in the first word generation via aes_decode_rcon.
RULE 5: If LMUL multiplied by VLEN is less than 128 bits, execution of VAESKF1.VI raises an illegal-instruction exception.
RULE 6: For each element group processed by VAESKF1.VI, the most-significant 32-bit word of the current round key (word 3) is rotated, substituted with aes_subword_fwd, XORed with the round constant derived from (round-1), and XORed with the least-significant word (word 0) to produce the first word of the next round key.
RULE 7: For each element group processed by VAESKF1.VI, the second word of the next round key is computed as the XOR of the first generated word and the second word of the current round key.
RULE 8: For each element group processed by VAESKF1.VI, the third word of the next round key is computed as the XOR of the second generated word and the third word of the current round key.
RULE 9: For each element group processed by VAESKF1.VI, the fourth word of the next round key is computed as the XOR of the third generated word and the fourth word of the current round key.
RULE 10: If the four 32-bit words generated for an element group by VAESKF1.VI are not written to the corresponding element group of the destination vector register vd, then that element group of vd will not contain the expected generated values (i.e., the register holds incorrect or unchanged data for that group).
RULE 11: When the pre-condition LMUL*VLEN >= 128 bits holds, VAESKF1.VI completes without raising an exception and retires successfully.

INSTRUCTION: VAESKF2.VI
FORMAT: vaeskf2.vi vd, vs2, zimm5
RULE 1: Encodings of VAESKF2.VI with SEW other than 32 are reserved.
RULE 2: If LMUL × VLEN is less than 128 (the EGW) then VAESKF2.VI MUST raise an illegal-instruction exception and retire as a failure.
RULE 3: VAESKF2.VI obtains the round number from the low 4 bits of the immediate (uimm[3:0]) and ignores uimm[4].
RULE 4: When the extracted 4-bit round number is less than 2 or greater than 14, VAESKF2.VI maps it into the valid range by inverting bit 3 of the immediate.
RULE 5: VAESKF2.VI maps out-of-range round numbers 0-1 to 8-9 and 15 to 7 by inverting bit 3 of the immediate.
RULE 6: VAESKF2.VI performs a single forward AES-256 key-schedule round producing the next round key from the previous round key supplied in vd and the current round key supplied in vs2.
RULE 7: For each element group, VAESKF2.VI computes the most-significant 32-bit word w[0] by applying aes_subword_fwd to the most-significant word of the current round key, optionally preceded by aes_rotword when the least-significant bit of the round number is 0, then XORing the result with the appropriate round constant and with the corresponding word from the previous round key.
RULE 8: When the least-significant bit of the round number is 1, VAESKF2.VI computes w[0] as aes_subword_fwd applied to the most-significant word of the current round key XORed with the corresponding word from the previous round key, without using a round constant.
RULE 9: When the least-significant bit of the round number is 0, VAESKF2.VI computes w[0] as aes_subword_fwd applied to aes_rotword of the most-significant word of the current round key XORed with the decoded round constant derived from (round number>>1)-1 and XORed with the corresponding word from the previous round key.
RULE 10: VAESKF2.VI computes w[1] as w[0] XOR the second 32-bit word of the previous round key.
RULE 11: VAESKF2.VI computes w[2] as w[1] XOR the third 32-bit word of the previous round key.
RULE 12: VAESKF2.VI computes w[3] as w[2] XOR the fourth 32-bit word of the previous round key.
RULE 13: VAESKF2.VI writes the four 32-bit words w[3:0] back to the destination vector register vd at the same element-group index from which the source elements were read.
RULE 14: VAESKF2.VI processes element groups starting at the index given by vstart divided by 4 up to (vl divided by 4) minus 1, inclusive.
RULE 15: VAESKF2.VI operates on 32-bit element width (EEW = 32) for all intermediate and final computations.

INSTRUCTION: VAESZ.VS
FORMAT: vaesz.vs vd, vs2
RULE 1: If the selected standard element width (SEW) for VAESZ.VS is not 32 bits, the encoding is reserved.
RULE 2: If the destination register group (vd) overlaps the source register group (vs2) for VAESZ.VS, the encoding is reserved.
RULE 3: For each 128-bit element group of vd, VAESZ.VS writes the bitwise XOR of that element group and the 128-bit round key from vs2 back to the same element group of vd.
RULE 4: If the current vector start index (vstart) is not a multiple of the element group size (EGS) for VAESZ.VS, an illegal-instruction exception is raised and the instruction does not retire successfully.
RULE 5: If the product of LMUL and VLEN is less than the effective group width (EGW) for VAESZ.VS, an illegal-instruction exception is raised and the instruction does not retire successfully.
RULE 6: VAESZ.VS processes element groups starting at index vstart/EGS up to (vl/EGS)−1, inclusive, applying the XOR operation to each group.
RULE 7: If VAESZ.VS finishes processing all applicable element groups without error, then it retires normally (no exception) and the destination register contains the expected result.
RULE 8: If a VAESZ.VS instruction is executed with an effective element width other than 32 bits, an effective group width other than 128 bits, or an effective group size other than 4 elements, then the core raises an illegal‑instruction exception; otherwise the instruction operates with EEW = 32 bits, EGW = 128 bits, and EGS = 4 elements.

INSTRUCTION: VAND.VI
FORMAT: vand.vi vd, vs2, simm5, vm
RULE 1: VAND.VI takes a destination vector register operand named vd and writes the result of the operation to vd.
RULE 2: VAND.VI may include an optional mask register operand named vm that controls which elements are updated.
RULE 3: When a mask register vm is supplied, VAND.VI updates only those elements of vd for which the corresponding mask bit is set, leaving other elements of vd unchanged.
RULE 4: When the mask register vm is omitted, VAND.VI behaves as if all mask bits are set and updates every active element of vd.
RULE 5: For each active element, VAND.VI computes the bitwise logical AND of the element from vs2 and the immediate imm and writes the result to the corresponding element of vd.
RULE 6: Applying VAND.VI with an immediate operand that is the bitwise inversion of a given constant produces the same architectural effect as a ".vi" form of the VANDN instruction would have produced.

INSTRUCTION: VASUB.VV
FORMAT: vasub.vv vd, vs2, vs1, vm
RULE 1: VASUB.VV MUST compute each destination element i as roundoff_signed(vs2[i] - vs1[i], 1), where roundoff_signed denotes a right-shift-by-one-bit followed by rounding according to the current vxrm rounding mode.
RULE 2: VASUB.VV MUST perform the subtraction vs2[i] - vs1[i] in infinite precision before any right-shift-by-one-bit or rounding is applied.
RULE 3: If the result of VASUB.VV exceeds the representable signed-integer range of the element, the overflow is ignored and the result wraps around modulo the element width.
RULE 4: Overflow for VASUB.VV can only occur when subtracting the smallest representable signed integer from the largest representable signed integer while vxrm is set to either rnu or rne.

INSTRUCTION: VASUB.VX
FORMAT: vasub.vx vd, vs2, rs1, vm
RULE 1: If VASUB.VX is executed, then each element of the destination vector register contains the signed saturating subtraction of the corresponding element of source vector vs2 and the scalar value in rs1.
RULE 2: VASUB.VX computes for each element i the infinite-precision value of vs2[i] minus the scalar value in rs1 before any rounding is applied.
RULE 3: VASUB.VX right-shifts the infinite-precision subtraction result for each element by one bit prior to rounding.
RULE 4: VASUB.VX rounds the one-bit-right-shifted result for each element according to the rounding mode specified in the vxrm CSR.
RULE 5: VASUB.VX stores the final rounded value for each element into the corresponding element of the destination vector vd.
RULE 6: VASUB.VX ignores arithmetic overflow and the resulting element value wraps around modulo the element width.

INSTRUCTION: VASUBU.VX
FORMAT: vasubu.vx vd, vs2, rs1, vm
RULE 1: VASUBU.VX must compute the subtraction of the unsigned integer value in scalar register rs1 from each unsigned element of source vector register vs2 using infinite-precision arithmetic before any rounding or truncation.
RULE 2: VASUBU.VX must right-shift the infinite-precision subtraction result by one bit and round the shifted value according to the unsigned rounding mode specified in the vxrm field.
RULE 3: VASUBU.VX must produce the final destination element by truncating the rounded value to the element width, with any overflow from the subtraction ignored and the result wrapping around modulo the element size.

INSTRUCTION: VBREV8.V
FORMAT: vbrev8.v vd, vs2, vm
RULE 1: VBREV8.V reverses the order of the eight bits in each byte element of the source vector and writes the resulting bytes to the corresponding elements of the destination vector.
RULE 2: VBREV8.V is defined only in the Zvkb extension; an implementation that does not implement Zvkb treats the VBREV8.V encoding as reserved.
RULE 3: VBREV8.V treats each processed element as SEW bits and reverses the bit order within each byte of the element while preserving the positions of the bytes.
RULE 4: VBREV8.V is defined for every supported standard element width (SEW), allowing the byte-wise bit-reversal operation to be applied regardless of the element size.
RULE 5: VBREV8.V does not modify any destination elements whose indices lie outside the active element range from vstart to vl.
RULE 6: If all architectural preconditions for VBREV8.V are satisfied, then the instruction retires normally and no exception is raised.

INSTRUCTION: VBREV.V
FORMAT: vbrev.v vd, vs2, vm
RULE 1: VBREV.V reverses the order of bits within each element of the source vector and writes the reversed elements to the destination vector register.
RULE 2: VBREV.V must reverse the order of bits in each element of the source vector register vs2 and write the resulting elements to the destination vector register vd.
RULE 3: For each element index i from the current vector start index vstart up to vl-1, VBREV.V reads the SEW-bit element from vs2 and writes to the corresponding element of vd a value whose bit at position (SEW-1-b) equals the bit at position b of the source element for every bit position b in the range 0 to SEW-1.
RULE 4: VBREV.V must perform the bit-reversal operation independently for each element within the active vector length and must not modify elements outside the range vstart to vl-1.

INSTRUCTION: VCLMUL.VV
FORMAT: vclmul.vv vd, vs2, vs1, vm
RULE 1: Encodings of VCLMUL.VV where the selected element width (SEW) is not 64 are reserved.
RULE 2: For each active element index i, VCLMUL.VV computes a 64-bit product by performing a carry-less multiplication of the two 64-bit operands using the algorithm: start with a zero result, and for each bit position j from 0 to 63, if the j-th bit of the multiplicand is 1 then XOR the result with the multiplier left-shifted by j.
RULE 3: The 64-bit product produced by VCLMUL.VV is the least-significant half of the full 128-bit carry-less product of the two 64-bit operands.
RULE 4: For each active element index i, VCLMUL.VV writes the computed 64-bit product into element i of destination register vd.
RULE 5: VCLMUL.VV completes execution and retires successfully without raising an exception when encoded with SEW equal to 64.

INSTRUCTION: VCLMUL.VX
FORMAT: vclmul.vx vd, vs2, rs1, vm
RULE 1: VCLMUL.VX is only defined for a selected element width (SEW) of 64 bits; any encoding that specifies an SEW value other than 64 is reserved.
RULE 2: VCLMUL.VX iterates over element indices i from the current vector start (vstart) to the active vector length minus one (vl-1) and performs the multiplication for each selected element.
RULE 3: VCLMUL.VX stores the least-significant 64 bits of the 128-bit carry-less product into the corresponding element of the destination vector register vd.
RULE 4: VCLMUL.VX leaves elements of vd whose indices are outside the range from vstart to vl-1 unchanged.
RULE 5: VCLMUL.VX completes execution without raising an exception and retires successfully.

INSTRUCTION: VCLMULH.VV
FORMAT: vclmulh.vv vd, vs2, vs1, vm
RULE 1: If the selected element width (SEW) for a VCLMULH.VV instruction is any value other than 64, the VCLMULH.VV encoding is reserved.
RULE 2: VCLMULH.VV writes to destination vector register vd the 64-bit result of the carry-less high-half multiplication for each active element index i.
RULE 3: For each active element index i from vstart up to vl-1, VCLMULH.VV computes a 64-bit product by invoking the clmulh function with operands op1 = vs1[i], op2 = vs2[i], and width = 64.
RULE 4: The final value of the result after processing all bit positions 1 through 63 is the value stored by VCLMULH.VV into vd[i].
RULE 5: VCLMULH.VV produces the most significant 64 bits of the 128-bit carry-less product of the corresponding 64-bit elements of vs2 and vs1.
RULE 6: If VCLMULH.VV is executed and all active elements are processed according to the defined algorithm, then the instruction completes without raising any exception and retires successfully.

INSTRUCTION: VCLMULH.VX
FORMAT: vclmulh.vx vd, vs2, rs1, vm
RULE 1: VCLMULH.VX MUST produce for each active element i the most-significant 64 bits of the 128-bit carry-less product of the 64-bit element from vs2 and the 64-bit integer value in rs1.
RULE 2: VCLMULH.VX writes the 64-bit result of the carry-less high-half multiplication into the destination vector register vd at the same element index i for all active elements.
RULE 3: VCLMULH.VX is defined only for SEW equal to 64; any encoding that selects an SEW other than 64 is reserved.
RULE 4: If VCLMULH.VX does not process all active elements, then the instruction does not retire with RETIRE_SUCCESS.

INSTRUCTION: VCLZ.V
FORMAT: vclz.v vd, vs2, vm
RULE 1: VCLZ.V MUST examine each element using the current SEW as the element width and scan bits from the most-significant bit (index SEW-1) down to the least-significant bit until a 1-bit is encountered.
RULE 2: If the scanned element of vs2 contains at least one 1-bit, VCLZ.V MUST set the destination element to SEW-1 minus the index of the first 1-bit encountered during the scan.
RULE 3: If the scanned element of vs2 is zero-valued, VCLZ.V MUST set the corresponding destination element to the value SEW.
RULE 4: VCLZ.V MUST process elements with indices i such that vstart ≤ i < vl and must not modify elements with indices i ≥ vl.
RULE 5: After completing the element-wise leading-zero count for all active elements, VCLZ.V MUST retire with a successful completion status.

INSTRUCTION: VCOMPRESS.VM
FORMAT: vcompress.vm vd, vs2, vs1
RULE 1: VCOMPRESS.VM compresses elements of the source vector register group selected by the mask register into contiguous elements at the beginning of the destination vector register group.
RULE 2: VCOMPRESS.VM uses the mask register specified by vs1 to select which of the first vl elements of the source vector register group vs2 are extracted and packed into the destination vector register vd.
RULE 3: VCOMPRESS.VM treats any elements of the destination vector register group that are not written by the compress operation as tail elements according to the current tail policy.
RULE 4: VCOMPRESS.VM is encoded with the vm field set to 1; an encoding of the same instruction with vm equal to 0 is reserved.
RULE 5: The destination vector register group must not overlap the source vector register group for VCOMPRESS.VM, otherwise the encoding is reserved.
RULE 6: The destination vector register group must not overlap the source mask register for VCOMPRESS.VM, otherwise the encoding is reserved.
RULE 7: A trap caused by VCOMPRESS.VM must report a vstart value of 0.

INSTRUCTION: VCPOP.M
FORMAT: vcpop.m rd, vs2, vm
RULE 1: VCPOP.M counts the number of active mask elements in the source register that have the value 1 and writes that count to the scalar integer register x[rd].
RULE 2: When a mask register (vm) is specified, VCPOP.M counts only those source mask elements for which the corresponding mask element in vm is true.
RULE 3: VCPOP.M writes the result to x[rd] even when the current vector length (vl) is zero, storing the value 0 in that case.
RULE 4: If VCPOP.M causes a trap, the value of the vstart CSR reported on the trap is always 0.
RULE 5: VCPOP.M raises an illegal-instruction exception if the vstart CSR is non-zero at the time the instruction is executed.
RULE 6: VCPOP.M must not be affected by the Zvkt extension.

INSTRUCTION: VCPOP.V
FORMAT: vcpop.v vd, vs2, vm
RULE 1: VCPOP.V writes the count of set bits for each element into the destination vector register vd, producing the output elements.
RULE 2: VCPOP.V performs a population count on each element of vs2 by counting the number of bits that are 1 in that element.
RULE 3: VCPOP.V processes element indices i starting at the current vstart value and continuing through the last active element index vl-1.
RULE 4: For each processed element i, VCPOP.V computes the output element as the sum of the SEW individual bit values of the input element and stores the result in vd element i with width SEW bits.
RULE 5: VCPOP.V completes execution with a successful retirement status (RETIRE_SUCCESS) and does not raise an architectural exception.

INSTRUCTION: VCTZ.V
FORMAT: vctz.v vd, vs2, vm
RULE 1: The VCTZ.V instruction performs a vector count-trailing-zeros operation on each element of the source vector.
RULE 2: VCTZ.V counts the number of consecutive zero bits starting from the least-significant bit of each element in the source vector register vs2.
RULE 3: If VCTZ.V reads a SEW‑sized element of vs2 for an index i that is less than vstart or greater than or equal to vl, then the corresponding destination vector element i contains an unpredictable value.
RULE 4: For a given element, VCTZ.V increments a counter j from 0 upward while the j-th bit of the element is 0 and stops when a bit equal to 1 is encountered.
RULE 5: When VCTZ.V encounters a 1 bit at position j in an element, it writes the integer value j into the corresponding element of the destination vector register vd.
RULE 6: If VCTZ.V scans all SEW bits of an element without finding a 1 bit, it writes the integer value SEW into the corresponding element of vd.
RULE 7: VCTZ.V writes each result using the same element width (SEW) as the source element.
RULE 8: If VCTZ.V completes the element‑wise computation for all active vector elements, then the instruction retires successfully (no exception is raised and the destination vector register contains the trailing‑zero count for each element).

INSTRUCTION: VDIV.VV
FORMAT: vdiv.vv vd, vs2, vs1, vm
RULE 1: VDIV.VV is a signed divide vector-vector instruction that reads two source vector registers vs2 and vs1 and writes the elementwise results to a destination vector register vd, optionally applying a mask vm.
RULE 2: For each element whose corresponding mask vm bit is 1, VDIV.VV must compute the signed quotient of the vs2 element divided by the vs1 element, rounding toward zero, and must store that quotient into the corresponding element of vd.
RULE 3: For each element whose corresponding mask vm bit is 0, VDIV.VV must leave the corresponding element of vd unchanged.
RULE 4: The signed quotient produced by VDIV.VV for any active element must be identical to the result of the RISC-V scalar signed division operation on the same operand values, including extreme-input cases.
RULE 5: When the divisor element (vs1) is zero, VDIV.VV must produce the same quotient as the scalar signed division by zero, which is all 1s (i.e., -1) for the element width.
RULE 6: When the dividend element is the most-negative integer and the divisor element is -1, VDIV.VV must produce the same quotient as the scalar signed division, which is the most-negative integer (no overflow).

INSTRUCTION: VDIV.VX
FORMAT: vdiv.vx vd, vs2, rs1, vm
RULE 1: VDIV.VX is a vector-scalar instruction that divides each element of source vector register vs2 by the signed integer value in scalar integer register rs1, stores the signed quotient into the corresponding element of destination vector register vd, and applies the vector mask vm to select active elements.
RULE 2: VDIV.VX performs signed integer division according to the RISC-V signed-division rules.
RULE 3: For every active element, VDIV.VX produces the identical quotient as a scalar signed-integer divide instruction executed with the same dividend element and the same divisor value, including all extreme input cases.
RULE 4: When rs1 contains zero, VDIV.VX writes the value –1 (all bits 1) to each active element of vd, matching the result of a scalar signed divide by zero.
RULE 5: When an active dividend element is the most-negative representable integer and rs1 contains –1, VDIV.VX writes the most-negative integer to that element of vd, matching the scalar signed-divide overflow result.

INSTRUCTION: VDIVU.VV
FORMAT: vdivu.vv vd, vs2, vs1, vm
RULE 1: VDIVU.VV performs an element-wise unsigned integer division of the corresponding elements of the source vector register vs2 by the elements of source vector register vs1, producing a quotient for each element in the destination vector register vd.
RULE 2: For every element, the quotient produced by VDIVU.VV MUST be identical to the result of the scalar unsigned divide operation defined by the RISC-V integer divide instruction, including the results for extreme input values.

INSTRUCTION: VDIVU.VX
FORMAT: vdivu.vx vd, vs2, rs1, vm
RULE 1: VDIVU.VX reads the unsigned integer elements of source vector register vs2 and the unsigned integer value in scalar register rs1, divides each element of vs2 by the value in rs1, and writes the resulting quotient to the corresponding element of destination vector register vd while applying the element-mask vm so that only elements with a mask bit set are updated.
RULE 2: VDIVU.VX must produce exactly the same quotient results as the RISC-V scalar unsigned integer divide instruction for every element, including the defined results for extreme cases such as division by zero or overflow.

INSTRUCTION: VFADD.VV
FORMAT: vfadd.vv vd, vs2, vs1, vm
RULE 1: In the VFADD.VV syntax, vs2 and vs1 specify the two source vector registers whose elements are added element-wise.
RULE 2: In the VFADD.VV syntax, vm specifies the mask register that controls which destination elements are written.

INSTRUCTION: VFCVT.F.X.V
FORMAT: vfcvt.f.x.v vd, vs2, vm
RULE 1: VFCVT.F.X.V converts each signed integer element in the source vector register vs2 to a floating-point element in the destination vector register vd, with both source and destination elements having the same SEW-wide size.
RULE 2: If a source integer element would trigger an exceptional condition in the scalar signed‑integer‑to‑floating‑point conversion (e.g., overflow or invalid operation), then executing VFCVT.F.X.V raises the same floating‑point exception flags in fflags and writes the same result value for that element as the scalar conversion would.
RULE 3: VFCVT.F.X.V uses the dynamic rounding mode specified in the frm field for all element conversions.

INSTRUCTION: VFCVT.F.XU.V
FORMAT: vfcvt.f.xu.v vd, vs2, vm
RULE 1: VFCVT.F.XU.V converts each unsigned integer element in the source vector register vs2 to a floating-point element in the destination vector register vd.
RULE 2: VFCVT.F.XU.V operates on vector elements whose size equals the current configured SEW, so source and destination elements are SEW-wide.
RULE 3: VFCVT.F.XU.V uses the dynamic rounding mode specified in the frm field to round the conversion result.
RULE 4: VFCVT.F.XU.V has no rounding-to-zero variant; the rounding mode is always taken from the frm field.
RULE 5: VFCVT.F.XU.V raises the same floating-point exception flags (invalid, overflow, underflow, inexact) as the equivalent scalar unsigned-integer-to-float conversion when those conditions occur.

INSTRUCTION: VFCVT.RTZ.X.F.V
FORMAT: vfcvt.rtz.x.f.v vd, vs2, vm
RULE 1: VFCVT.RTZ.X.F.V converts each floating-point element of the source vector register vs2 to a signed integer element and writes the result to the corresponding element of the destination vector register vd.
RULE 2: VFCVT.RTZ.X.F.V may be executed with an optional mask register vm, and for each element whose mask bit is zero the destination element is not written and retains its previous value.
RULE 3: VFCVT.RTZ.X.F.V must be used only when the element width (SEW) of the source vector register vs2 and the destination vector register vd are identical.
RULE 4: VFCVT.RTZ.X.F.V must round each conversion toward zero, ignoring the dynamic rounding mode specified in frm.
RULE 5: VFCVT.RTZ.X.F.V must handle exceptional conditions (such as overflow, NaN, or inexact results) using the same architectural rules as the corresponding scalar floating-point-to-integer conversion instructions.

INSTRUCTION: VFCVT.RTZ.XU.F.V
FORMAT: vfcvt.rtz.xu.f.v vd, vs2, vm
RULE 1: VFCVT.RTZ.XU.F.V must convert each floating-point element in source vector register vs2 to an unsigned integer element in destination vector register vd by truncating the value toward zero.
RULE 2: VFCVT.RTZ.XU.F.V must round all conversions toward zero and must ignore the dynamic rounding mode specified in the frm field.
RULE 3: VFCVT.RTZ.XU.F.V must apply the same architectural handling of exceptional conditions (such as overflow, NaN, or out-of-range values) as defined for the equivalent scalar floating-point-to-unsigned-integer conversion instructions.

INSTRUCTION: VFCVT.X.F.V
FORMAT: vfcvt.x.f.v vd, vs2, vm
RULE 1: VFCVT.X.F.V must convert each element of the source floating-point vector to a signed integer element in the destination vector.
RULE 2: VFCVT.X.F.V must treat each source and destination element as SEW-wide (single-width) vector elements.
RULE 3: VFCVT.X.F.V must apply the same exceptional-condition handling rules to each element conversion as are defined for the scalar floating-point-to-signed-integer conversion instructions.
RULE 4: VFCVT.X.F.V must use the dynamic rounding mode currently encoded in the floating-point rounding-mode register (frm) for all element conversions.

INSTRUCTION: VFCVT.XU.F.V
FORMAT: vfcvt.xu.f.v vd, vs2, vm
RULE 1: VFCVT.XU.F.V converts each SEW-wide floating-point element in the source vector register vs2 to an unsigned integer of the same SEW and writes the result to the corresponding element of the destination vector register vd.
RULE 2: VFCVT.XU.F.V must use the dynamic rounding mode specified in the frm field for all element conversions.
RULE 3: VFCVT.XU.F.V must handle exceptional conditions (such as NaN, overflow, or inexact) in exactly the same way as the scalar floating-point-to-unsigned-integer conversion instructions.

INSTRUCTION: VFDIV.VF
FORMAT: vfdiv.vf vd, vs2, fs1, vm
RULE 1: VFDIV.VF divides each element of the source vector register vs2 by the scalar floating-point value held in the scalar register rs1 and writes the resulting elements to the destination vector register vd.
RULE 2: VFDIV.VF uses the optional mask register vm to determine which element positions are active; for each element whose mask bit is clear, VFDIV.VF leaves the corresponding element of vd unchanged.
RULE 3: If the optional mask register vm is not specified for VFDIV.VF, the instruction treats all element positions as active and writes a result to every element of vd.

INSTRUCTION: VFDIV.VV
FORMAT: vfdiv.vv vd, vs2, vs1, vm
RULE 1: VFDIV.VV MUST perform element-wise floating-point division of each element of the source vector register vs2 (dividend) by the corresponding element of the source vector register vs1 (divisor).
RULE 2: VFDIV.VV MUST write the result of each element-wise division into the corresponding element of the destination vector register vd.
RULE 3: VFDIV.VV may be encoded with an optional mask register vm that, when present, applies the standard vector mask semantics to each element of the operation.

INSTRUCTION: VFIRST.M
FORMAT: vfirst.m rd, vs2, vm
RULE 1: VFIRST.M must write the integer value -1 to x[rd] when no active element of vs2 has the value 1.
RULE 2: VFIRST.M must write a value to x[rd] even when the current vector length vl is zero, and the value written in that case must be -1 because no mask elements are active.
RULE 3: When VFIRST.M triggers a trap, the architectural vstart reported with the trap must be zero.
RULE 4: VFIRST.M must raise an illegal-instruction exception if the architectural register vstart is non-zero at the time the instruction is executed.
RULE 5: VFIRST.M is not affected by Zvkt.

INSTRUCTION: VFMACC.VF
FORMAT: vfmacc.vf vd, fs1, vs2, vm
RULE 1: If a mask register vm is supplied to VFMACC.VF, only the elements i for which vm[i] is true are considered active, and elements with vm[i] false leave the corresponding destination element unchanged.
RULE 2: If no mask register vm is supplied to VFMACC.VF, all element positions of the destination vector are treated as active.
RULE 3: For each active element i, VFMACC.VF computes the product of the scalar source value T1 and the vector source element T2[i], adds the existing destination element vd[i], and writes the rounded result back to vd[i] using the current floating-point rounding mode frm.
RULE 4: The rounding performed by VFMACC.VF after the fused multiply-accumulate follows the current frm rounding mode and is applied only once per active element.
RULE 5: VFMACC.VF computes for each element i the product of the scalar floating-point value held in integer register rs1 and the i-th element of source vector register vs2, adds the original i-th element of destination vector register vd, and writes the resulting sum back to vd[i].
RULE 6: VFMACC.VF overwrites each destination element vd[i] with the fused multiply-add result, replacing the previous addend value in vd[i].

INSTRUCTION: VFMACC.VV
FORMAT: vfmacc.vv vd, vs1, vs2, vm
RULE 1: VFMACC.VV performs a fused multiply-accumulate operation on corresponding elements of two source vectors and an accumulator vector.
RULE 2: VFMACC.VV takes a destination/accumulator vector vd, source vectors T1 and T2 that contain widened FP32 values, and a mask vm, and writes the result to vd respecting the mask.
RULE 3: For each element index i where the mask vm bit is true, VFMACC.VV multiplies T1[i] by T2[i] to produce an unrounded product, adds that product to the original value of vd[i], rounds the sum according to the current frm rounding mode, and stores the rounded result in vd[i].
RULE 4: For each element index i where the mask vm bit is false, VFMACC.VV leaves the element vd[i] unchanged.
RULE 5: The rounding of the sum performed by VFMACC.VV follows the rounding mode specified in the frm register.
RULE 6: VFMACC.VV must raise the same floating-point exceptions (Overflow, Underflow, Inexact, Invalid) as defined for vfwmaccbf16.vv when the rounded result or the operation meets the corresponding exception conditions.
RULE 7: VFMACC.VV overwrites each destination element vd[i] with the result of (vs1[i] * vs2[i]) + vd[i] for every element i that is processed.
RULE 8: VFMACC.VV updates a destination element vd[i] only when the corresponding mask bit in vm is true; when the mask bit is false, vd[i] retains its previous value.
RULE 9: VFMACC.VV performs the element-wise operation only for element indices i that are less than the current vector length, leaving elements with indices i ≥ VL unchanged.

INSTRUCTION: VFMADD.VF
FORMAT: vfmadd.vf vd, fs1, vs2, vm
RULE 1: VFMADD.VF performs the multiply and add as a single-width fused operation, meaning the intermediate product and sum are computed without intermediate rounding and the final result is rounded only once to the element's format.
RULE 2: VFMADD.VF overwrites the multiplicand, so the original value of each destination element vd[i] is used as the multiplicand in the fused multiply-add before being replaced by the computed result.

INSTRUCTION: VFMAX.VF
FORMAT: vfmax.vf vd, vs2, fs1, vm
RULE 1: VFMAX.VF vd, vs2, rs1, vm performs the maximumNumber operation between each active element of the source vector register vs2 and the floating-point value held in the scalar register rs1, writing the result to the corresponding element of the destination vector register vd.
RULE 2: VFMAX.VF follows the definition of the maximumNumber operation as defined for the scalar floating-point maximum instructions in version 2.2 of the RISC-V F/D/Q extensions.
RULE 3: VFMAX.VF applies the maximumNumber operation only to those vector elements that are active according to the current vector length setting.
RULE 4: VFMAX.VF uses the optional mask operand vm to control element activity, making an element active only if the corresponding mask bit is set.

INSTRUCTION: VFMAX.VV
FORMAT: vfmax.vv vd, vs2, vs1, vm
RULE 1: VFMAX.VV MUST perform the maximumNumber operation on each active element of the vector.
RULE 2: VFMAX.VV MUST exhibit the same behavior as the scalar floating-point maximum instruction defined in version 2.2 of the RISC-V F/D/Q extension.
RULE 3: For each active element, VFMAX.VV MUST compare the corresponding elements of source vector registers vs2 and vs1 and select the greater value according to the maximumNumber rule.
RULE 4: VFMAX.VV MUST write the selected maximumNumber value for each active element into the corresponding element of destination vector register vd.

INSTRUCTION: VFMERGE.VFM
FORMAT: vfmerge.vfm vd, vs2, fs1, v0
RULE 1: VFMERGE.VFM must perform its operation on every element index i such that i ≥ vstart and i < vl, independent of the value of the mask bit for that element.
RULE 2: VFMERGE.VFM is encoded as a masked instruction with the vm field set to zero, which causes the instruction to ignore mask-controlled element-wise execution and apply its operation to all active elements.
RULE 3: For each element i processed by VFMERGE.VFM, if the mask bit v0.mask[i] is zero, VFMERGE.VFM must copy the corresponding element vs2[i] into the destination element vd[i].
RULE 4: For each element i processed by VFMERGE.VFM, if the mask bit v0.mask[i] is one, VFMERGE.VFM must copy the scalar floating-point register specified by rs1 into the destination element vd[i].
RULE 5: VFMERGE.VFM must have a vm field value that is not 1 and a vs2 field value that is not v0.

INSTRUCTION: VFMIN.VF
FORMAT: vfmin.vf vd, vs2, fs1, vm
RULE 1: For every active element, VFMIN.VF computes the minimumNumber of the element and the scalar value and writes the resulting floating-point value to the corresponding element of the destination vector register group vd.
RULE 2: For every inactive element, VFMIN.VF leaves the corresponding element of the destination vector register group vd unchanged.
RULE 3: VFMIN.VF applies the architectural definition of the scalar floating-point minimumNumber instruction from version 2.2 of the RISC-V F/D/Q extension to each active element-wise computation.

INSTRUCTION: VFMIN.VV
FORMAT: vfmin.vv vd, vs2, vs1, vm
RULE 1: VFMIN.VV MUST compute the floating-point minimum of each pair of corresponding elements from vs2 and vs1 and write the result to the matching element of vd for every element that is active according to the mask vm.
RULE 2: VFMIN.VV MUST not modify any element of vd for which the corresponding mask bit in vm is inactive.
RULE 3: VFMIN.VV MUST perform the minimumNumber operation with the same handling of special cases (such as NaNs, signed zeros, and infinities) as the scalar floating-point minimum instruction defined in version 2.2 of the RISC-V F/D/Q extension.

INSTRUCTION: VFMSAC.VV
FORMAT: vfmsac.vv vd, vs1, vs2, vm
RULE 1: VFMSAC.VV performs a floating-point multiply-subtract-accumulator operation that for each element i computes (vs1[i] * vs2[i]) – vd[i] and writes the result to vd[i].

INSTRUCTION: VFMSUB.VF
FORMAT: vfmsub.vf vd, fs1, vs2, vm
RULE 1: VFMSUB.VF reads the scalar floating-point register f[rs1] and uses its value as the multiplicand for all element operations of the instruction.
RULE 2: For each element index i that is less than the current vector length, VFMSUB.VF computes the destination element vd[i] as (f[rs1] * vd[i]) minus vs2[i].
RULE 3: When the mask bit for element i is false, VFMSUB.VF leaves the destination element vd[i] unchanged.
RULE 4: VFMSUB.VF updates only those destination elements vd[i] for which the mask bit is true and the element index i is within the current vector length; all other elements of vd remain unmodified.
RULE 5: The multiplication and subtraction performed by VFMSUB.VF are executed as a single fused multiply-subtract operation with no intermediate rounding.
RULE 6: The result of the fused operation performed by VFMSUB.VF is rounded according to the currently active floating-point rounding mode.
RULE 7: If the fused operation of VFMSUB.VF yields a NaN for an element, the NaN is propagated to the corresponding destination element in accordance with IEEE 754 rules.

INSTRUCTION: VFMUL.VF
FORMAT: vfmul.vf vd, vs2, fs1, vm
RULE 1: VFMUL.VF must compute the product of each element of source vector register vs2 and the scalar floating-point value in register rs1, writing the results to destination vector register vd.
RULE 2: VFMUL.VF must operate on single-width floating-point elements, requiring that the element width of vd and vs2 matches the width of the scalar in rs1.
RULE 3: VFMUL.VF must respect the optional mask register specified by vm, performing the multiplication only for elements whose mask bit is set and leaving the remaining elements of vd unchanged.

INSTRUCTION: VFMUL.VV
FORMAT: vfmul.vv vd, vs2, vs1, vm
RULE 1: VFMUL.VV is a vector-vector instruction that multiplies each element of the first source vector register vs2 by the corresponding element of the second source vector register vs1 using floating-point arithmetic.
RULE 2: VFMUL.VV writes the result of each elementwise floating-point multiplication to the destination vector register vd for elements for which the corresponding mask bit in the mask register vm is true.
RULE 3: VFMUL.VV leaves unchanged the elements of the destination vector register vd for which the corresponding mask bit in the mask register vm is false.

INSTRUCTION: VFMV.F.S
FORMAT: vfmv.f.s fd, vs2
RULE 1: VFMV.F.S transfers a single SEW-wide element from element 0 of the source vector register to the destination scalar floating-point register, and it ignores LMUL and any vector register grouping.
RULE 2: VFMV.F.S copies the value of element 0 of vector register vs2 into scalar floating-point register f[rd].
RULE 3: VFMV.F.S must perform its operation and write the destination scalar register even when vstart ≥ vl or when vl = 0.
RULE 4: Encodings of VFMV.F.S with the mask disabled (vm = 0) are reserved.

INSTRUCTION: VFMV.S.F
FORMAT: vfmv.s.f vd, fs1
RULE 1: VFMV.S.F copies the value from the specified scalar floating-point register into element 0 of the destination vector register.
RULE 2: VFMV.S.F ignores the LMUL setting and any vector register grouping when performing the copy.
RULE 3: VFMV.S.F performs no write to the destination register when the architectural register vstart is greater than or equal to the current vector length vl.
RULE 4: VFMV.S.F does not update any element of the destination vector register group when the current vector length vl is zero, irrespective of the value of vstart.

INSTRUCTION: VFMV.V.F
FORMAT: vfmv.v.f vd, fs1
RULE 1: VFMV.V.F must copy the floating-point value held in the scalar register identified by rs1 into every element of the destination vector register group specified by vd.
RULE 2: VFMV.V.F is defined as an unmasked operation, therefore the vm field of its encoding must be 1 and all elements of the destination vector register group are treated as active.
RULE 3: VFMV.V.F must have the vs2 field of its encoding set to the register identifier v0; any encoding with a vs2 value other than v0 is reserved.

INSTRUCTION: VFNCVT.F.F.W
FORMAT: vfncvt.f.f.w vd, vs2, vm
RULE 1: VFNCVT.F.F.W converts each double-width floating-point element in the source vector register group vs2 to a single-width floating-point element in the corresponding position of the destination vector register group vd.
RULE 2: VFNCVT.F.F.W writes a converted element to a destination element only when the corresponding mask bit in vm is 1; when the mask bit is 0 the destination element retains its previous value.
RULE 3: For a given vector length, VFNCVT.F.F.W produces one single-width result element for each active element index up to the configured vector length, reading the double-width source element at the same index from vs2.
RULE 4: If VFNCVT.F.F.W is executed with EEW = 16, then the source operand bits are interpreted as a binary16 floating‑point value and the destination register contains the result of converting that binary16 value.

INSTRUCTION: VFNCVT.F.X.W
FORMAT: vfncvt.f.x.w vd, vs2, vm
RULE 1: VFNCVT.F.X.W must convert each double-width signed integer element in the source vector register vs2 to a single-width floating-point element and write the converted element to the corresponding element of the destination vector register vd.
RULE 2: VFNCVT.F.X.W performs a narrowing conversion, so the width of each destination floating-point element is half the width of the source signed-integer element.
RULE 3: VFNCVT.F.X.W must obey the same vector register overlap constraints that apply to other narrowing instructions.
RULE 4: If VFNCVT.F.X.W is executed without an explicitly programmed rounding mode, then the conversion result in the destination floating‑point register is produced using the implementation’s default rounding mode for narrowing conversions.

INSTRUCTION: VFNCVT.F.XU.W
FORMAT: vfncvt.f.xu.w vd, vs2, vm
RULE 1: VFNCVT.F.XU.W converts each double-width unsigned integer element in the source vector register vs2 to a single-width floating-point element and writes the result to the corresponding element of the destination vector register vd.
RULE 2: VFNCVT.F.XU.W treats the elements of vs2 as unsigned integers whose width is twice the width of the floating-point elements produced in vd.
RULE 3: If a mask register vm is provided, VFNCVT.F.XU.W updates only those elements of vd for which the corresponding mask bit in vm is true, and leaves all other elements of vd unchanged.
RULE 4: VFNCVT.F.XU.W MUST obey the same vector register overlap constraints as other narrowing instructions, so the destination register vd may not overlap the source register vs2 in any configuration that is prohibited for narrowing operations.

INSTRUCTION: VFNCVT.ROD.F.F.W
FORMAT: vfncvt.rod.f.f.w vd, vs2, vm
RULE 1: VFNCVT.ROD.F.F.W MUST perform a vector narrowing conversion from a wider floating-point element type to a narrower floating-point element type using the round-towards-odd rounding mode.
RULE 2: VFNCVT.ROD.F.F.W narrows each element from a larger floating-point format (such as FP32) to the BF16 format as a halving step in a sequence of conversions.
RULE 3: VFNCVT.ROD.F.F.W applies the round-towards-odd rounding mode to every element it converts, regardless of any other rounding-mode configuration.
RULE 4: After one or more executions of VFNCVT.ROD.F.F.W, a final conversion from FP32 to BF16 may be performed with a rounding mode different from round-towards-odd, and VFNCVT.ROD.F.F.W does not dictate that final rounding mode.
RULE 5: VFNCVT.ROD.F.F.W must observe the same vector-register overlap constraints as other narrowing instructions.
RULE 6: If a finite source element exceeds the representable range of the single-width destination format, VFNCVT.ROD.F.F.W converts it to the largest finite destination value having the same sign.
RULE 7: If a conversion is performed as a series of halving steps and any step before the final does not use VFNCVT.ROD.F.F.W, then the final destination register value and the floating‑point exception flags (fflags) will not match those produced by a single direct conversion.

INSTRUCTION: VFNCVT.RTZ.X.F.W
FORMAT: vfncvt.rtz.x.f.w vd, vs2, vm
RULE 1: VFNCVT.RTZ.X.F.W converts each double-width floating-point element in source vector register vs2 to a signed integer element of half the width and writes the converted element to the corresponding element of destination vector register vd.
RULE 2: VFNCVT.RTZ.X.F.W performs the conversion using truncating rounding, i.e., round-toward-zero.
RULE 3: VFNCVT.RTZ.X.F.W must obey the vector-register overlap constraints that apply to all narrowing instructions, allowing vd and vs2 to overlap only in ways permitted for narrowing operations.

INSTRUCTION: VFNCVT.RTZ.XU.F.W
FORMAT: vfncvt.rtz.xu.f.w vd, vs2, vm
RULE 1: VFNCVT.RTZ.XU.F.W must convert each double-width floating-point element in the source vector register vs2 to an unsigned integer element of half the width and write the converted element to the corresponding position in the destination vector register vd.
RULE 2: VFNCVT.RTZ.XU.F.W must perform the conversion using truncating rounding, i.e., round-toward-zero.
RULE 3: VFNCVT.RTZ.XU.F.W must obey the same vector register overlap constraints that apply to other narrowing instructions.

INSTRUCTION: VFNCVT.XU.F.W
FORMAT: vfncvt.xu.f.w vd, vs2, vm
RULE 1: VFNCVT.XU.F.W MUST convert each element of the source vector register from double-width floating-point format to an unsigned integer of the destination element width, placing the converted elements into the destination vector register.
RULE 2: VFNCVT.XU.F.W MUST satisfy the same vector register overlap constraints that apply to other narrowing vector instructions.

INSTRUCTION: VFNCVTBF16.F.F.W
FORMAT: vfncvtbf16.f.f.w vd, vs2, vm
RULE 1: VFNCVTBF16.F.F.W must read each element of the source vector as an IEEE-754 binary32 value, convert it to the bfloat16 format, and write the converted value to the corresponding element of the destination vector.
RULE 2: VFNCVTBF16.F.F.W is available only when the Zvfbfmin vector BF16 conversion extension is implemented.
RULE 3: VFNCVTBF16.F.F.W requires the Zve32f vector extension; the instruction is not defined on implementations that do not provide Zve32f.
RULE 4: VFNCVTBF16.F.F.W converts each 32-bit floating-point element in the source vector register vs2 to a 16-bit bfloat16 element in the destination vector register vd, applying the rounding mode specified in the frm register.
RULE 5: VFNCVTBF16.F.F.W must be executed with a selected element width (SEW) of 16 bits; any encoding that specifies an SEW other than 16 is reserved.
RULE 6: VFNCVTBF16.F.F.W may raise the floating-point overflow exception if a converted value exceeds the maximum representable bfloat16 magnitude.
RULE 7: VFNCVTBF16.F.F.W may raise the floating-point underflow exception if a converted value is non-zero but smaller than the minimum positive normal bfloat16 magnitude after rounding.
RULE 8: VFNCVTBF16.F.F.W may raise the floating-point inexact exception when the conversion of a 32-bit floating-point element to bfloat16 does not produce an exact representation.

INSTRUCTION: VFNMACC.VF
FORMAT: vfnmacc.vf vd, fs1, vs2, vm
RULE 1: VFNMACC.VF computes each active element i as vd[i] = -(f[rs1] * vs2[i]) - vd[i].
RULE 2: For each element i, VFNMACC.VF updates vd[i] with the computed result only if the corresponding mask bit vm[i] is true; otherwise vd[i] retains its prior value.
RULE 3: In VFNMACC.VF the destination vector register vd serves both as the subtrahend operand and as the register that receives the result, overwriting its previous contents for active elements.
RULE 4: VFNMACC.VF performs the fused multiply-add operation using the floating-point format (single-precision or double-precision) defined by the element type of the vector registers, and the operation is single-width.
RULE 5: VFNMACC.VF does not introduce any additional permission checks, exception behavior, or rounding-mode specifications beyond those defined by the standard floating-point arithmetic for the given element type.

INSTRUCTION: VFNMADD.VF
FORMAT: vfnmadd.vf vd, fs1, vs2, vm
RULE 1: VFNMADD.VF is a floating-point negate-multiply-add instruction that overwrites the first multiplicand operand.

INSTRUCTION: VFNMADD.VV
FORMAT: vfnmadd.vv vd, vs1, vs2, vm
RULE 1: VFNMADD.VV overwrites the first multiplicand operand, meaning the original contents of vd are replaced by the computed result for each element.
RULE 2: VFNMADD.VV performs the computation element-wise across all elements up to the current vector length.

INSTRUCTION: VFNMSAC.VV
FORMAT: vfnmsac.vv vd, vs1, vs2, vm
RULE 1: VFNMSAC.VV must, for each element i whose mask bit is set, compute -(vs1[i] * vs2[i]) + vd[i] using the element's floating-point format and write the result to vd[i].
RULE 2: VFNMSAC.VV must leave vd[i] unchanged for any element i whose mask bit is not set.
RULE 3: VFNMSAC.VV overwrites the subtrahend operand vd with the computed result for each active element.

INSTRUCTION: VFNMSUB.VF
FORMAT: vfnmsub.vf vd, fs1, vs2, vm
RULE 1: VFNMSUB.VF reads each element i of the destination vector register vd as operand vd[i] before the instruction writes the result.
RULE 2: For every element i for which the corresponding mask bit in vm is 1, VFNMSUB.VF computes the product of f[rs1] and the original vd[i], negates the product, adds vs2[i], and writes the resulting value to element i of vd.
RULE 3: For every element i for which the corresponding mask bit in vm is 0, VFNMSUB.VF leaves element i of vd unchanged.
RULE 4: VFNMSUB.VF performs the arithmetic using the current floating-point rounding mode and updates the floating-point exception flags according to the result of each element's computation.
RULE 5: VFNMSUB.VF updates only the elements of the destination register vd that are written and does not modify any other architectural registers or memory locations.
RULE 6: VFNMSUB.VF operates on all vector elements up to the current vector length as defined by the vector configuration.

INSTRUCTION: VFNMSUB.VV
FORMAT: vfnmsub.vv vd, vs1, vs2, vm
RULE 1: VFNMSUB.VV MUST compute for each element i that is selected by the mask (or for all elements if no mask is provided) the result vd[i] = -(vs1[i] * original_vd[i]) + vs2[i].
RULE 2: VFNMSUB.VV uses the original value of the destination register vd as the first multiplicand in the fused operation before vd is overwritten.
RULE 3: VFNMSUB.VV uses the source register vs1 as the second multiplicand of the fused operation.
RULE 4: VFNMSUB.VV uses the source register vs2 as the addend of the fused operation.
RULE 5: VFNMSUB.VV MUST leave vd[i] unchanged for any element i whose corresponding mask bit is zero when a mask register is supplied.

INSTRUCTION: VFREC7.V
FORMAT: vfrec7.v vd, vs2, vm
RULE 1: VFREC7.V produces an output element whose sign bit is identical to the sign bit of the corresponding input element.
RULE 2: If the input element x to VFREC7.V is negative infinity, VFREC7.V returns negative zero and raises no exception.
RULE 3: If the input element x to VFREC7.V satisfies –2^(B+1) < x ≤ –2^B and x is normal, VFREC7.V returns a subnormal result y such that –2^(B+1) ≥ y > –2^B and raises no exception.
RULE 4: If the input element x to VFREC7.V satisfies –2^B < x ≤ –2^(B-1) and x is normal, VFREC7.V returns a subnormal result y such that –2^B ≥ y > –2^(B-1) and raises no exception.
RULE 5: If the input element x to VFREC7.V satisfies –2^(B-1) < x ≤ –2^(–B+1) and x is normal, VFREC7.V returns a normal result y such that –2^(–B+1) ≥ y > –2^(B-1) and raises no exception.
RULE 6: If the input element x to VFREC7.V satisfies –2^(–B+1) < x ≤ –2^(–B) and x is subnormal with leading-significand bits "1…", VFREC7.V returns a normal result y such that –2^(B-1) ≥ y > –2^B and raises no exception.
RULE 7: If the input element x to VFREC7.V satisfies –2^(–B) < x ≤ –2^(–(B+1)) and x is subnormal with leading-significand bits "01…", VFREC7.V returns a normal result y such that –2^B ≥ y > –2^(B+1) and raises no exception.
RULE 8: If the input element x to VFREC7.V satisfies –2^(–(B+1)) < x < –0.0, the input is subnormal with leading-significand bits "00…", and the current rounding mode is RUP or RTZ, VFREC7.V returns the greatest-magnitude negative finite value, and raises the inexact (NX) and overflow (OF) exceptions.
RULE 9: If the input element x to VFREC7.V satisfies –2^(–(B+1)) < x < –0.0, the input is subnormal with leading-significand bits "00…", and the current rounding mode is RDN, RNE or RMM, VFREC7.V returns negative infinity and raises the inexact (NX) and overflow (OF) exceptions.
RULE 10: If the input element x to VFREC7.V is –0.0, VFREC7.V returns negative infinity and raises the divide-by-zero (DZ) exception.
RULE 11: If the input element x to VFREC7.V is +0.0, VFREC7.V returns positive infinity and raises the divide-by-zero (DZ) exception.
RULE 12: If the input element x to VFREC7.V satisfies +0.0 < x < 2^(–(B+1)), the input is subnormal with leading-significand bits "00…", and the current rounding mode is RUP, RNE or RMM, VFREC7.V returns positive infinity and raises the inexact (NX) and overflow (OF) exceptions.
RULE 13: If the input element x to VFREC7.V satisfies +0.0 < x < 2^(–(B+1)), the input is subnormal with leading-significand bits "00…", and the current rounding mode is RDN or RTZ, VFREC7.V returns the greatest finite positive value and raises the inexact (NX) and overflow (OF) exceptions.
RULE 14: If the input element x to VFREC7.V satisfies 2^(–(B+1)) ≤ x < 2^(–B) and the input is subnormal with leading-significand bits "01…", VFREC7.V returns a normal result y such that 2^(B+1) > y ≥ 2^B and raises no exception.
