RULE 15: If the input element x to VFREC7.V satisfies 2^(–B) ≤ x < 2^(–B+1) and the input is subnormal with leading-significand bits "1…", VFREC7.V returns a normal result y such that 2^B > y ≥ 2^(B-1) and raises no exception.
RULE 16: If the input element x to VFREC7.V satisfies 2^(B-1) ≤ x < 2^B and x is normal, VFREC7.V returns a subnormal result y such that 2^(–B+1) > y ≥ 2^(–B) (subnormal with leading-significand bits "1…") and raises no exception.
RULE 17: If the input element x to VFREC7.V satisfies 2^B ≤ x < 2^(B+1) and x is normal, VFREC7.V returns a subnormal result y such that 2^(–B) > y ≥ 2^(–(B+1)) (subnormal with leading-significand bits "01…") and raises no exception.
RULE 18: If the input element x to VFREC7.V is positive infinity, VFREC7.V returns positive zero and raises no exception.
RULE 19: If the input element x to VFREC7.V is a quiet NaN, VFREC7.V returns the canonical NaN representation and raises no exception.
RULE 20: If the input element x to VFREC7.V is a signalling NaN, VFREC7.V returns the canonical NaN representation and raises the invalid operation (NV) exception.
RULE 21: Subnormal inputs whose magnitude is at least 2^(–(B+1)) cause VFREC7.V to produce normal outputs, whereas other subnormal inputs cause VFREC7.V to produce infinite outputs.
RULE 22: Normal inputs whose magnitude is at least 2^(B-1) cause VFREC7.V to produce subnormal outputs, whereas other normal inputs cause VFREC7.V to produce normal outputs.
RULE 23: When VFREC7.V raises an overflow exception, the exact output value is selected according to the dynamic rounding mode that is in effect at the time of the exception.

INSTRUCTION: VFREDMAX.VS
FORMAT: vfredmax.vs vd, vs2, vs1, vm
RULE 1: VFREDMAX.VS performs a reduction that yields the maximum value of the elements specified by the source vectors.

INSTRUCTION: VFREDMIN.VS
FORMAT: vfredmin.vs vd, vs2, vs1, vm
RULE 1: VFREDMIN.VS MUST compute the minimum of all active elements of source vector register group vs2, using the scalar value held in the first element of source vector register vs1 as the initial accumulator value.
RULE 2: VFREDMIN.VS MUST write the final minimum value to the first element of destination vector register vd and MUST leave all other elements of vd unchanged.
RULE 3: VFREDMIN.VS MUST consider only elements with indices less than the current vector length (VL) when performing the reduction.
RULE 4: VFREDMIN.VS MUST include an element of vs2 in the reduction only if the corresponding mask bit in vm is set to one; elements with a mask bit of zero are excluded.
RULE 5: VFREDMIN.VS MUST perform the comparison and produce the result using the element data type of vs2 (and vs1).
RULE 6: VFREDMIN.VS MUST raise an illegal-instruction exception if the element type is not a supported floating-point type for the minimum operation.

INSTRUCTION: VFREDOSUM.VS
FORMAT: vfredosum.vs vd, vs2, vs1, vm
RULE 1: VFREDOSUM.VS must compute an ordered sum reduction of the elements contained in the source vector groups.
RULE 2: VFREDOSUM.VS writes the result of the ordered sum reduction to the destination register operand vd.

INSTRUCTION: VFRSQRT7.V
FORMAT: vfrsqrt7.v vd, vs2, vm
RULE 1: When the source element of VFRSQRT7.V is a finite negative value (-∞ ≤ x < -0.0), VFRSQRT7.V produces a canonical NaN and raises the invalid operation (NV) exception.
RULE 2: When the source element of VFRSQRT7.V is –0.0, VFRSQRT7.V produces –∞ and raises the divide-by-zero (DZ) exception.
RULE 3: When the source element of VFRSQRT7.V is +0.0, VFRSQRT7.V produces +∞ and raises the divide-by-zero (DZ) exception.
RULE 4: When the source element of VFRSQRT7.V is a positive finite value (+0.0 < x < +∞), VFRSQRT7.V produces an estimate of 1 / √x and raises no floating-point exception.
RULE 5: When the source element of VFRSQRT7.V is +∞, VFRSQRT7.V produces +0.0 and raises no floating-point exception.
RULE 6: When the source element of VFRSQRT7.V is a quiet NaN (qNaN), VFRSQRT7.V produces a canonical NaN and raises no floating-point exception.
RULE 7: When the source element of VFRSQRT7.V is a signaling NaN (sNaN), VFRSQRT7.V produces a canonical NaN and raises the invalid operation (NV) exception.
RULE 8: For all positive normal and subnormal source elements, VFRSQRT7.V produces a normal (non-subnormal, non-special) result.
RULE 9: The result produced by VFRSQRT7.V is independent of the current dynamic rounding mode.
RULE 10: VFRSQRT7.V places the seven-bit table output as the seven most-significant bits of the result significand (after the leading one) and sets all remaining lower bits of the result significand to zero.
RULE 11: VFRSQRT7.V computes the result exponent as floor((3 * B - 1 - normalized exponent) / 2), where B is the exponent bias of the element width.
RULE 12: VFRSQRT7.V copies the sign of the source element to the result element.
RULE 13: When VFRSQRT7.V is executed with SEW = 32, the element value 0x00718ABC yields the result 0x5F080000, and the element value 0x7F765432 yields the result 0x1F820000.

INSTRUCTION: VFSGNJ.VF
FORMAT: vfsgnj.vf vd, vs2, fs1, vm
RULE 1: For each element i that is active (the mask vm is absent or its mask bit is set), VFSGNJ.VF must write to vd[i] a value whose sign bit is the sign bit of the scalar floating-point register rs1 and whose remaining bits are copied from the corresponding bits of vs2[i].
RULE 2: For each element i that is inactive because the mask vm is present and its mask bit is clear, VFSGNJ.VF must leave vd[i] unchanged.
RULE 3: If the optional mask register vm is omitted, VFSGNJ.VF must treat all vector elements as active and update every element of the destination vector vd.
RULE 4: VFSGNJ.VF must not raise any architecturally defined exceptions or cause side-effects beyond the normal vector-scalar register reads and the destination write.

INSTRUCTION: VFSGNJN.VF
FORMAT: vfsgnjn.vf vd, vs2, fs1, vm
RULE 1: VFSGNJN.VF must compute each active element's result by copying all bits except the sign bit from the corresponding element of source vector register vs2.
RULE 2: VFSGNJN.VF must set the sign bit of each active element of the result to the logical negation of the sign bit of scalar floating-point register rs1.
RULE 3: VFSGNJN.VF must write the computed result into destination vector register vd for every element whose mask bit in vm is true.
RULE 4: VFSGNJN.VF must leave unchanged the elements of destination vector register vd for which the corresponding mask bit in vm is false.

INSTRUCTION: VFSGNJN.VV
FORMAT: vfsgnjn.vv vd, vs2, vs1, vm
RULE 1: VFSGNJN.VV must set the sign bit of each destination element *i* to the logical complement of the sign bit of element *i* of source vector **vs1**.
RULE 2: VFSGNJN.VV must produce element-wise negation of a vector when the two source vectors **vs1** and **vs2** are the same, because the magnitude bits are copied from **vs2** and the sign bit is complemented from **vs1**.

INSTRUCTION: VFSGNJX.VF
FORMAT: vfsgnjx.vf vd, vs2, fs1, vm
RULE 1: VFSGNJX.VF must produce each active element of the destination vector by copying the sign bit from the scalar floating-point register rs1 and copying all remaining bits from the corresponding element of the source vector register vs2.
RULE 2: VFSGNJX.VF must leave any destination element unchanged for which the corresponding mask bit in vm is clear.
RULE 3: VFSGNJX.VF must apply the sign-injection operation using the element width (SEW) currently configured for the vector unit, where the sign bit is the most-significant bit of that element format.

INSTRUCTION: VFSGNJX.VV
FORMAT: vfsgnjx.vv vd, vs2, vs1, vm
RULE 1: VFSGNJX.VV produces each element of the destination vector by copying all bits except the sign bit from the corresponding element of the vs2 source operand.
RULE 2: VFSGNJX.VV yields the absolute value of a vector when the two source operands are the same register, because the resulting elements contain the magnitude of the source elements with a positive sign.

INSTRUCTION: VFSLIDE1DOWN.VF
FORMAT: vfslide1down.vf vd, vs2, fs1, vm
RULE 1: VFSLIDE1DOWN.VF copies the element at index i+1 of the source vector register group vs2 into the destination vector register vd at index i for every index i satisfying vstart ≤ i < vl-1, provided that the mask element v0.mask[i] is enabled.
RULE 2: VFSLIDE1DOWN.VF leaves the destination element vd[i] unchanged for every index i where i < vstart.
RULE 3: VFSLIDE1DOWN.VF writes the floating-point register f[rs1] into the destination element vd[vl-1] when the mask element v0.mask[vl-1] is enabled.
RULE 4: If a mask element v0.mask[i] is disabled, then executing VFSLIDE1DOWN.VF does not raise an exception and the architectural result for the corresponding destination element vd[i] is not constrained—it may remain unchanged (undisturbed) or take any value (mask‑agnostic).
RULE 5: VFSLIDE1DOWN.VF applies the tail policy to every destination element vd[i] where i ≥ vl and i < VLMAX.
RULE 6: VFSLIDE1DOWN.VF does not modify any destination elements beyond the architectural limit VLMAX.
RULE 7: VFSLIDE1DOWN.VF places the scalar argument taken from floating-point register f[rs1] at location vl-1 in the destination vector register only if that element is active; otherwise the update follows the mask-agnostic or undisturbed policy.
RULE 8: VFSLIDE1DOWN.VF exhibits element-wise behavior identical to the integer-register version of VFSLIDE1DOWN, applied to the floating-point scalar argument.

INSTRUCTION: VFSQRT.V
FORMAT: vfsqrt.v vd, vs2, vm
RULE 1: For each element position where the mask vm is true (or when no mask is applied), VFSQRT.V computes the square root of the corresponding element in vs2 and stores the result in the same element position of vd.
RULE 2: For each element position where the mask vm is false (or masked off), VFSQRT.V leaves the corresponding element of vd unchanged.

INSTRUCTION: VFSUB.VF
FORMAT: vfsub.vf vd, vs2, fs1, vm
RULE 1: VFSUB.VF computes for each element i of the destination vector register vd the value vs2[i] minus the floating-point value in register f[rs1] and writes the result to vd[i].

INSTRUCTION: VFWADD.VF
FORMAT: vfwadd.vf vd, vs2, fs1, vm
RULE 1: VFWADD.VF MUST read each SEW-wide element from source vector register vs2 and add it to the scalar value from rs1 using floating-point addition.
RULE 2: VFWADD.VF MUST write the result of each element-wise addition into the corresponding element of destination vector register vd.
RULE 3: VFWADD.VF MUST produce each result element with a width of 2 × SEW bits, implementing a widening floating-point addition.
RULE 4: VFWADD.VF MUST apply the element-wise addition only to elements for which the mask vm is true, leaving masked-off elements unchanged.

INSTRUCTION: VFWADD.WF
FORMAT: vfwadd.wf vd, vs2, fs1, vm
RULE 1: VFWADD.WF must be a vector widening floating-point add instruction whose result element width is twice the standard element width (2 × SEW).
RULE 2: VFWADD.WF must compute each 2 × SEW result element as the sum of a SEW-wide element from vs2 and the SEW-wide scalar in rs1.
RULE 3: VFWADD.WF must write its results to a destination vector register group vd that holds elements of width 2 × SEW.
RULE 4: VFWADD.WF must read the scalar floating-point register rs1 as a SEW-wide operand that is added to every element of vs2.
RULE 5: VFWADD.WF may be executed with an optional vector mask vm; for each element index where the mask bit is 1, VFWADD.WF must perform the addition and write the 2 × SEW result to vd, and for mask bits equal to 0 VFWADD.WF must leave the corresponding element of vd unchanged.
RULE 6: VFWADD.WF must perform the operation element-wise, producing vd[i] = (vs2[i] + rs1) widened to 2 × SEW precision for each active element i.

INSTRUCTION: VFWADD.WV
FORMAT: vfwadd.wv vd, vs2, vs1, vm
RULE 1: VFWADD.WV must add each pair of corresponding SEW-wide floating-point elements from source vectors vs2 and vs1 and must write the sum as a 2 × SEW-wide element to the matching element position of destination vector vd.
RULE 2: VFWADD.WV must produce a destination vector vd whose element width is exactly twice the element width of the source vectors vs2 and vs1 (2 × SEW).
RULE 3: VFWADD.WV must operate as a vector-vector instruction, processing all active elements up to the current vector length.

INSTRUCTION: VFWCVT.F.F.V
FORMAT: vfwcvt.f.f.v vd, vs2, vm
RULE 1: VFWCVT.F.F.V must obey the same vector register overlap constraints as other widening vector instructions.
RULE 2: VFWCVT.F.F.V converts each element of the source single-width IEEE floating-point vector to a double-width IEEE floating-point element that exactly represents the original value.
RULE 3: If VFWCVT.F.F.V does not produce element values identical to those obtained by executing the equivalent one‑or‑more doubling steps, or if it raises any floating‑point exception flag that the doubling steps would not raise, then the destination vector register contains a value different from the expected result or the fflags CSR contains additional exception bits.
RULE 4: When VFWCVT.F.F.V executes, each source element of effective element width (EEW) 16 is interpreted as a binary16 floating-point value.
RULE 5: VFWCVT.F.F.V converts each binary16 source element to a binary32 destination element.

INSTRUCTION: VFWCVT.F.X.V
FORMAT: vfwcvt.f.x.v vd, vs2, vm
RULE 1: VFWCVT.F.X.V must convert each signed integer element of the source vector register vs2 into a double-width IEEE floating-point element placed in the corresponding element position of the destination vector register vd.
RULE 2: If the destination register vd overlaps the source register vs2 in a way that violates the widening vector register overlap constraints, then executing VFWCVT.F.X.V raises an illegal‑instruction exception (mcause set to the illegal‑instruction code).
RULE 3: VFWCVT.F.X.V must produce a double-width floating-point result that exactly represents the original single-width signed integer value for every element, because a double-width IEEE floating-point format can always represent a single-width integer exactly.

INSTRUCTION: VFWCVT.F.XU.V
FORMAT: vfwcvt.f.xu.v vd, vs2, vm
RULE 1: VFWCVT.F.XU.V converts each unsigned integer element in the source vector register vs2 to a double-width IEEE floating-point element and writes the result to the corresponding element of the destination vector register vd, applying the optional vector mask vm.
RULE 2: If the source and destination vector register groups used by VFWCVT.F.XU.V overlap in any way that violates the standard widening‑instruction register overlap constraints, then the instruction raises an illegal‑instruction exception.
RULE 3: VFWCVT.F.XU.V must produce a double-width IEEE floating-point result that exactly represents the unsigned integer input value for every element.
RULE 4: VFWCVT.F.XU.V must not raise any additional floating-point exception flags.

INSTRUCTION: VFWCVT.RTZ.X.F.V
FORMAT: vfwcvt.rtz.x.f.v vd, vs2, vm
RULE 1: VFWCVT.RTZ.X.F.V converts each element of the source vector register vs2 from a single-width floating-point value to a double-width signed integer value in the destination vector register vd by applying round-toward-zero truncation to the fractional part.
RULE 2: VFWCVT.RTZ.X.F.V uses the round-toward-zero (RTZ) rounding mode, which discards the fractional portion of each floating-point source element during conversion.
RULE 3: VFWCVT.RTZ.X.F.V must obey the same vector-register overlap constraints that apply to other widening vector instructions.

INSTRUCTION: VFWCVT.RTZ.XU.F.V
FORMAT: vfwcvt.rtz.xu.f.v vd, vs2, vm
RULE 1: VFWCVT.RTZ.XU.F.V MUST convert each floating-point element in source vector register vs2 to an unsigned integer element of double the width, rounding toward zero (truncation), and write the result to the corresponding element in destination vector register vd.
RULE 2: VFWCVT.RTZ.XU.F.V performs a widening conversion such that the destination element type is exactly twice the bit-width of the source floating-point element type.

INSTRUCTION: VFWCVT.X.F.V
FORMAT: vfwcvt.x.f.v vd, vs2, vm
RULE 1: VFWCVT.X.F.V MUST convert each element of the source vector register vs2 from a floating-point format to a signed integer of double the element width and store the converted elements in the corresponding positions of the destination vector register vd.
RULE 2: If VFWCVT.X.F.V is executed with source and destination vector registers overlapping in a way prohibited for widening vector instructions, then it raises an illegal‑instruction exception (mcause = 2).
RULE 3: VFWCVT.X.F.V may be implemented as a sequence of one or more widening steps that double the element width at each step, and such an implementation must produce results equivalent to a single-step conversion and must not raise any additional exception flags beyond those defined for the conversion.

INSTRUCTION: VFWCVT.XU.F.V
FORMAT: vfwcvt.xu.f.v vd, vs2, vm
RULE 1: VFWCVT.XU.F.V converts each element in the source vector register vs2 from a floating-point format to an unsigned integer whose element width is double the width of the source element and writes the converted elements into the destination vector register vd.
RULE 2: VFWCVT.XU.F.V must obey the same vector register overlap constraints as other widening vector instructions, prohibiting any overlapping of destination and source registers that is disallowed for widening operations.

INSTRUCTION: VFWCVTBF16.F.F.V
FORMAT: vfwcvtbf16.f.f.v vd, vs2, vm
RULE 1: VFWCVTBF16.F.F.V converts each BF16 element in the source vector to a corresponding FP32 element in the destination vector.
RULE 2: VFWCVTBF16.F.F.V produces a destination vector whose elements are 32-bit floating-point values that represent the same numeric value as the original BF16 elements.
RULE 3: VFWCVTBF16.F.F.V is a reserved encoding when the selected element width (SEW) is any value other than 16.
RULE 4: VFWCVTBF16.F.F.V must perform an exact widening conversion from a BF16 source element to an FP32 result element.
RULE 5: VFWCVTBF16.F.F.V, if the BF16 input element is normal or infinity, must produce an FP32 result whose bit pattern equals the BF16 bits shifted left by 16 positions with the lower 16 bits set to zero.
RULE 6: VFWCVTBF16.F.F.V performs a widening conversion of each BF16 element in the source vector to a corresponding FP32 element in the destination vector.

INSTRUCTION: VFWMACC.VF
FORMAT: vfwmacc.vf vd, fs1, vs2, vm
RULE 1: VFWMACC.VF computes for each element index i selected by the mask vm the destination element as vd[i] = (f[rs1] * vs2[i]) + vd[i].
RULE 2: VFWMACC.VF treats the multiplier inputs f[rs1] and vs2[i] as SEW-wide values while the addend vd[i] and the destination element are 2 × SEW-wide values.
RULE 3: VFWMACC.VF overwrites the 2 × SEW-wide addend with the result of the fused multiply-add operation.

INSTRUCTION: VFWMACC.VV
FORMAT: vfwmacc.vv vd, vs1, vs2, vm
RULE 1: If VFWMACC.VV executes, then each element i of the destination vector register contains the SEW‑wide floating‑point product of the source elements vs1[i] and vs2[i].
RULE 2: VFWMACC.VV adds the product computed for element i to the current 2×SEW-wide floating-point value stored in destination register vd[i] and writes the sum back to vd[i].
RULE 3: VFWMACC.VV overwrites the original wide addend in vd with the result of the fused multiply-add for each element.
RULE 4: VFWMACC.VV uses SEW-wide inputs for the multiplier operands and a 2×SEW-wide destination and addend width.

INSTRUCTION: VFWMACCBF16.VF
FORMAT: vfwmaccbf16.vf vd, fs1, vs2, vm
RULE 1: If the selected element width (SEW) for VFWMACCBF16.VF is any value other than 16, the encoding is reserved.
RULE 2: VFWMACCBF16.VF multiplies the scalar BF16 value from rs1 with each BF16 element from vs2 to produce an unrounded product for each element.
RULE 3: VFWMACCBF16.VF adds each unrounded product to the corresponding 32-bit floating-point element of the destination vector register vd.
RULE 4: VFWMACCBF16.VF rounds each sum according to the rounding mode specified in the frm register before writing the result to the corresponding element of vd.
RULE 5: VFWMACCBF16.VF updates only those elements for which the vector mask vm is true; elements with vm false remain unchanged in vd.
RULE 6: VFWMACCBF16.VF has the same architectural effect as executing the sequence: fcvt.s.bf16 T1, rs1; vfwcvtbf16.f.f.v T2, vs2, vm; vfmacc.vf vd, T1, T2, vm.

INSTRUCTION: VFWMACCBF16.VV
FORMAT: vfwmaccbf16.vv vd, vs1, vs2, vm
RULE 1: VFWMACCBF16.VV encoding is reserved when the selected SEW is any value other than 16.
RULE 2: VFWMACCBF16.VV performs a widening fused multiply-accumulate where each pair of BF16 elements from the source registers is multiplied to produce an unrounded product that is added to the corresponding FP32 accumulator element from the destination register.
RULE 3: VFWMACCBF16.VV rounds the result of the unrounded product plus accumulator according to the current rounding mode specified in the frm register.
RULE 4: In the vector-vector form VFWMACCBF16.VV reads BF16 elements from source registers vs1 and vs2, reads the FP32 accumulator element from destination register vd, and writes the rounded FP32 result back to the same destination register vd.
RULE 5: VFWMACCBF16.VV is architecturally equivalent to converting BF16 elements in vs1 to FP32 with vfwcvtbf16.f.f.v, converting BF16 elements in vs2 to FP32 with vfwcvtbf16.f.f.v, and then performing a vector fused multiply-add of the two FP32 vectors into the accumulator vector vd with vfmacc.vv, all under the same mask vm.

INSTRUCTION: VFWMSAC.VF
FORMAT: vfwmsac.vf vd, fs1, vs2, vm
RULE 1: VFWMSAC.VF computes for each element i the expression (f[rs1] * vs2[i]) − vd[i] using SEW-wide multiplication and 2 × SEW-wide subtraction, producing a 2 × SEW-wide result.
RULE 2: VFWMSAC.VF writes the 2 × SEW-wide result back to element i of destination vector register vd, overwriting the original value.

INSTRUCTION: VFWMSAC.VV
FORMAT: vfwmsac.vv vd, vs1, vs2, vm
RULE 1: VFWMSAC.VV is a widening floating-point fused multiply-add instruction that overwrites the wide addend with the computed result.
RULE 2: VFWMSAC.VV treats each addend element vd[i] and each destination element as 2 × SEW-wide floating-point values.
RULE 3: VFWMSAC.VV computes for every element i the value (vs1[i] * vs2[i]) − vd[i] and stores the 2 × SEW result into vd[i], replacing the previous content.

INSTRUCTION: VFWMUL.VF
FORMAT: vfwmul.vf vd, vs2, fs1, vm
RULE 1: VFWMUL.VF must multiply each element of the source vector register vs2 by the scalar floating-point value held in the scalar floating-point register rs1 and must write the resulting widened product into the corresponding element of the destination vector register vd.
RULE 2: VFWMUL.VF must produce each product in a floating-point format that is twice the precision of the elements of vs2, thereby widening the result before it is stored in vd.
RULE 3: If the optional mask register vm is supplied, VFWMUL.VF must perform the multiplication only for those element positions whose mask bit is set, and must leave the destination element unchanged for positions whose mask bit is clear.
RULE 4: VFWMUL.VF must read its scalar operand from a floating-point register rs1 and must read its vector multiplicand elements from the vector register vs2, while the destination vector register vd receives the results.

INSTRUCTION: VFWMUL.VV
FORMAT: vfwmul.vv vd, vs2, vs1, vm
RULE 1: If a VFWMUL.VV instruction executes, then each destination vector element has double the source element width and contains the correctly rounded product of the corresponding source elements.

INSTRUCTION: VFWNMSAC.VF
FORMAT: vfwnmsac.vf vd, fs1, vs2, vm
RULE 1: VFWNMSAC.VF computes for every element index i the value -(f[rs1] * vs2[i]) + vd[i] and stores that value into the corresponding element of vd.
RULE 2: VFWNMSAC.VF overwrites the wide addend element in vd with the result of the fused multiply-negate-add operation, so the previous addend value is not retained.
RULE 3: VFWNMSAC.VF uses SEW-wide floating-point values for the scalar register rs1 and the vector source vs2, while each element of the destination vector vd is 2 × SEW bits wide.

INSTRUCTION: VFWNMSAC.VV
FORMAT: vfwnmsac.vv vd, vs1, vs2, vm
RULE 1: VFWNMSAC.VV treats the elements of vs1 and vs2 as SEW-wide floating-point values for the multiplication operand.
RULE 2: VFWNMSAC.VV overwrites the wide addend in vd with the result of the fused operation for each element that is active.
RULE 3: For each active element i, VFWNMSAC.VV computes the fused operation vd[i] = -(vs1[i] * vs2[i]) + vd[i] using floating-point arithmetic.
RULE 4: For each element i for which the mask vm is false, VFWNMSAC.VV leaves the corresponding element of vd unchanged.

INSTRUCTION: VFWREDOSUM.VS
FORMAT: vfwredosum.vs vd, vs2, vs1, vm
RULE 1: VFWREDOSUM.VS produces a reduction result whose element width is twice the source element width (2*SEW) and writes that double-width result to the destination vector register.
RULE 2: VFWREDOSUM.VS promotes each SEW-width element in the source vector vs2 to 2*SEW bits before adding it to a 2*SEW-bit accumulator, performing the reduction in the same manner as the single-width reduction case.
RULE 3: VFWREDOSUM.VS ignores any element of vs2 that is masked off by the vector mask vm, handling inactive elements in the same way as vfredosum.vs.
RULE 4: VFWREDOSUM.VS propagates NaN payloads in the same manner as vfredosum.vs, so that if any contributing element is a NaN the final reduction result is a NaN with the appropriate payload.

INSTRUCTION: VFWSUB.VF
FORMAT: vfwsub.vf vd, vs2, fs1, vm
RULE 1: VFWSUB.VF computes each element of the destination vector register vd as the floating-point subtraction of the corresponding SEW-width element from source vector register vs2 and the SEW-width scalar value from source floating-point register rs1, producing a result element of width 2 × SEW.
RULE 2: VFWSUB.VF requires that the destination vector register vd be configured for element width 2 × SEW while the source vector register vs2 and the source floating-point register rs1 are configured for element width SEW.
RULE 3: If a mask register vm is specified with VFWSUB.VF, the instruction updates only those elements of vd whose corresponding mask bit is true; elements whose mask bit is false retain their prior values.
RULE 4: If no mask register is supplied, VFWSUB.VF treats the operation as if all mask bits are true and updates every element of vd.
RULE 5: If VFWSUB.VF is encoded with rs1 not referencing a scalar floating‑point register, or with vs2 or vd not referencing vector register groups, then executing the instruction raises an illegal‑instruction exception.

INSTRUCTION: VFWSUB.VV
FORMAT: vfwsub.vv vd, vs2, vs1, vm
RULE 1: VFWSUB.VV computes each destination element as the floating-point subtraction of the corresponding element of source vector register vs1 from the corresponding element of source vector register vs2, producing a result element whose width is twice the original SEW (2 × SEW).
RULE 2: VFWSUB.VV stores each computed widened result element into the corresponding element of destination vector register vd.
RULE 3: VFWSUB.VV may be executed with a mask register vm; for any element whose mask bit is clear, VFWSUB.VV leaves the corresponding element of vd unchanged.

INSTRUCTION: VFWSUB.WV
FORMAT: vfwsub.wv vd, vs2, vs1, vm
RULE 1: VFWSUB.WV performs an element-wise subtraction of each element in source vector register vs1 from the corresponding element in source vector register vs2, producing a result element whose width is twice the standard element width (2 × SEW).
RULE 2: VFWSUB.WV stores each widened-precision subtraction result into the corresponding element of destination vector register vd.
RULE 3: VFWSUB.WV uses an optional vector mask register vm to control element-wise activation, and for any element whose mask bit is clear the destination element in vd is not written and retains its previous value.
RULE 4: If the vector mask operand vm is omitted in the VFWSUB.WV instruction, all vector elements are treated as active and the subtraction is performed on every element.

INSTRUCTION: VGHSH.VV
FORMAT: vghsh.vv vd, vs2, vs1
RULE 1: For VGHSH.VV any encoding with SEW other than 32 is reserved.
RULE 2: VGHSH.VV must raise an illegal-instruction exception and cause RETIRE_FAIL when LMUL × VLEN is less than EGW (128).
RULE 3: VGHSH.VV treats each input byte of the source registers as having its bits reversed before any arithmetic is performed.
RULE 4: VGHSH.VV bit-reverses each output byte after the polynomial multiplication to produce the final result in standard GCM ordering.
RULE 5: If VGHSH.VV is executed, then the destination register contains ((Yi XOR Xi) × H) reduced modulo the irreducible polynomial x¹²⁸ + x⁷ + x² + x + 1.
RULE 6: VGHSH.VV performs the GF(2¹²⁸) multiplication as a carry-less multiply of two 128-bit polynomials followed by reduction modulo the GHASH irreducible polynomial.
RULE 7: If the most‑significant bit of the shifted H operand is 1 during a VGHSH.VV multiplication loop, then after that iteration the intermediate‑product register contains the previous intermediate product XOR 0x87.
RULE 8: VGHSH.VV processes vector elements from index floor(vstart / EGS) up to floor(vl / EGS) − 1, inclusive, where each element has an effective element width of 128 bits.
RULE 9: If VGHSH.VV does not apply a byte‑wise bit reversal to the second source register element before using it, then the destination register element written after the instruction will differ from the value computed using the correctly reversed operand.
RULE 10: VGHSH.VV writes the bit-reversed accumulator Z back to the destination register element as the next partial hash Yi+1.
RULE 11: If VGHSH.VV is executed, then the effective element width is 128 bits, the element group size is 4, and each vector register uses a selected element width of 32 bits.
RULE 12: VGHSH.VV is defined as the operation of VGMUL.VV with the first source operand (vs1) hard-wired to zero.
RULE 13: All architectural behavior specified for VGMUL.VV applies to VGHSH.VV when the first source operand is hard-wired to zero.

INSTRUCTION: VGMUL.VV
FORMAT: vgmul.vv vd, vs2
RULE 1: If VGMUL.VV is executed with an element group size that is not 128 bits (i.e., not four 32‑bit elements), then it raises an illegal‑instruction exception (mcause set to the illegal‑instruction code).
RULE 2: If VGMUL.VV is executed while the vstart CSR value is not a multiple of four, then the instruction raises an illegal‑instruction exception (mcause = 2) and no architectural state is modified.
RULE 3: VGMUL.VV treats each 128-bit element of its source operands as a polynomial over GF(2) and produces a 128-bit polynomial result equal to the carry-less product of the two operands reduced modulo the GHASH irreducible polynomial x^128 + x^7 + x^2 + x + 1.
RULE 4: Before the carry-less multiplication, VGMUL.VV reverses the order of bits within each byte of the multiplier element read from Vd and the multiplicand element read from Vs2.
RULE 5: After the multiplication and reduction, VGMUL.VV reverses the order of bits within each byte of the 128-bit product before writing the result back to Vd.
RULE 6: VGMUL.VV raises an illegal-instruction exception and retires with failure if LMUL × VLEN is less than 128 bits.
RULE 7: For each 128-bit element index i from floor(VSTART/EGS) to floor(VL/EGS) − 1, VGMUL.VV reads the i-th element of Vd, applies byte-wise bit reversal, treats the result as the multiplier Y, reads the i-th element of Vs2, applies byte-wise bit reversal, treats the result as the multiplicand H, computes the carry-less product Z, applies byte-wise bit reversal to Z, and writes the final 128-bit value to the i-th element of Vd.
RULE 8: VGMUL.VV does not modify any vector register other than the destination register Vd.
RULE 9: VGMUL.VV is functionally identical to the vghsh.vv instruction when the first source operand of vghsh.vv (vs1) is set to zero.

INSTRUCTION: VID.V
FORMAT: vid.v vd, vm
RULE 1: VID.V writes the index of each element, starting at 0 and incrementing by one for each subsequent element, into the corresponding element of the destination vector register group for element positions 0 through VL-1.
RULE 2: VID.V may be executed with a mask and the mask does not alter the index value that is written to any element that is active under the mask.
RULE 3: VID.V must have the VS2 field set to register v0, and any encoding with VS2 not equal to v0 is reserved.
RULE 4: When the index value produced by VID.V would exceed the width of the destination element's SEW, the result is truncated to retain only the least-significant SEW bits.

INSTRUCTION: VIOTA.M
FORMAT: viota.m vd, vs2, vm
RULE 1: VIOTA.M reads a source vector mask register and writes to each element of the destination vector register group the count of mask bits in source elements whose indices are less than that element's index.
RULE 2: If VIOTA.M is masked, only those source mask elements whose corresponding mask bit is enabled are counted in the prefix sum.
RULE 3: If the destination SEW is larger than the number of bits needed to represent the sum, VIOTA.M zero-extends the sum to fill the destination element.
RULE 4: If the sum produced by VIOTA.M exceeds the destination SEW, VIOTA.M retains only the least-significant SEW bits of the sum.
RULE 5: When a trap occurs on VIOTA.M, the trap reports vstart=0 and execution restarts from the first element upon resumption.
RULE 6: If VIOTA.M is executed with vstart nonzero, an illegal-instruction exception MUST be raised.
RULE 7: VIOTA.M is not affected by the Zvkt extension.

INSTRUCTION: VL1RE8.V
FORMAT: vl1re8.v vd, (rs1)
RULE 1: VL1RE8.V v3,(a0) loads VLEN/8 bytes from the memory address held in the scalar register a0 into the destination vector register group v3.
RULE 2: The nf field of VL1RE8.V must encode a power-of-2 number of registers and the destination register numbers must be aligned to a vector register group, otherwise the encoding is reserved.
RULE 3: VL1RE8.V must be encoded with NFIELDS equal to 1, and any VL1RE8.V encoding that specifies an NFIELDS value other than 1 results in a reserved encoding.
RULE 4: VL1RE8.V is reserved when vstart ≥ evl (i.e., when vstart ≥ VLEN/8).
RULE 5: VL1RE8.V stores the loaded bytes into the destination register group in the same order as they appear in memory, without any byte-layout transformation.

INSTRUCTION: VL1RE16.V
FORMAT: vl1re16.v vd, (rs1)
RULE 1: VL1RE16.V loads a whole vector register group consisting of a single vector register from memory.
RULE 2: VL1RE16.V operates like an unmasked unit-stride load with the base address supplied in scalar integer register rs1.
RULE 3: VL1RE16.V computes its effective vector length as evl = NFIELDS × VLEN / EEW, which for VL1RE16.V evaluates to evl = VLEN / 16.
RULE 4: VL1RE16.V does not suppress element writes when vstart ≥ vl; the usual rule that no elements are written if vstart ≥ vl does not apply.
RULE 5: If vstart ≥ evl, then executing VL1RE16.V raises an illegal‑instruction exception.
RULE 6: VL1RE16.V may raise a misaligned address exception if the base address is not naturally aligned to the larger of 2 bytes (EEW/8) or SEWMIN/8 bytes.
RULE 7: VL1RE16.V transfers VLEN/16 halfwords (i.e., VLEN/16 × 2 bytes) from the memory address in the base register into the destination vector register.

INSTRUCTION: VL1RE32.V
FORMAT: vl1re32.v vd, (rs1)
RULE 1: If the nf field of a VL1RE32.V instruction does not encode NFIELDS = 1, then an illegal‑instruction exception is raised.
RULE 2: VL1RE32.V MUST use a destination vector register number that is aligned as a vector register group, and the instruction encoding is reserved if this alignment is not satisfied.
RULE 3: If VL1RE32.V is encoded with an NFIELDS value other than 1, 2, 4, or 8, then executing the instruction raises an illegal‑instruction exception.
RULE 4: VL1RE32.V computes its effective vector length evl as VLEN / 32, regardless of the current vtype and vl settings.
RULE 5: VL1RE32.V is reserved when vstart ≥ VLEN / 32.
RULE 6: VL1RE32.V writes the raw byte contents from memory directly into the destination register group without any byte-reordering.
RULE 7: VL1RE32.V may raise a misaligned address exception if the base address is not naturally aligned to the greater of 4 bytes (EEW/8) or SEWMIN/8.

INSTRUCTION: VL1RE64.V
FORMAT: vl1re64.v vd, (rs1)
RULE 1: VL1RE64.V performs a whole-register load using the base address contained in the scalar integer register rs1.
RULE 2: VL1RE64.V is reserved if the destination vector register numbers are not aligned as a vector register group.
RULE 3: VL1RE64.V is reserved when the architectural register vstart holds a value greater than or equal to evl.
RULE 4: VL1RE64.V loads VLEN/64 doublewords (VLEN/8 bytes) from the memory address in rs1 into the destination vector register.
RULE 5: VL1RE64.V writes the loaded data to the destination register group using the same byte layout as in memory, independent of the element width EEW.
RULE 6: VL1RE64.V may raise a misaligned address exception if the base address is not naturally aligned to the larger of 8 bytes (EEW/8) or the implementation's minimum SEW in bytes.
RULE 7: VL1RE64.V obtains the base address from the scalar integer register specified by rs1, exactly as unmasked unit-stride load instructions do.
RULE 8: VL1RE64.V transfers a number of bytes equal to the current value of the vlenb register.

INSTRUCTION: VL2RE8.V
FORMAT: vl2re8.v vd, (rs1)
RULE 1: VL2RE8.V v2,(a0) loads vector registers v2 and v3 with 2 × VLEN/8 bytes from the memory address held in scalar register a0.
RULE 2: When VL2RE8.V transfers multiple registers, the lowest-numbered destination register receives data from the lowest-addressed memory locations and each subsequent register receives the next contiguous block of memory.
RULE 3: The usual rule that no elements are written when vstart ≥ vl does not apply to VL2RE8.V; VL2RE8.V is reserved if vstart ≥ evl.
RULE 4: The number of bytes transferred by VL2RE8.V can be obtained from the vlenb CSR and equals 2 × VLEN/8 bytes.

INSTRUCTION: VL2RE32.V
FORMAT: vl2re32.v vd, (rs1)
RULE 1: VL2RE32.V loads the vector register group v2-v3 with 2 × VLEN/32 words from the memory address contained in scalar register a0.
RULE 2: VL2RE32.V must have the encoded number of registers be a power of 2 and the vector register numbers be aligned as required for a vector register group, otherwise the encoding is reserved.
RULE 3: VL2RE32.V supports only NFIELDS values of 1, 2, 4, or 8; any other NFIELDS value makes the encoding reserved.
RULE 4: VL2RE32.V does not obey the usual rule that no elements are written when vstart ≥ vl.
RULE 5: VL2RE32.V does not treat the condition that vstart exceeds the largest element index for the current vtype as a reserved case.
RULE 6: VL2RE32.V is reserved if vstart ≥ evl.
RULE 7: VL2RE32.V operates like an unmasked unit-stride load, using the base address supplied in the scalar x register specified by rs1.
RULE 8: The number of bytes transferred by VL2RE32.V equals 2 × VLEN / 8 bytes and can be determined by reading the vlenb register.

INSTRUCTION: VL2RE64.V
FORMAT: vl2re64.v vd, (rs1)
RULE 1: VL2RE64.V v2, (a0) loads the vector registers v2-v3 with 2 * VLEN / 64 doublewords taken from the memory address contained in a0.
RULE 2: VL2RE64.V supports only NFIELDS values of 1, 2, 4, or 8; any other NFIELDS value is reserved.
RULE 3: VL2RE64.V requires that the destination vector register numbers be aligned as a vector register group, and mis-aligned register numbers make the encoding reserved.
RULE 4: VL2RE64.V operates with an effective vector length evl equal to NFIELDS * VLEN / EEW, independent of the current vtype and vl settings.
RULE 5: VL2RE64.V does not apply the usual rule that no elements are written when vstart >= vl.
RULE 6: VL2RE64.V does not treat vstart exceeding the largest element index for the current vtype as a reserved condition.
RULE 7: VL2RE64.V is reserved if vstart >= evl.
RULE 8: VL2RE64.V may raise a misaligned address exception when the base address in rs1 is not naturally aligned to the larger of EEW / 8 bytes or the implementation's smallest supported SEW size in bytes (SEWMIN / 8).
RULE 9: VL2RE64.V writes the same byte layout to the destination register group as exists in memory regardless of EEW, allowing the encoded EEW to serve as a hint for implementations that may rearrange data internally.

INSTRUCTION: VL4RE8.V
FORMAT: vl4re8.v vd, (rs1)
RULE 1: VL4RE8.V loads vector registers v4 through v7 (four consecutive registers) from memory starting at the address held in the scalar register specified as the base address (e.g., a0).
RULE 2: If the NFIELDS field of a VL4RE8.V instruction is not 1, 2, 4, or 8, the instruction raises an illegal‑instruction exception (mcause set to the illegal‑instruction code).
RULE 3: VL4RE8.V ignores the current vtype and vl settings; these settings do not affect its operation.
RULE 4: VL4RE8.V is reserved when the architectural register vstart is greater than or equal to the computed evl.
RULE 5: VL4RE8.V does not apply the usual rule that no elements are written when vstart is greater than or equal to vl.
RULE 6: VL4RE8.V writes the same byte data to the destination register group regardless of the EEW value because in-register byte layouts are identical to in-memory byte layouts.

INSTRUCTION: VL4RE16.V
FORMAT: vl4re16.v vd, (rs1)
RULE 1: VL4RE16.V loads a whole vector register group consisting of four consecutive vector registers.
RULE 2: VL4RE16.V requires that the NFIELDS field encode a power-of-two number of registers and that the destination vector registers be aligned as a vector register group, otherwise the encoding is reserved.
RULE 3: VL4RE16.V supports only NFIELDS values of 1, 2, 4, or 8, and any other NFIELDS value makes the encoding reserved.
RULE 4: When VL4RE16.V transfers multiple registers, the lowest-numbered destination register receives data from the lowest-numbered memory addresses and each subsequent register receives the next contiguous block of memory.
RULE 5: VL4RE16.V operates with an effective vector length evl equal to NFIELDS multiplied by VLEN divided by EEW, independent of the current settings in vtype and vl.
RULE 6: VL4RE16.V does not apply the usual property that no elements are written when vstart ≥ vl.
RULE 7: If vstart exceeds the largest element index for the current vtype when executing VL4RE16.V, then the instruction executes without raising an illegal‑instruction exception (mcause is not set to the illegal‑instruction code).
RULE 8: VL4RE16.V is reserved if vstart ≥ evl.
RULE 9: The byte layout of data loaded by VL4RE16.V into the destination register group is identical to the in-memory byte layout, so the same data is written regardless of the EEW value.

INSTRUCTION: VL4RE32.V
FORMAT: vl4re32.v vd, (rs1)
RULE 1: VL4RE32.V loads a whole vector register group consisting of four consecutive vector registers starting at the specified base register.
RULE 2: If the mew or width fields of a VL4RE32.V instruction do not encode an element width of 32 bits, then the instruction raises an illegal‑instruction exception.
RULE 3: VL4RE32.V requires that the encoded number of registers (four) be a power of 2 and that the destination vector registers be aligned as a vector register group, otherwise the encoding is reserved.
RULE 4: VL4RE32.V writes to the destination register group using the same byte layout as the source memory layout, so the data stored in the registers is identical to the memory representation regardless of EEW.
RULE 5: VL4RE32.V operates with an effective vector length evl equal to NFIELDS × VLEN / EEW, which for this instruction evaluates to 4 × VLEN / 32, independent of the current vtype and vl settings.
RULE 6: VL4RE32.V is reserved if the architectural register vstart is greater than or equal to evl, and the usual condition that no elements are written when vstart ≥ vl does not apply.
RULE 7: VL4RE32.V transfers a total of 4 × VLEN/8 bytes (four 32-bit words) from memory to the vector register group.

INSTRUCTION: VL4RE64.V
FORMAT: vl4re64.v vd, (rs1)
RULE 1: VL4RE64.V loads the vector registers v4 through v7 with doubleword elements from consecutive memory locations starting at the address held in the scalar register specified by rs1.
RULE 2: VL4RE64.V has NFIELDS equal to 4, indicating that four consecutive vector registers are loaded.
RULE 3: VL4RE64.V aligns the vector registers as a register group such that the lowest-numbered register receives the lowest-numbered memory addresses and successive registers are placed contiguously in memory.
RULE 4: VL4RE64.V is reserved when the architectural vstart register holds a value greater than or equal to evl.
RULE 5: The usual rule that no elements are written when vstart ≥ vl does not apply to VL4RE64.V.
RULE 6: VL4RE64.V uses the scalar x register given by rs1 as the base address for its unit-stride memory accesses.
RULE 7: VL4RE64.V transfers a total of 4 × vlenb bytes from memory to the destination vector register group.

INSTRUCTION: VL8RE8.V
FORMAT: vl8re8.v vd, (rs1)
RULE 1: VL8RE8.V must have its nf field set to 8, and any encoding whose nf field is not one of 1, 2, 4, or 8 is reserved.
RULE 2: VL8RE8.V requires that the encoded number of registers be a power of two and that the vector register numbers be aligned as a vector register group, and the encoding is reserved if either condition is violated.
RULE 3: If VL8RE8.V is executed, then it ignores the current vtype and vl registers and uses the effective vector length computed by RULE 4, so the number of elements processed and the resulting VL field equal that effective length regardless of the prior vtype or vl settings.
RULE 4: VL8RE8.V does not apply the usual rule that no elements are written when vstart ≥ vl.
RULE 5: VL8RE8.V is reserved if vstart ≥ evl (i.e., if vstart ≥ VLEN).
RULE 6: VL8RE8.V performs an unmasked unit-stride load, applying no mask to the memory operation.
RULE 7: VL8RE8.V may raise a misaligned address exception on a whole-register load if the base address in rs1 is not naturally aligned to the larger of EEW/8 bytes or SEWMIN/8 bytes; for VL8RE8.V the required alignment is at least SEWMIN/8 bytes because EEW/8 equals 1 byte.
RULE 8: VL8RE8.V writes to the destination register group the same byte layout that exists in memory, so the in-register byte arrangement is identical to the in-memory byte arrangement regardless of EEW.
RULE 9: VL8RE8.V does not modify the architectural registers vl or vtype.
RULE 10: VL8RE8.V loads exactly 8 × VLEN/8 bytes from memory into the vector registers v8 through v15.

INSTRUCTION: VL8RE16.V
FORMAT: vl8re16.v vd, (rs1)
RULE 1: VL8RE16.V writes the same data to the destination register group regardless of the encoded EEW value.
RULE 2: If VL8RE16.V is executed with an NFIELDS field other than 8, then it raises an illegal‑instruction exception.
RULE 3: VL8RE16.V computes its effective vector length (evl) as 8 × VLEN / EEW, and this evl is used independently of the current vtype and vl settings.
RULE 4: VL8RE16.V is reserved when the architectural vstart register is greater than or equal to the computed evl.
RULE 5: The usual architectural rule that no elements are written when vstart ≥ vl does not apply to VL8RE16.V.
RULE 6: VL8RE16.V behaves like an unmasked unit-stride load, using the scalar register rs1 as the base address for the memory operation.

INSTRUCTION: VL8RE32.V
FORMAT: vl8re32.v vd, (rs1)
RULE 1: VL8RE32.V loads the vector register group v8-v15 from memory starting at the address contained in the scalar integer register specified as rs1.
RULE 2: VL8RE32.V is reserved if the encoded number of registers is not a power of two.
RULE 3: VL8RE32.V is reserved if the vector register numbers are not aligned as a vector register group.
RULE 4: VL8RE32.V is reserved for NFIELDS values other than 1, 2, 4, or 8.
RULE 5: VL8RE32.V computes its effective vector length evl as NFIELDS × VLEN ÷ EEW, which for VL8RE32.V equals 8 × VLEN ÷ 32.
RULE 6: VL8RE32.V uses the computed evl instead of the current vl and vtype settings.
RULE 7: VL8RE32.V does not apply the usual rule that no elements are written when vstart ≥ vl.
RULE 8: VL8RE32.V is reserved if vstart ≥ evl.

INSTRUCTION: VL8RE64.V
FORMAT: vl8re64.v vd, (rs1)
RULE 1: For VL8RE64.V the nf field must encode a power-of-2 number of registers and the destination vector register numbers must be aligned as a vector register group, otherwise the encoding is reserved.
RULE 2: For VL8RE64.V only NFIELDS values of 1, 2, 4, or 8 are supported; any other NFIELDS value makes the encoding reserved.
RULE 3: For VL8RE64.V the NFIELDS value is 8, causing the instruction to load vector registers v8 through v15.
RULE 4: For VL8RE64.V the instruction is reserved when the architectural register vstart is greater than or equal to evl.
RULE 5: For VL8RE64.V the usual property that no elements are written when vstart ≥ vl does not apply.
RULE 6: For VL8RE64.V the usual property that the instruction is reserved when vstart exceeds the largest element index for the current vtype does not apply.
RULE 7: For VL8RE64.V the byte layout of the loaded data in the destination registers matches the byte layout of the data in memory, independent of the EEW.
RULE 8: For VL8RE64.V the base address is taken from the scalar integer register specified by rs1, and the instruction behaves like an unmasked unit-stride load.
RULE 9: For VL8RE64.V v8,(a0) the instruction loads vector registers v8 through v15 with 8 × VLEN / 64 doubleword elements from the memory address held in a0.

INSTRUCTION: VLE8.V
FORMAT: vle8.v vd, (rs1), vm
RULE 1: VLE8.V loads an 8-bit element from memory for each active vector element, using a unit-stride address that increments by one byte for successive elements starting from the byte address held in the scalar register rs1.
RULE 2: VLE8.V stores each loaded 8-bit value into the corresponding element position of the destination vector register vd without any additional widening or sign-extension.
RULE 3: VLE8.V applies the mask operand; when the mask encoding is v0.t, only those element positions whose mask bit is true are loaded, and element positions with a false mask bit retain their previous value in vd.
RULE 4: VLE8.V operates as an unmasked load when the mask operand is omitted, loading all element positions of vd.

INSTRUCTION: VLE8FF.V
FORMAT: vle8ff.v vd, (rs1), vm
RULE 1: VLE8FF.V executes as a normal vector load but takes a trap only for a synchronous exception that occurs on element 0.
RULE 2: If a synchronous exception occurs on element 0 during execution of VLE8FF.V, the vector length register vl is not modified and the trap is taken.
RULE 3: If a synchronous exception would occur on any element with index greater than 0 during execution of VLE8FF.V, the trap is not taken and vl is reduced to the index of that element.
RULE 4: When VLE8FF.V executes without raising an exception and vstart = 0 with vl > 0, the implementation must process at least one element and may reduce vl to reflect processing fewer than the original vl elements.
RULE 5: If VLE8FF.V would cause a debug data-watchpoint trap on any element after the first, the implementation should not reduce vl and must deliver the debug trap.

INSTRUCTION: VLE16.V
FORMAT: vle16.v vd, (rs1), vm
RULE 1: VLE16.V must load 16-bit elements from memory starting at the address contained in rs1 and increment the address by 2 bytes for each successive element (unit-stride) into the destination vector register vd.
RULE 2: VLE16.V must use the mask encoding vm to control element-wise writes, writing a loaded element to vd only when the corresponding mask bit is 1 and leaving the element in vd unchanged when the mask bit is 0.
RULE 3: VLE16.V specifies that the vd operand identifies the destination vector register that receives the loaded 16-bit elements.

INSTRUCTION: VLE16FF.V
FORMAT: vle16ff.v vd, (rs1), vm
RULE 1: VLE16FF.V MUST execute as a regular load and MUST only take a trap caused by a synchronous exception on element 0.
RULE 2: If element 0 raises an exception during execution of VLE16FF.V, vl MUST remain unchanged and the trap MUST be taken.
RULE 3: If an element with index greater than 0 raises an exception during VLE16FF.V, the trap MUST NOT be taken and vl MUST be reduced to that element's index.
RULE 4: If no exception is raised during VLE16FF.V, the implementation MAY process fewer than vl elements and reduce vl accordingly, but MUST process at least one element when vstart = 0 and vl > 0.

INSTRUCTION: VLE32.V
FORMAT: vle32.v vd, (rs1), vm
RULE 1: VLE32.V must read a sequence of 32-bit elements from memory starting at the address contained in integer register rs1 and using a unit stride of four bytes for successive elements.
RULE 2: VLE32.V must write each 32-bit element that is successfully read into the corresponding element of the destination vector register group vd for which the element's mask bit in vm is 1.
RULE 3: VLE32.V must leave the destination element unchanged for any element whose mask bit in vm is 0.
RULE 4: VLE32.V must not modify any integer register, including rs1, as part of its normal operation.

INSTRUCTION: VLE32FF.V
FORMAT: vle32ff.v vd, (rs1), vm
RULE 1: VLE32FF.V MUST execute as a regular load except that it will only take a trap caused by a synchronous exception on element 0.
RULE 2: If element 0 raises an exception during VLE32FF.V, the trap is taken and vl MUST NOT be modified.
RULE 3: If any element greater than 0 raises an exception during VLE32FF.V, the trap MUST NOT be taken and vl MUST be reduced to the index of that element.
RULE 4: If vstart = 0 and vl > 0 for VLE32FF.V, then at least one element MUST be processed.

INSTRUCTION: VLE64.V
FORMAT: vle64.v vd, (rs1), vm
RULE 1: VLE64.V loads a sequence of 64-bit elements from memory starting at the address contained in integer register rs1 and stores the loaded elements into the destination vector register group specified by vd.
RULE 2: VLE64.V uses a unit stride of 8 bytes, so the effective address for element i is rs1 plus i × 8 bytes.
RULE 3: VLE64.V applies the vector mask vm such that for each element i, if the corresponding mask bit is 1 the element is loaded from memory, and if the mask bit is 0 the element in vd is left unchanged.
RULE 4: VLE64.V writes only the elements selected by the mask vm; elements not selected retain their previous values in the destination vector register group.
RULE 5: VLE64.V operates on a 64-bit element width irrespective of other vector configuration settings.

INSTRUCTION: VLM.V
FORMAT: vlm.v vd, (rs1)
RULE 1: VLM.V transfers mask values from memory to the destination vector register.
RULE 2: VLM.V performs a unit-stride load of bytes and operates like an unmasked byte load with EEW = 8.
RULE 3: VLM.V computes its effective vector length as evl = ceil(vl / 8), which corresponds to EMUL = 1.
RULE 4: If VLM.V writes defined values to destination register elements beyond the current VL (e.g., all zeros or any deterministic pattern), then the required tail‑agnostic policy is violated, observable as those tail elements containing the defined values instead of being left unspecified.
RULE 5: VLM.V interprets the vstart register in units of bytes for this instruction.
RULE 6: If the vill bit in vtype is set, then executing VLM.V raises an illegal‑instruction exception.

INSTRUCTION: VLOXEI8.V
FORMAT: vloxei8.v vd, (rs1), vs2, vm
RULE 1: VLOXEI8.V performs an ordered indexed load of vector elements whose size is defined by the current SEW.
RULE 2: VLOXEI8.V executes an ordered 8-bit indexed load, using 8-bit indices to address SEW-sized data elements.
RULE 3: VSXEI8.V is an alias for VLOXEI8.V and therefore exhibits identical architectural behavior.

INSTRUCTION: VLOXEI16.V
FORMAT: vloxei16.v vd, (rs1), vs2, vm
RULE 1: VLOXEI16.V loads each vector element using a 16-bit index taken from the corresponding element of source vector register vs2 and a base address held in integer register rs1.
RULE 2: VLOXEI16.V stores each loaded element into the corresponding element of destination vector register vd.
RULE 3: VLOXEI16.V loads data whose width equals the currently configured standard element width (SEW).
RULE 4: VLOXEI16.V respects the element-wise mask vm, leaving the destination element unchanged and suppressing the memory access for any element whose mask bit is clear.

INSTRUCTION: VLOXEI32.V
FORMAT: vloxei32.v vd, (rs1), vs2, vm
RULE 1: VLOXEI32.V MUST perform an ordered 32-bit indexed load of SEW (Standard-Element-Width) data.
RULE 2: VLOXEI32.V MUST use the statically encoded EEW that corresponds to the index element width, not the width of the loaded data.
RULE 3: VLOXEI32.V uses vd as the destination vector register that receives the loaded elements.
RULE 4: VLOXEI32.V uses vs2 to provide the byte-offsets applied to the base address for each element.
RULE 5: VLOXEI32.V uses vm as the mask register that determines which elements are loaded.

INSTRUCTION: VLOXEI64.V
FORMAT: vloxei64.v vd, (rs1), vs2, vm
RULE 1: VLOXEI64.V MUST execute an ordered load that reads vector elements of standard element width (SEW) from memory using 64-bit index values.
RULE 2: VLOXEI64.V MUST interpret the statically encoded EEW field as the element width for the index vector and must not apply that EEW to the width of the loaded data elements.

INSTRUCTION: VLSE8.V
FORMAT: vlse8.v vd, (rs1), rs2, vm
RULE 1: VLSE8.V must support negative and zero stride values.
RULE 2: If rs2 ≠ x0 and the stride value loaded from the address in rs2 equals zero, then VLSE8.V performs a separate memory load for each active element, causing the target address to be read exactly once per active element (the accesses may occur in any order).
RULE 3: VLSE8.V implementations must appear to perform each memory access.
RULE 4: If a VLSE8.V instruction reads from a non‑idempotent memory address, then the implementation must issue a load transaction to that address; if the load is optimized away, the device’s side‑effect observable state (e.g., an access‑counter CSR or register) will not change, indicating a missing memory access.

INSTRUCTION: VLSE16.V
FORMAT: vlse16.v vd, (rs1), rs2, vm
RULE 1: VLSE16.V supports negative and zero stride values.
RULE 2: If rs2 ≠ x0 and the register specified by rs2 contains zero, then VLSE16.V must issue a separate memory access for each active element (order of accesses is undefined).
RULE 3: If VLSE16.V omits any memory read required by its architectural definition, then the destination vector register will contain values that differ from the corresponding memory locations.
RULE 4: If a VLSE16.V instruction accesses a non‑idempotent memory‑mapped I/O region, then every element load must be performed; if any load is omitted, the device will not observe that access and its observable state (e.g., a device‑specific status CSR) will remain unchanged, revealing the violation.

INSTRUCTION: VLSE32.V
FORMAT: vlse32.v vd, (rs1), rs2, vm
RULE 1: If a VLSE32.V instruction omits any memory access required by the architectural specification, then the destination vector register will contain values that do not match the corresponding memory contents.
RULE 2: If a VLSE32.V instruction accesses a non‑idempotent memory region and the implementation eliminates any required memory read, then the memory‑mapped I/O register that records those accesses will not be incremented (its readback value remains unchanged), indicating a violation.

INSTRUCTION: VLSE64.V
FORMAT: vlse64.v vd, (rs1), rs2, vm
RULE 1: VLSE64.V must support both negative and zero stride values.
RULE 2: If a VLSE64.V constant‑stride instruction reads memory locations that are modified between its element accesses, then the destination vector register may contain any combination of the old and new values because the element accesses are unordered.
RULE 3: Implementations of VLSE64.V must appear to perform each memory access prescribed by the instruction.
RULE 4: If a VLSE64.V instruction accesses a non‑idempotent memory region and any element load is omitted (i.e., optimized away), then the corresponding device‑observable side‑effect for that element will be missing, which can be detected by reading the device’s status CSR or other architectural state that reflects the access.

INSTRUCTION: VLSEG2E32.V
FORMAT: vlseg2e32.v vd, (rs1), vm
RULE 1: VLSEG2E32.V MUST be implemented as a unit-stride segment load that transfers packed contiguous memory segments into a group of destination vector registers.
RULE 2: If NFIELDS is not 2 or EEW is not 32 bits for VLSEG2E32.V, then an illegal‑instruction exception is raised.
RULE 3: When VLSEG2E32.V performs a load, the destination register specified as vd MUST receive the first field of each segment, and the next vector register in the same register group MUST receive the second field of each segment.
RULE 4: VLSEG2E32.V MUST use the current vector length (VL) and element width configuration that were established by the most recent vsetvli instruction.

INSTRUCTION: VLSEG8E8.V
FORMAT: vlseg8e8.v vd, (rs1), vm
RULE 1: When VLSEG8E8.V executes, the destination register specified by vd receives the first field of the loaded segment.
RULE 2: VLSEG8E8.V loads eight vector registers, forming a destination register group that begins with vd.
RULE 3: Each of the eight vector registers loaded by VLSEG8E8.V receives an 8-bit (byte) field from memory.
RULE 4: VLSEG8E8.V uses the address in rs1 as a unit-stride base address for memory accesses.
RULE 5: VLSEG8E8.V applies the optional mask vm to each element load operation.
RULE 6: VLSEG8E8.V has NFIELDS equal to 8 and an element width of 8 bits.

INSTRUCTION: VLSSEG3E8.V
FORMAT: vlsseg3e8.v vd, (rs1), rs2, vm
RULE 1: VLSSEG3E8.V loads three 8-bit elements per segment, storing the first element in the vector register group beginning at vd, the second element in the group beginning at vd+1, and the third element in the group beginning at vd+2.
RULE 2: For each element index i, VLSSEG3E8.V reads a byte from the memory address rs1 + i × rs2 and writes that byte to element i of the first segment (vd[i]).
RULE 3: For each element index i, VLSSEG3E8.V reads a byte from the memory address rs1 + i × rs2 + 1 and writes that byte to element i of the second segment (vd+1[i]).
RULE 4: For each element index i, VLSSEG3E8.V reads a byte from the memory address rs1 + i × rs2 + 2 and writes that byte to element i of the third segment (vd+2[i]).
RULE 5: The stride supplied in rs2 for VLSSEG3E8.V is a signed byte-stride, therefore negative, zero, and positive stride values are all supported.
RULE 6: VLSSEG3E8.V may perform the three field accesses of each segment in any order, and the architectural result is unaffected even when the stride causes the segments to overlap in memory.

INSTRUCTION: VLUXEI8.V
FORMAT: vluxei8.v vd, (rs1), vs2, vm
RULE 1: VLUXEI8.V loads SEW-sized elements from memory into the destination vector register vd using 8-bit indices taken from the source vector register vs2.
RULE 2: For each element i, VLUXEI8.V computes the effective address by adding the sign-extended 8-bit index value from vs2[i] to the base address held in integer register rs1.
RULE 3: VLUXEI8.V updates element i of vd with the memory byte retrieved from the effective address only if the corresponding mask bit vm[i] is 1, and leaves element i of vd unchanged when vm[i] is 0.

INSTRUCTION: VLUXEI16.V
FORMAT: vluxei16.v vd, (rs1), vs2, vm
RULE 1: VLUXEI16.V MUST load 16-bit SEW elements from memory into the destination vector register specified by vd.
RULE 2: VLUXEI16.V computes the memory address for each element i by adding the byte offset contained in element i of vs2 to the base address held in rs1.
RULE 3: If a memory location accessed by VLUXEI16.V is modified after one element has been loaded but before another element is loaded, then the destination vector element for the later load must still contain the value that was present before the first load (the load order must not affect the architectural result).
RULE 4: VLUXEI16.V updates element i of vd only if the corresponding mask bit i in vm is set; if the mask bit is clear, element i of vd retains its previous value.

INSTRUCTION: VLUXEI32.V
FORMAT: vluxei32.v vd, (rs1), vs2, vm
RULE 1: VLUXEI32.V MUST perform an unordered indexed load of SEW data using a 32-bit element index.
RULE 2: VLUXEI32.V MUST use a statically encoded EEW that applies to the index rather than to the loaded data elements.

INSTRUCTION: VLUXEI64.V
FORMAT: vluxei64.v vd, (rs1), vs2, vm
RULE 1: VLUXEI64.V must perform an unordered indexed load that uses 64-bit index elements to compute memory addresses and loads vector elements of the current SEW.

INSTRUCTION: VLUXSEG3EI8.V
FORMAT: vluxseg3ei8.v vd, (rs1), vs2, vm
RULE 1: VLUXSEG3EI8.V executed as vluxseg3ei8.v v4, (x5), v3 loads the byte at address x5+v3[i] into element i of v4, the byte at address x5+v3[i]+1 into element i of v5, and the byte at address x5+v3[i]+2 into element i of v6 for each active element i.
RULE 2: VLUXSEG3EI8.V computes each segment base address by adding the scalar base address in rs1 to the corresponding byte-offset element in the index vector vs2.
RULE 3: VLUXSEG3EI8.V requires that the data vector register group have EEW equal to SEW and EMUL equal to LMUL.
RULE 4: If the index vector register group for VLUXSEG3EI8.V does not encode EEW as the element width or its EMUL field is not equal to (EEW / SEW) × LMUL, then the instruction raises an illegal‑instruction exception.
RULE 5: VLUXSEG3EI8.V requires that EMUL multiplied by NFIELDS be less than or equal to 8 for the data vector register group.
RULE 6: VLUXSEG3EI8.V requires that the destination vector register group must not overlap the source index vector register group; overlapping makes the encoding reserved.
RULE 7: VLUXSEG3EI8.V allows restart of an indexed segment load that raises an exception partway through loading a structure because the EMUL × NFIELDS ≤ 8 constraint is satisfied.

INSTRUCTION: VMACC.VV
FORMAT: vmacc.vv vd, vs1, vs2, vm
RULE 1: VMACC.VV performs an integer multiply-add operation that overwrites the addend vector register.
RULE 2: For each element index i where the mask bit vm[i] is 1, VMACC.VV computes the product of the corresponding elements vs1[i] and vs2[i].
RULE 3: For each active element i, VMACC.VV adds the low half of the product vs1[i] * vs2[i] to the original value of vd[i] and writes the sum to vd[i].
RULE 4: For each element index i where the mask bit vm[i] is 0, VMACC.VV leaves the element vd[i] unchanged.
RULE 5: The low half of the product generated by VMACC.VV is the portion used in the addition; the high half is discarded.
RULE 6: VMACC.VV is classified as a destructive overwrite-addend instruction, meaning the original contents of vd are replaced for active elements.
RULE 7: If VMACC.VV is executed, then only the standard integer‑vector privilege checks apply: any condition that would raise an illegal‑instruction or privilege exception for a normal integer vector instruction causes the same exception for VMACC.VV, and no additional VMACC.VV‑specific traps or exceptions are generated.

INSTRUCTION: VMACC.VX
FORMAT: vmacc.vx vd, rs1, vs2, vm
RULE 1: VMACC.VX computes, for each element i whose mask vm bit is 1, vd[i] = (low-half(x[rs1] * vs2[i])) + vd[i].
RULE 2: VMACC.VX leaves vd[i] unchanged for each element i whose mask vm bit is 0.
RULE 3: The low-half used by VMACC.VX is defined as the least-significant XLEN bits of the full integer product of x[rs1] and vs2[i].
RULE 4: VMACC.VX overwrites the destination vector register vd with the result of the multiply-add operation, destroying the original addend values.

INSTRUCTION: VMADC.VI
FORMAT: vmadc.vi vd, vs2, simm5
RULE 1: VMADC.VI MUST compute, for each element i of the active vector length, the carry-out of the addition of vs2[i] and the immediate operand imm with no carry-in added.
RULE 2: VMADC.VI MUST write the carry-out bit produced for element i into the mask field of the destination vector register vd.
RULE 3: VMADC.VI MUST operate on and write back all elements of the active vector length even if the corresponding mask bit in v0.mask is zero.
RULE 4: VMADC.VI MUST NOT read the destination mask register as a source operand; only vs2 and the immediate are source operands.

INSTRUCTION: VMADC.VIM
FORMAT: vmadc.vim vd, vs2, simm5, v0
RULE 1: VMADC.VIM computes each destination mask bit vd.mask[i] as the carry-out of the addition of the corresponding element vs2[i], the encoded immediate, and the corresponding v0 mask bit v0.mask[i].
RULE 2: VMADC.VIM is encoded with vm=0 and therefore must operate on and write back all body elements regardless of the element mask.
RULE 3: If VMADC.VIM does not apply a tail‑agnostic policy, then after execution the destination mask register contains non‑zero mask bits for element indices ≥ the current VL.
RULE 4: For each element i, VMADC.VIM takes the carry-in from the implicit register v0 mask bit v0.mask[i].
RULE 5: VMADC.VIM writes the resulting per-element carry-out into the destination register vd in mask-register format.
RULE 6: VMADC.VIM may write its result to any destination vector register that does not violate the architectural source/destination overlap restrictions.

INSTRUCTION: VMADC.VV
FORMAT: vmadc.vv vd, vs2, vs1
RULE 1: VMADC.VV adds each active element i of vs2 and vs1 using the element width defined by the current SEW, does not add a carry-in, and writes the resulting carry-out to mask bit i of vd.
RULE 2: VMADC.VV processes and writes back every body element of the destination mask register regardless of any element-wise masking.
RULE 3: If VMADC.VV does not treat tail elements as tail‑agnostic, then after execution the destination mask register contains a defined value (e.g., all zeros) for mask bits at indices ≥ VL, which can be observed by reading that register.
RULE 4: VMADC.VV is defined only for the unmasked vm field value of 1, and in this form no carry-in is added to the sum.
RULE 5: The encoding of VMADC.VV does not reserve the case where the destination register vd is v0, so vd may be v0.
RULE 6: VMADC.VV does not treat the encoding with destination register v0 as reserved; that reservation applies only to vadc and vsbc.

INSTRUCTION: VMADC.VX
FORMAT: vmadc.vx vd, vs2, rs1
RULE 1: VMADC.VX computes for each element i within the active vector length the carry-out of the addition of vs2[i] and the scalar integer value in rs1, and stores that carry-out into mask bit i of the destination vector register vd.
RULE 2: If VMADC.VX produces results for vector elements i ≥ VL, then those tail elements have no defined architectural effect (their values are unpredictable) and the instruction does not raise an exception.
RULE 3: VMADC.VX includes no carry-in to the per-element addition because it is the unmasked variant (vm = 1).
RULE 4: VMADC.VX processes and writes back mask bits for all body elements of the active vector length even when a mask register is present.
RULE 5: VMADC.VX writes the resulting mask bits into the mask field of the destination vector register vd.

INSTRUCTION: VMADC.VXM
FORMAT: vmadc.vxm vd, vs2, rs1, v0
RULE 1: VMADC.VXM with vm=0 (masked) adds each element of source vector vs2, the scalar value broadcast from integer register rs1, and the corresponding mask bit from the implicit vector register v0, and writes the resulting carry-out into the mask bit of destination register vd for every element i.
RULE 2: VMADC.VXM with vm=1 (unmasked) adds each element of source vector vs2 and the scalar value broadcast from integer register rs1 without any carry-in, and writes the resulting carry-out into the mask bit of destination register vd for every element i.
RULE 3: VMADC.VXM operates on and writes back all body elements of the destination register regardless of whether the instruction is masked or unmasked.
RULE 4: If the tail elements (indices ≥ VL) of the source vectors are altered, then the mask bits of the destination register for indices < VL must be identical to the result obtained when those tail elements are zero, while the mask bits for indices ≥ VL may contain any value.
RULE 5: VMADC.VXM obtains its carry-in bits exclusively from the mask layout of the implicit vector register v0.
RULE 6: VMADC.VXM stores its carry-out result in the mask layout of the destination vector register vd.

INSTRUCTION: VMADD.VV
FORMAT: vmadd.vv vd, vs1, vs2, vm
RULE 1: For each element index i where the corresponding mask element vm[i] is true, VMADD.VV must compute the low half of the product of vs1[i] and the original vd[i] and then add vs2[i] to produce the result written to vd[i].
RULE 2: For each element index i where the corresponding mask element vm[i] is false, VMADD.VV must leave the original value of vd[i] unchanged.
RULE 3: The low half of the product used by VMADD.VV consists of the least significant XLEN bits of the full-width integer multiplication result.
RULE 4: VMADD.VV must overwrite the destination register vd with the computed results, thereby destroying the original multiplicand values.

INSTRUCTION: VMADD.VX
FORMAT: vmadd.vx vd, rs1, vs2, vm
RULE 1: VMADD.VX must be destructive by overwriting the original contents of the destination vector register vd with the computed results, thereby replacing the first multiplicand.

INSTRUCTION: VMAND.MM
FORMAT: vmand.mm vd, vs2, vs1
RULE 1: VMAND.MM performs an element-wise logical AND of two source mask registers and writes the result to a destination mask register.
RULE 2: For each mask element, VMAND.MM sets the destination mask bit only when the corresponding bits of both source mask registers are set, and clears it otherwise.
RULE 3: VMAND.MM computes each mask element of the destination register as the logical AND of the corresponding mask elements of the two source mask registers, i.e., vd.mask[i] = vs2.mask[i] && vs1.mask[i].
RULE 4: Execution of VMAND.MM must not modify the vlmul field in vtype.
RULE 5: Elements of the mask registers with indices less than the current vstart value are left unchanged by VMAND.MM.
RULE 6: After VMAND.MM completes, the architectural vstart register is reset to zero.
RULE 7: VMAND.MM is always executed unmasked, meaning that no elements are considered inactive regardless of any masking configuration.
RULE 8: Encodings of VMAND.MM that set the vm bit to 0 are reserved.
RULE 9: For VMAND.MM, mask elements with indices greater than or equal to the current vector length (vl) are updated according to the tail-agnostic policy.

INSTRUCTION: VMANDN.MM
FORMAT: vmandn.mm vd, vs2, vs1
RULE 1: VMANDN.MM must compute for each element i that vd.mask[i] = vs2.mask[i] && !vs1.mask[i].
RULE 2: VMANDN.MM may use the destination register vd that is the same physical register as either source register vs2 or vs1.
RULE 3: For VMANDN.MM execution, elements with indices less than the current vstart value must remain unchanged.
RULE 4: After VMANDN.MM completes, the architectural register vstart must be reset to zero.
RULE 5: Execution of VMANDN.MM must not modify the architectural register vlmul.
RULE 6: For VMANDN.MM, elements with indices greater than or equal to the current vl value (tail elements) must be updated according to the tail-agnostic policy.
RULE 7: VMANDN.MM is always executed unmasked; there are no inactive elements for this instruction.
RULE 8: Encodings of VMANDN.MM with the vm field equal to zero are reserved.
RULE 9: The form VMANDN.MM vd, src2, src1 implements the logical function src2 && !src1 for each mask element.
RULE 10: The form VMANDN.MM vd, src1, src2 implements the logical function src1 && !src2 for each mask element.

INSTRUCTION: VMAX.VV
FORMAT: vmax.vv vd, vs2, vs1, vm
RULE 1: VMAX.VV is a signed integer maximum instruction that selects the greater of two signed integer operands.
RULE 2: For each element position i, VMAX.VV computes the signed maximum of the i-th element of source register vs2 and the i-th element of source register vs1 and writes the result to the i-th element of destination register vd when the corresponding mask bit is 1 or when no mask is supplied.
RULE 3: When a mask bit for element i is 0 in the mask register vm, VMAX.VV leaves the i-th element of destination register vd unchanged.
RULE 4: VMAX.VV operates on vector-vector data, meaning it processes corresponding elements of the two source vector registers versus each other.

INSTRUCTION: VMAX.VX
FORMAT: vmax.vx vd, vs2, rs1, vm
RULE 1: VMAX.VX is a signed integer maximum instruction that computes the signed integer maximum of the source operands.

INSTRUCTION: VMAXU.VV
FORMAT: vmaxu.vv vd, vs2, vs1, vm
RULE 1: VMAXU.VV MUST compute the element-wise unsigned maximum of the corresponding elements of source vector registers vs2 and vs1 and store each result element into the corresponding element of destination vector register vd.
RULE 2: VMAXU.VV MUST operate in vector-vector mode, requiring both source operands to be vector registers and producing a vector result in a vector register.

INSTRUCTION: VMAXU.VX
FORMAT: vmaxu.vx vd, vs2, rs1, vm
RULE 1: VMAXU.VX computes, for each element index i, the unsigned maximum of the unsigned value of the source vector element vs2[i] and the unsigned scalar value read from integer register rs1.
RULE 2: VMAXU.VX writes the unsigned maximum result into destination vector element vd[i] when the corresponding mask bit in vm is 1, and preserves the prior value of vd[i] when that mask bit is 0.
RULE 3: VMAXU.VX treats the scalar operand obtained from integer register rs1 as an unsigned integer for the maximum comparison with each element of vs2.

INSTRUCTION: VMERGE.VIM
FORMAT: vmerge.vim vd, vs2, simm5, v0
RULE 1: VMERGE.VIM must obtain the mask bits for each element i from the mask register v0 (v0.mask[i]).
RULE 2: For each element i with index satisfying vstart ≤ i < vl, VMERGE.VIM must write vd[i] = imm when v0.mask[i] is one, and must write vd[i] = vs2[i] when v0.mask[i] is zero.
RULE 3: For each element i that does not satisfy vstart ≤ i < vl, VMERGE.VIM must leave the destination element vd[i] unchanged.
RULE 4: VMERGE.VIM must operate only on the body elements, i.e., the set of elements from vstart up to the current vector length vl.
RULE 5: VMERGE.VIM must be encoded as a masked instruction with the vm field set to zero, causing the mask bits from v0 to be applied to the operation.

INSTRUCTION: VMERGE.VVM
FORMAT: vmerge.vvm vd, vs2, vs1, v0
RULE 1: VMERGE.VVM operates on all body elements, i.e., the element indices from vstart up to the current vector length vl.
RULE 2: In VMERGE.VVM the mask operand is supplied by a mask register such as v0.
RULE 3: For each element index i processed by VMERGE.VVM, if the mask bit v0.mask[i] is zero then the destination element vd[i] is set to the element vs2[i].
RULE 4: For each element index i processed by VMERGE.VVM, if the mask bit v0.mask[i] is one then the destination element vd[i] is set to the element vs1[i].

INSTRUCTION: VMFEQ.VF
FORMAT: vmfeq.vf vd, vs2, fs1, vm
RULE 1: VMFEQ.VF writes a 0 to mask element i of vd when the compared floating-point value from vs2 element i is not equal to the scalar floating-point value from rs1 or when either operand is NaN.
RULE 2: VMFEQ.VF raises an invalid-operation exception if and only if a signaling NaN is supplied as either the vector element or the scalar operand during the comparison.
RULE 3: VMFEQ.VF implements the comparison semantics defined for the scalar floating-point equal-compare instruction for each element of the vector.
