RULE 4: VMFEQ.VF stores the destination mask vector in a single vector register according to the element layout defined for mask registers.
RULE 5: VMFEQ.VF may use the same register for the destination mask register vd and the source mask register v0.
RULE 6: If VMFEQ.VF writes a mask register when the effective vector length VL is less than the register length, then for every mask element index i ≥ VL the resulting mask‑register bit is unspecified (tail‑agnostic), i.e., the architecture imposes no defined value for those elements.

INSTRUCTION: VMFEQ.VV
FORMAT: vmfeq.vv vd, vs2, vs1, vm
RULE 1: VMFEQ.VV writes its result to the destination vector register specified by vd.
RULE 2: VMFEQ.VV uses the mask register specified by vm to predicate the element-wise operation.

INSTRUCTION: VMFGE.VF
FORMAT: vmfge.vf vd, vs2, fs1, vm
RULE 1: VMFGE.VF writes its result to a single destination mask register (vd) that holds one mask bit per element as defined for mask vectors.
RULE 2: VMFGE.VF permits the destination mask register (vd) to be the same register as the source mask register v0.
RULE 3: VMFGE.VF compares each active element of the source vector register vs2 with the scalar floating-point value in rs1 using the greater-than-or-equal relation.
RULE 4: VMFGE.VF may use an optional mask register (vm) to enable or disable element-wise operation, and elements with a mask bit of zero are not written to the destination.
RULE 5: For each active element, VMFGE.VF sets the corresponding destination mask bit to 1 if the vs2 element is greater than or equal to the scalar rs1, otherwise it sets the bit to 0.
RULE 6: VMFGE.VF raises the invalid-operation exception when either the vs2 element or the scalar rs1 is a signaling or quiet NaN.
RULE 7: When either operand is NaN, VMFGE.VF writes 0 to the corresponding destination mask element.

INSTRUCTION: VMFGT.VF
FORMAT: vmfgt.vf vd, vs2, fs1, vm
RULE 1: VMFGT.VF performs an element-wise greater-than comparison between each element of the source vector register vs2 and the scalar floating-point value in rs1.
RULE 2: VMFGT.VF writes the result of each element comparison to a single destination mask register vd.
RULE 3: If VMFGT.VF writes to a mask register, then every mask register element with index i ≥ VL is cleared to zero (the destination mask register’s tail bits are zero).
RULE 4: VMFGT.VF updates only those elements of vd whose corresponding mask bit in the mask register vm is active; elements with an inactive mask bit are not written.
RULE 5: VMFGT.VF writes a 1 to an element of vd when the element of vs2 is greater than the scalar in rs1 and neither operand is NaN.
RULE 6: VMFGT.VF writes a 0 to an element of vd when the element of vs2 is not greater than the scalar in rs1 or when either operand is NaN.
RULE 7: VMFGT.VF raises the invalid-operation floating-point exception when any operand involved in a comparison is a signaling NaN.
RULE 8: VMFGT.VF raises the invalid-operation floating-point exception when any operand involved in a comparison is a quiet NaN.

INSTRUCTION: VMFLE.VF
FORMAT: vmfle.vf vd, vs2, fs1, vm
RULE 1: VMFLE.VF writes its result to a single destination mask register, and the destination mask register may be the same register as source mask register v0.
RULE 2: VMFLE.VF operates under a tail-agnostic policy, causing mask elements beyond the effective vector length to be written as 0.
RULE 3: VMFLE.VF compares each active element of the source vector register versus the scalar floating-point register using the scalar floating-point compare-less-than-or-equal operation.
RULE 4: VMFLE.VF raises the invalid-operation exception for any element where either the vector element or the scalar operand is a NaN, regardless of whether the NaN is signaling or quiet.
RULE 5: VMFLE.VF writes a 0 to the corresponding mask element for any element where either operand is a NaN.
RULE 6: VMFLE.VF writes a 1 to the corresponding mask element only when the vector element is ordered (neither operand is NaN) and the vector element is less-than-or-equal to the scalar operand.
RULE 7: VMFLE.VF writes a 0 to the corresponding mask element when both operands are numbers and the vector element is greater than the scalar operand.
RULE 8: VMFLE.VF updates mask elements only for those elements for which the mask register vm is true; mask elements with vm false remain unchanged.

INSTRUCTION: VMFLE.VV
FORMAT: vmfle.vv vd, vs2, vs1, vm
RULE 1: VMFLE.VV performs an element-wise comparison for each element i evaluating whether vs2[i] ≤ vs1[i] and writes the Boolean result to the corresponding element of the destination mask register vd.
RULE 2: VMFLE.VV writes a mask element value of 1 when the comparison vs2[i] ≤ vs1[i] is true and writes a mask element value of 0 when the comparison is false.
RULE 3: VMFLE.VV always writes its result to a single mask vector register, and the element layout of that mask register follows the vector register element layout defined for mask vectors.
RULE 4: VMFLE.VV permits the destination mask register to be the same physical register as the source mask register v0.
RULE 5: VMFLE.VV operates under a tail-agnostic policy, meaning that elements beyond the active vector length do not affect the result.
RULE 6: VMFLE.VV raises an invalid-operation exception when either operand of any element is a signaling NaN or a quiet NaN.
RULE 7: When either operand of an element is NaN, VMFLE.VV writes 0 to the corresponding destination mask element.

INSTRUCTION: VMFLT.VF
FORMAT: vmflt.vf vd, vs2, fs1, vm
RULE 1: VMFLT.VF MUST set destination mask element vd[i] to 0 when vs2[i] is not less than rs1, including any case where either operand is NaN.
RULE 2: VMFLT.VF MUST raise the invalid-operation exception whenever a signaling or quiet NaN appears in either operand of the comparison.
RULE 3: VMFLT.VF MUST write a 0 to the destination mask element corresponding to any element for which a NaN is present in either operand.
RULE 4: VMFLT.VF MUST store the resulting mask vector in a single vector register whose element layout follows the definition in Section 31.4.5.
RULE 5: VMFLT.VF MAY include an optional mask operand vm that controls element activity, and VMFLT.VF MUST leave inactive elements unchanged.

INSTRUCTION: VMFLT.VV
FORMAT: vmflt.vv vd, vs2, vs1, vm
RULE 1: VMFLT.VV compares each pair of corresponding elements from source vectors vs2 and vs1 and writes the per-element comparison result to the mask register specified by vd.
RULE 2: VMFLT.VV stores the mask result in a single vector register whose element layout follows the defined layout for mask vectors.
RULE 3: VMFLT.VV always operates under a tail-agnostic policy because it writes to a mask register.
RULE 4: VMFLT.VV raises the invalid-operation exception when any element of either source operand is a signaling NaN or a quiet NaN.
RULE 5: VMFLT.VV writes a zero to a destination mask element when either corresponding source element is NaN.
RULE 6: VMFLT.VV writes a one to a destination mask element when the vs2 element is less than the vs1 element and neither operand is NaN.

INSTRUCTION: VMFNE.VV
FORMAT: vmfne.vv vd, vs2, vs1, vm
RULE 1: VMFNE.VV must write the comparison result to each element of the destination mask register vd, using the mask register vm to control element-wise activity.
RULE 2: VMFNE.VV must set a destination mask element to 1 when the corresponding element of either source vector register (vs2 or vs1) is NaN.
RULE 3: VMFNE.VV must set a destination mask element to 1 when the corresponding elements of the two source vectors are not equal and neither element is NaN.
RULE 4: VMFNE.VV must set a destination mask element to 0 when the corresponding elements of the two source vectors are equal and neither element is NaN.
RULE 5: VMFNE.VV must raise an invalid-operation exception if any source element is a signaling NaN, and must not raise that exception for quiet NaNs or non-NaN values.
RULE 6: VMFNE.VV must store its result mask in a single vector register whose element layout follows the standard vector mask layout.
RULE 7: If VMFNE.VV does not apply a tail‑agnostic policy to elements with index i ≥ VL, then the corresponding bits in the destination mask register are defined by the element‑wise comparison of the source operands (rather than being undefined).

INSTRUCTION: VMIN.VV
FORMAT: vmin.vv vd, vs2, vs1, vm
RULE 1: VMIN.VV computes the element-wise signed integer minimum of the corresponding elements of vs2 and vs1 and writes the result to the corresponding element of vd for each element that is enabled by the mask.
RULE 2: If the vm field is omitted, VMIN.VV writes the result to every element of vd.
RULE 3: When a mask register vm is present, VMIN.VV updates only those elements of vd whose corresponding mask bit is 1, leaving other elements unchanged.

INSTRUCTION: VMIN.VX
FORMAT: vmin.vx vd, vs2, rs1, vm
RULE 1: VMIN.VX MUST be classified as a signed integer minimum operation.
RULE 2: For each active element index i, VMIN.VX MUST compute the signed minimum of the scalar value in rs1 and the element vs2[i] and produce that value as result element i.
RULE 3: VMIN.VX MUST write each result element to the corresponding element of destination vector register vd for all element positions for which the mask vm is true, or for all elements when the mask is omitted.
RULE 4: VMIN.VX MUST leave unchanged any destination element for which the mask vm is false when a mask is provided.
RULE 5: VMIN.VX MUST treat the mask operand vm as optional, and when it is not provided all vector elements are considered active.

INSTRUCTION: VMINU.VV
FORMAT: vminu.vv vd, vs2, vs1, vm
RULE 1: VMINU.VV must compute for each element the smaller of the two unsigned integer values taken from the corresponding elements of source vector registers vs2 and vs1.
RULE 2: VMINU.VV applies the mask register vm to predicate the element-wise operation, updating only those elements for which the corresponding mask bit is true.

INSTRUCTION: VMNAND.MM
FORMAT: vmnand.mm vd, vs2, vs1
RULE 1: VMNAND.MM vd, vs2, vs1 computes each mask element vd.mask[i] as the logical NAND of the corresponding mask elements vs2.mask[i] and vs1.mask[i], i.e., vd.mask[i] = !(vs2.mask[i] && vs1.mask[i]).
RULE 2: VMNAND.MM may write its result to a destination vector register that is the same register as either source vector register.
RULE 3: VMNAND.MM leaves mask elements with indices less than the current vstart unchanged and resets vstart to zero after the instruction completes.
RULE 4: VMNAND.MM is always executed unmasked, therefore no element is considered inactive and any encoding with the vm field equal to zero is reserved.
RULE 5: VMNAND.MM updates mask elements whose indices are greater than or equal to the current vl according to the tail-agnostic policy.
RULE 6: VMNAND.MM operates on a single vector register irrespective of the vlmul setting in vtype.
RULE 7: VMNAND.MM does not modify the vlmul field in vtype.
RULE 8: When the two source operands of VMNAND.MM refer to the same mask register, the instruction produces the logical NOT of that mask register.
RULE 9: When the two source operands of VMNAND.MM refer to different mask registers, the instruction produces the logical NAND of the two source mask registers.

INSTRUCTION: VMNOR.MM
FORMAT: vmnor.mm vd, vs2, vs1
RULE 1: If VMNOR.MM is executed with any source or destination register that is not a mask register (i.e., its elements are wider than one bit), then the instruction raises an illegal‑instruction exception.
RULE 2: VMNOR.MM does not modify the vlmul field.
RULE 3: VMNOR.MM permits the destination register vd to be the same register as either source register vs1 or vs2.
RULE 4: VMNOR.MM leaves mask elements with indices less than vstart unchanged.
RULE 5: VMNOR.MM resets vstart to zero after execution.
RULE 6: VMNOR.MM is always executed unmasked, meaning there are no inactive elements during its operation.
RULE 7: VMNOR.MM treats encodings with the vm field equal to zero as reserved.
RULE 8: VMNOR.MM updates mask elements with indices greater than or equal to vl according to the tail-agnostic policy.

INSTRUCTION: VMOR.MM
FORMAT: vmor.mm vd, vs2, vs1
RULE 1: VMOR.MM does not modify the vlmul architectural register.
RULE 2: For every element index i, VMOR.MM sets vd.mask[i] to the logical OR of vs2.mask[i] and vs1.mask[i].
RULE 3: VMOR.MM leaves mask elements with indices less than the current vstart unchanged and resets vstart to zero after the instruction completes.
RULE 4: VMOR.MM is always executed unmasked, so no elements are inactive during its execution.
RULE 5: If a VMOR.MM instruction is decoded with the vm field equal to zero, then an illegal‑instruction exception is raised.
RULE 6: If VMOR.MM updates a mask element whose index is ≥ vl, then the corresponding mask bit is cleared to 0 in the destination mask register.

INSTRUCTION: VMORN.MM
FORMAT: vmorn.mm vd, vs2, vs1
RULE 1: VMORN.MM computes each mask element of the destination register vd as the logical OR of the corresponding element of source register vs2 and the logical NOT of the corresponding element of source register vs1.
RULE 2: If any element of the mask register supplied to VMORN.MM has bits other than the least‑significant bit set, then VMORN.MM ignores those higher bits and the destination register reflects only the LSB of each element (each element is treated as a single‑bit value).
RULE 3: VMORN.MM always uses a single vector register width regardless of the vlmul setting in vtype, and the execution of VMORN.MM does not change the architectural value of vlmul.
RULE 4: VMORN.MM permits the destination mask register vd to be the same architectural register as either source register vs1 or source register vs2.
RULE 5: VMORN.MM leaves vd.mask[i] unchanged for every element index i that is less than the current value of vstart.
RULE 6: VMORN.MM resets the architectural register vstart to zero after execution.
RULE 7: VMORN.MM is defined only for encodings with the mask bit vm set to one; encodings with vm equal to zero are reserved.
RULE 8: If VMORN.MM is executed, then the destination register contains the elementwise OR result for every element position—no element is masked to zero.
RULE 9: VMORN.MM updates vd.mask[i] for every element index i that is greater than or equal to the current vector length vl according to the tail-agnostic policy, writing the computed logical result to the destination element.
RULE 10: VMORN.MM always applies the NOT operation to the second source operand and ORs the first source operand with that negated value, regardless of which source register appears first in the instruction syntax.

INSTRUCTION: VMSBC.VV
FORMAT: vmsbc.vv vd, vs2, vs1
RULE 1: VMSBC.VV takes a destination vector register vd and source vector registers vs2 and vs1 and performs a vector-vector subtraction without using any borrow-in.
RULE 2: For each element index i, VMSBC.VV sets vd.mask[i] to the borrow-out resulting from subtracting the i-th element of vs1 from the i-th element of vs2.
RULE 3: The borrow-out produced by VMSBC.VV for an element is 1 if and only if the full-precision difference (vs2[i] – vs1[i]) is negative before any truncation, otherwise the borrow-out is 0.
RULE 4: VMSBC.VV always follows a tail-agnostic policy, meaning that tail elements are not written and do not affect the execution of the instruction.
RULE 5: VMSBC.VV operates on and writes back mask bits for all body elements of the vector register group, even when element masking is enabled.
RULE 6: The mask bits written by VMSBC.VV are stored in the mask-register layout of the destination vector register vd.
RULE 7: When the vector mask register vm is set to 1 (unmasked execution), VMSBC.VV does not use any borrow-in and does not read the mask bits of v0.

INSTRUCTION: VMSBC.VVM
FORMAT: vmsbc.vvm vd, vs2, vs1, v0
RULE 1: VMSBC.VVM subtracts each element of source vector register vs1 from the corresponding element of source vector register vs2 and also subtracts the borrow-in bit supplied by the mask field of the implicit v0 register for each element.
RULE 2: VMSBC.VVM must obtain its borrow-in (carry-in) for each element exclusively from the mask bit v0.mask[i] of the implicit v0 register.
RULE 3: VMSBC.VVM writes the borrow-out (carry-out) for each element to the mask field of the destination vector register vd, and the destination register may be any vector register that satisfies the architectural source/destination overlap restrictions.
RULE 4: VMSBC.VVM sets vd.mask[i] to 1 if and only if the untruncated subtraction result vs2[i] – vs1[i] – v0.mask[i] is negative; otherwise vd.mask[i] is set to 0.
RULE 5: VMSBC.VVM is a masked operation with vm = 0, meaning the mask register does not inhibit element processing.
RULE 6: If VMSBC.VVM is executed, then for every element index i ≥ VL the corresponding bit in the destination mask register is cleared to 0 (the tail bits are zero).

INSTRUCTION: VMSBC.VX
FORMAT: vmsbc.vx vd, vs2, rs1
RULE 1: VMSBC.VX MUST read the scalar integer value from integer register rs1, sign-extend it to the element width, and subtract it from each element of source vector register vs2 to compute a borrow output for each element.
RULE 2: VMSBC.VX MUST write the borrow output for element i as a single-bit mask value to the corresponding mask bit vd.mask[i] of the destination vector register vd.
RULE 3: VMSBC.VX defines the borrow output to be 1 if and only if the untruncated difference vs2[i] − x[rs1] is negative; otherwise the borrow output is 0.
RULE 4: VMSBC.VX MUST write mask bits for all body elements of the destination register even if the operation is masked.
RULE 5: VMSBC.VX produces a mask register as its architectural result and does NOT produce any SEW-width data result.
RULE 6: VMSBC.VX MUST store the mask bits in the destination register using the mask-register layout defined for mask registers.

INSTRUCTION: VMSBC.VXM
FORMAT: vmsbc.vxm vd, vs2, rs1, v0
RULE 1: VMSBC.VXM performs a vector-scalar masked subtraction that writes the borrow-out to the mask field of the destination vector register vd.
RULE 2: For each element index i within the active vector length, VMSBC.VXM computes difference = vs2[i] – x[rs1] – v0.mask[i].
RULE 3: VMSBC.VXM sets vd.mask[i] to 1 if the pre-truncation difference for element i is negative, otherwise it sets vd.mask[i] to 0.
RULE 4: The borrow-in used by VMSBC.VXM for each element i is taken from the mask bit v0.mask[i] of the implicit v0 register.
RULE 5: The borrow-out produced by VMSBC.VXM for each element i is stored as the mask bit vd.mask[i] of the destination register.
RULE 6: If VMSBC.VXM is executed, then the mask register bits beyond the current VL are undefined and may read as either 0 or 1.
RULE 7: If any source operand of VMSBC.VXM overlaps the destination mask register, then the instruction raises an illegal‑instruction exception.

INSTRUCTION: VMSBF.M
FORMAT: vmsbf.m vd, vs2, vm
RULE 1: VMSBF.M always treats mask destination tail elements as tail-agnostic regardless of the setting of the vta bit.
RULE 2: If VMSBF.M is executed with a source operand that is not a mask register, or with a destination operand that is not a mask register, then it raises an illegal‑instruction exception; otherwise, the destination mask register is written with the computed result.
RULE 3: VMSBF.M writes a 1 to each active destination mask element that precedes the first active source mask element containing a 1, and writes a 0 to that first 1 element and to all subsequent active destination elements.
RULE 4: If no active source mask element contains a 1, VMSBF.M writes a 1 to every active destination mask element.
RULE 5: When VMSBF.M causes a trap, the vstart field in the trap record is reported as 0.
RULE 6: VMSBF.M raises an illegal-instruction exception if it is executed with vstart not equal to 0.
RULE 7: If the destination register is the same as the source register, or (when masking is active) the same as mask register v0, then VMSBF.M raises an illegal‑instruction exception.
RULE 8: VMSBF.M vd, vs2 MUST set each mask bit in the destination register vd to true for vector element positions that are earlier than the position of the first true mask bit in the source register vs2, and to false for that position and all later positions.
RULE 9: When the source mask register vs2 is generated by vmseq.vi to flag zero-byte flags, VMSBF.M vd, vs2 produces a destination mask vd that has true bits for all elements preceding the first zero-byte flag in vs2 and false bits for the element containing the zero-byte flag and all subsequent elements.

INSTRUCTION: VMSEQ.VI
FORMAT: vmseq.vi vd, vs2, simm5, vm
RULE 1: VMSEQ.VI writes a 1 to each element of the destination mask register if the element-wise comparison of the source vector register vs2 with the immediate value evaluates to true, and writes a 0 otherwise.
RULE 2: If a VMSEQ.VI instruction is executed, then reading the mask register bits for element indices ≥ VL yields undefined values (the architecture does not define their contents).
RULE 3: When the destination mask register of VMSEQ.VI is v0, each resulting mask element equals the logical AND of the newly computed comparison result and the original value of the corresponding element in v0, implementing a mask-undisturbed policy.

INSTRUCTION: VMSEQ.VV
FORMAT: vmseq.vv vd, vs2, vs1, vm
RULE 1: VMSEQ.VV MUST write a 1 to each destination mask-register element when the element-wise equality comparison evaluates to true and MUST write a 0 to each destination mask-register element when the comparison evaluates to false.
RULE 2: VMSEQ.VV writes its result to a single vector register that holds the destination mask vector.
RULE 3: When the destination register of VMSEQ.VV is v0, the instruction ANDs the newly computed mask bits with the existing bits of v0, thereby preserving bits that were previously zero (mask-undisturbed policy).
RULE 4: VMSEQ.VV always writes all elements of the destination mask register and therefore operates under a tail-agnostic policy.

INSTRUCTION: VMSEQ.VX
FORMAT: vmseq.vx vd, vs2, rs1, vm
RULE 1: VMSEQ.VX must write a 1 to each element of the destination mask register vd for which the corresponding element of source vector register vs2 is equal to the scalar integer in integer register rs1, and must write a 0 to each element for which the comparison is false.
RULE 2: VMSEQ.VX must store the result mask in a single vector register whose element layout follows the mask vector layout defined for vector registers.
RULE 3: If a VMSEQ.VX instruction is executed with a given vector length VL, then the architectural state of every vector element whose index i ≥ VL is unspecified; the implementation may leave those tail elements unchanged and is not required to write them.
RULE 4: When the destination register vd of VMSEQ.VX is the mask register v0, VMSEQ.VX must combine the newly computed mask with the prior contents of v0 by performing a logical AND, thereby preserving mask bits that are zero (mask-undisturbed behavior).

INSTRUCTION: VMSGT.VI
FORMAT: vmsgt.vi vd, vs2, simm5, vm
RULE 1: VMSGT.VI writes a mask element value of 1 to each element of the destination mask register when the signed value in the corresponding element of the source vector register is greater than the signed immediate operand, and writes a mask element value of 0 otherwise.
RULE 2: VMSGT.VI stores the destination mask vector in a single vector register whose element layout follows the layout defined in Section 31.4.5.
RULE 3: VMSGT.VI permits the destination mask register to be the same register as the source vector mask register v0.
RULE 4: VMSGT.VI evaluates a signed-greater-than comparison between each element of the source vector register and the signed immediate operand.
RULE 5: If a VMSGT.VI instruction includes a mask operand vm and a mask bit for element i is 0, then after execution the i‑th element of the destination register retains its pre‑instruction value (the element is not written); if the mask bit for element i is 1, then the i‑th element contains the result of the signed‑greater‑than comparison; if the instruction omits vm, all elements behave as if their mask bits were 1.
RULE 6: VMSGT.VI operates under a tail-agnostic policy, so elements beyond the current vector length do not affect architectural state.

INSTRUCTION: VMSGT.VX
FORMAT: vmsgt.vx vd, vs2, rs1, vm
RULE 1: VMSGT.VX writes a 1 to each destination mask register element when the signed greater-than comparison between the corresponding element of the source vector register vs2 and the scalar integer in rs1 evaluates true, and writes a 0 otherwise.
RULE 2: VMSGT.VX stores its result mask elements in a single destination vector register whose element layout follows the standard vector mask element layout.

INSTRUCTION: VMSGTU.VX
FORMAT: vmsgtu.vx vd, vs2, rs1, vm
RULE 1: VMSGTU.VX writes a mask element value of 1 to each destination mask register element when the unsigned-greater-than comparison between the corresponding element of vs2 and the scalar integer rs1 evaluates to true, and writes 0 otherwise.
RULE 2: VMSGTU.VX stores its destination mask vector in a single vector register, and that register may be the same register as the source vector mask register v0.
RULE 3: VMSGTU.VX performs an unsigned-greater-than comparison of each element of the source vector register vs2 against the scalar integer register rs1.
RULE 4: If software expects mask register elements beyond the effective vector length to have a defined value after executing VMSGTU.VX, then reading those elements may return any value because the architecture does not define their state.
RULE 5: When the destination register of VMSGTU.VX is v0, the instruction updates only those mask elements enabled by the active mask, leaving disabled elements unchanged, thereby implementing mask-undisturbed behavior.

INSTRUCTION: VMSIF.M
FORMAT: vmsif.m vd, vs2, vm
RULE 1: If a VMSIF.M instruction writes a tail element of the mask result to a value that is not the original value, not all‑ones, and not a value permitted by the agnostic policy, then that tail element’s architectural value is undefined (the mask register contains an unpredictable value).
RULE 2: VMSIF.M sets each element in the destination mask register up to and including the first element whose corresponding source mask bit is set.
RULE 3: If VMSIF.M is executed, then after execution the bits of the destination mask register beyond the current VL are cleared to 0 (tail‑agnostic), observable by reading the register’s value.
RULE 4: If VMSIF.M causes a trap, the architectural register vstart must be 0 at trap entry.
RULE 5: VMSIF.M must raise an illegal-instruction exception when executed with vstart not equal to 0.
RULE 6: VMSIF.M v0, v1 sets each element of the destination mask v0 to true for every element from the start of the vector through the first element whose corresponding element in the source mask v1 is true, and clears all subsequent elements.
RULE 7: When the source mask v1 is produced by a preceding vmseq.vi that flags zero-byte, VMSIF.M v0, v1 creates a destination mask v0 that is true for all elements up to and including the first zero-byte element indicated by v1.

INSTRUCTION: VMSLE.VI
FORMAT: vmsle.vi vd, vs2, simm5, vm
RULE 1: VMSLE.VI writes a 1 to each element of the destination mask register when the signed less-than-or-equal comparison of the corresponding source vector element with the sign-extended immediate operand evaluates true, and writes a 0 otherwise.
RULE 2: VMSLE.VI stores the resulting mask vector in a single vector register using the standard vector element layout.
RULE 3: VMSLE.VI may use the same vector register as both the destination mask register and the source mask register (v0).
RULE 4: VMSLE.VI's 5-bit immediate field is sign-extended and the immediate value must be in the range –16 to +15.

INSTRUCTION: VMSLE.VV
FORMAT: vmsle.vv vd, vs2, vs1, vm
RULE 1: VMSLE.VV writes a 1 to each destination mask register element vd[i] when the signed integer comparison vs2[i] ≤ vs1[i] evaluates true for that element, and writes a 0 to vd[i] when the comparison evaluates false.
RULE 2: VMSLE.VV always operates under a tail-agnostic policy, meaning that elements beyond the current vector length are treated as inactive and do not affect the result.
RULE 3: When the destination register of VMSLE.VV is v0, the instruction updates each active element by AND-ing the newly computed mask bit with the existing bit in v0, preserving bits of v0 that are masked off according to the mask-undisturbed policy.
RULE 4: VMSLE.VV does not generate any exceptions or require special permissions beyond those defined for generic integer compare instructions.

INSTRUCTION: VMSLEU.VI
FORMAT: vmsleu.vi vd, vs2, simm5, vm
RULE 1: VMSLEU.VI writes a 1 to each element of the destination mask register when the unsigned less-or-equal comparison between the corresponding element of the source vector register and the immediate evaluates true, and writes a 0 otherwise.
RULE 2: VMSLEU.VI stores its result mask in a single vector register whose element layout follows the standard vector element layout.
RULE 3: VMSLEU.VI immediate operand must be a 5-bit signed value in the range 0 to 15; encodings with an immediate outside this range are reserved.
RULE 4: For an allowed immediate N (0 ≤ N ≤ 15), VMSLEU.VI performs the same comparison as VMSLTU.VI with immediate N+1, giving an effective VMSLTU.VI range of 1 to 16.
RULE 5: When the sign-extended 5-bit immediate has its high bit set, VMSLEU.VI supports unsigned immediate values in the range (2 × SEW − 16) to (2 × SEW − 1), enabling comparisons against those unsigned values.
RULE 6: If VMSLTU.VI is executed with an unsigned immediate whose value is not in the range (2 × SEW − 15) to (2 × SEW) while the high bit of the immediate is set, then the instruction raises an illegal‑instruction exception (mcause set to the illegal‑instruction code).
RULE 7: VMSLEU.VI operates under a tail-agnostic mask policy, so elements beyond the current vector length are treated as masked off.
RULE 8: If the destination register of VMSLEU.VI is v0, the instruction updates the mask by ANDing the newly computed mask bits with the existing mask bits, preserving other bits under a mask-undisturbed policy.

INSTRUCTION: VMSLEU.VV
FORMAT: vmsleu.vv vd, vs2, vs1, vm
RULE 1: VMSLEU.VV writes a mask element value of 1 to the destination register for each element index i when the unsigned integer value in source vector register vs2 at element i is less than or equal to the unsigned integer value in source vector register vs1 at element i, and writes a mask element value of 0 otherwise.
RULE 2: VMSLEU.VV uses the elements of source vector register vs2 as the left operand and the elements of source vector register vs1 as the right operand in the unsigned less-or-equal comparison.
RULE 3: VMSLEU.VV stores the resulting mask vector in a single destination vector register.
RULE 4: VMSLEU.VV permits the destination mask register to be the same register as source mask register v0.
RULE 5: If the effective vector length (VL) is less than the maximum vector length, then after executing VMSLEU.VV the mask bits for all element positions i ≥ VL must retain the same values they held before the instruction.
RULE 6: When the destination register of VMSLEU.VV is v0, the instruction updates only those mask bits that are already set, leaving mask bits that are clear unchanged (mask-undisturbed policy).

INSTRUCTION: VMSLEU.VX
FORMAT: vmsleu.vx vd, vs2, rs1, vm
RULE 1: VMSLEU.VX writes a 1 to each element of the destination mask register element when the unsigned comparison of the corresponding element of source vector register vs2 to the scalar integer register rs1 evaluates true, and writes a 0 otherwise.
RULE 2: VMSLEU.VX stores the destination mask vector in a single vector register whose element layout follows the mask vector element format.
RULE 3: VMSLEU.VX may use the same vector register for both the destination mask and the source vector mask register (v0).
RULE 4: VMSLEU.VX always operates under a tail-agnostic policy because it writes a mask register.
RULE 5: If the destination register of VMSLEU.VX is v0, the instruction effectively ANDs the newly generated mask with the existing mask bits, preserving previously set mask bits (mask-undisturbed policy).
RULE 6: VMSLEU.VX implements the unsigned "≤" comparison of each element of source vector register vs2 against scalar integer register rs1 and produces the resulting mask in the destination register.

INSTRUCTION: VMSLT.VV
FORMAT: vmslt.vv vd, vs2, vs1, vm
RULE 1: VMSLT.VV writes a 1 to each element of the destination mask register when the signed less-than comparison between the corresponding elements of the two source vector registers evaluates to true, and writes a 0 otherwise.
RULE 2: VMSLT.VV stores its result mask in a single vector register, never spanning multiple registers.
RULE 3: VMSLT.VV permits the destination mask register to be the same register used as a source mask register (v0).
RULE 4: VMSLT.VV performs the signed less-than comparison as "source-register-1 element < source-register-2 element" for each element position.
RULE 5: VMSLT.VV respects the mask operand vm, updating only those elements for which the mask bit is true and leaving other elements unchanged.
RULE 6: When the destination register of VMSLT.VV is v0, the instruction combines the newly computed mask with the existing mask in v0 using a logical AND, so that each element of v0 becomes 1 only if the prior mask element was 1 and the signed less-than comparison is true.

INSTRUCTION: VMSLT.VX
FORMAT: vmslt.vx vd, vs2, rs1, vm
RULE 1: VMSLT.VX must perform a signed less-than comparison between each element of the source vector register vs2 and the scalar integer register rs1.
RULE 2: VMSLT.VX must write a mask element value of 1 to the destination mask register for each element where the signed comparison evaluates to true, and a value of 0 otherwise.
RULE 3: VMSLT.VX stores the mask result in a single vector register whose element layout follows the standard mask vector layout.
RULE 4: VMSLT.VX may use the same register for the destination mask and the source mask register, allowing vd to be v0.
RULE 5: VMSLT.VX respects an optional mask operand vm by updating destination mask elements only for elements where vm is true and leaving other elements unchanged.
RULE 6: When the destination register of VMSLT.VX is v0, the instruction must apply a mask-undisturbed policy, effectively ANDing the newly computed mask with the existing bits of v0 for elements where the mask vm is false.

INSTRUCTION: VMSLTU.VV
FORMAT: vmsltu.vv vd, vs2, vs1, vm
RULE 1: VMSLTU.VV compares each element i of the source vector register vs2 with the corresponding element i of the source vector register vs1 as unsigned integers and writes the value 1 to the destination mask register element i if the comparison vs2[i] < vs1[i] is true, otherwise writes the value 0.
RULE 2: VMSLTU.VV writes a result to destination mask element i only when the corresponding mask bit i in the mask register vm is true; when the mask bit i is false, VMSLTU.VV leaves destination mask element i unchanged.
RULE 3: VMSLTU.VV permits the destination mask register vd to be the same physical register as the mask source register v0.
RULE 4: When the destination mask register vd of VMSLTU.VV is v0, the instruction logically ANDs each computed result bit with the prior value of v0, thereby preserving any mask bits that are not set by the unsigned comparison.

INSTRUCTION: VMSLTU.VX
FORMAT: vmsltu.vx vd, vs2, rs1, vm
RULE 1: VMSLTU.VX performs an unsigned less-than comparison between each element of source vector register vs2 and the scalar integer register rs1, writing 1 to the corresponding element of destination mask register vd when the comparison is true and 0 otherwise.
RULE 2: The destination mask vector produced by VMSLTU.VX is stored in a single vector register whose element layout follows the definition given in Section 31.4.5.
RULE 3: VMSLTU.VX may use the same vector register for the destination mask register vd and the source vector register vs2 (i.e., register overlap is permitted).
RULE 4: If any permission check or exception condition that would cause VMSLT.VX or VMSLEU.VX to raise an exception is violated when executing VMSLTU.VX, then VMSLTU.VX raises the same exception (e.g., an illegal‑instruction exception with mcause set accordingly) and exhibits the same architectural effects as those instructions.

INSTRUCTION: VMSNE.VI
FORMAT: vmsne.vi vd, vs2, simm5, vm
RULE 1: VMSNE.VI performs a not-equal comparison between each element of source vector register vs2 and the sign-extended immediate operand.
RULE 2: VMSNE.VI must write a 1 to each element of the destination mask register when the corresponding not-equal comparison is true and must write a 0 when the comparison is false.
RULE 3: VMSNE.VI stores its result mask vector in a single vector register.
RULE 4: VMSNE.VI may use the same vector register (v0) for both the destination mask register and the source mask register.
RULE 5: VMSNE.VI operates under a tail-agnostic policy, meaning that elements beyond the current vector length do not affect the architectural result.
RULE 6: If VMSNE.VI's destination register is v0, the instruction updates only the mask elements that are set, preserving mask elements that are cleared (mask-undisturbed policy).

INSTRUCTION: VMSNE.VV
FORMAT: vmsne.vv vd, vs2, vs1, vm
RULE 1: VMSNE.VV writes a 1 to each element of its destination mask register when the integer not-equal comparison for that element evaluates true, and writes a 0 otherwise.
RULE 2: VMSNE.VV stores its result mask in a single vector register, and the element layout of that mask follows the standard vector element width definition.
RULE 3: If VMSNE.VV writes a mask register, then for every element index i ≥ VL the resulting mask bit is undefined (the hardware may leave the previous mask bit unchanged), i.e., the tail elements of the destination mask register are not required to be cleared.
RULE 4: When VMSNE.VV writes its result to the mask register v0, elements that are not written retain their previous mask bits, effectively performing an AND with the existing mask under a mask-undisturbed policy.

INSTRUCTION: VMSNE.VX
FORMAT: vmsne.vx vd, vs2, rs1, vm
RULE 1: VMSNE.VX must compare each active element of source vector register vs2 with the integer value in scalar register rs1 and determine true when the two values are not equal.
RULE 2: VMSNE.VX must write the integer 1 to the corresponding element of destination mask register vd when the not-equal comparison for that element is true, and must write the integer 0 when the comparison is false.
RULE 3: VMSNE.VX must store the destination mask vector in a single vector register whose element layout follows the mask-register layout defined for vector masks.
RULE 4: If the destination register vd is v0, VMSNE.VX must update each element of v0 by logically ANDing the previously stored mask bit with the newly computed not-equal result, effectively preserving mask bits that were previously zero.
RULE 5: VMSNE.VX must respect the optional mask operand vm; for any element whose corresponding mask bit in vm is zero, VMSNE.VX must leave the destination element unchanged.

INSTRUCTION: VMSOF.M
FORMAT: vmsof.m vd, vs2, vm
RULE 1: In VMSOF.M, mask-destination tail elements are always treated as tail-agnostic regardless of the vta setting.
RULE 2: If VMSOF.M writes a tail element with a value that is not among the permitted values for that element, then an illegal‑instruction exception is raised.
RULE 3: In VMSOF.M, mask tail elements are always treated as agnostic, allowing them to be written at bit granularity without architectural significance.
RULE 4: When a trap is taken on VMSOF.M, the architectural vstart field is reported as zero.
RULE 5: VMSOF.M raises an illegal-instruction exception when the architectural vstart field is non-zero.
RULE 6: If the source mask register contains at least one set bit, VMSOF.M sets only the first (lowest-index) element of the destination mask register to one and clears all other elements; if the source mask register contains no set bits, VMSOF.M clears all elements of the destination mask register.

INSTRUCTION: VMUL.VV
FORMAT: vmul.vv vd, vs2, vs1, vm
RULE 1: If VMUL.VV is executed with element width SEW, then for each active element the destination register element contains the signed 2 × SEW‑bit product of the two source SEW‑bit operands.
RULE 2: VMUL.VV must write the low SEW-bit half of each 2 × SEW-bit product to the corresponding element of the destination vector register.
RULE 3: VMUL.VV must select elements for operation according to the mask register vm when it is present, and must operate on all elements when the mask register is not present.
RULE 4: VMUL.VV must not apply any rounding, scaling, or saturation to the multiplication result.
RULE 5: VMUL.VV must not use any rounding-mode fields in its operation.

INSTRUCTION: VMUL.VX
FORMAT: vmul.vx vd, vs2, rs1, vm
RULE 1: VMUL.VX is a vector-scalar signed multiply instruction that computes a signed SEW-bit × SEW-bit product for each element of the source vector register vs2 with the SEW-bit scalar contained in integer register rs1.
RULE 2: VMUL.VX returns one half of the 2 × SEW-bit product in the SEW-bit-wide destination register vd.
RULE 3: VMUL.VX writes the low SEW-bit word of each 2 × SEW-bit product into the corresponding element of the destination vector register vd.

INSTRUCTION: VMULH.VV
FORMAT: vmulh.vv vd, vs2, vs1, vm
RULE 1: VMULH.VV MUST multiply each SEW-bit signed element of source vector register vs2 by the corresponding SEW-bit signed element of source vector register vs1 to produce a 2 × SEW-bit signed product and MUST write the upper SEW bits of each product to the corresponding element of the destination vector register vd.
RULE 2: The value written by VMULH.VV to each element of vd is SEW-bit-wide and MUST contain exactly the high half of the 2 × SEW-bit signed product of the paired source elements.
RULE 3: VMULH.VV MUST perform a simple fractional multiply with no scaling, rounding, or saturation applied to the high-half result.
RULE 4: VMULH.VV MUST NOT signal an overflow condition because the instruction discards the low half of the product.
RULE 5: VMULH.VV shares the same high-word-write behavior as the scalar-variant vmulh.vx, meaning both return the high bits of a signed multiplication.
RULE 6: VMULH.VV returns the high word of the product of each pair of corresponding elements of its two source vectors.
RULE 7: VMULH.VV is not defined for element width 64 bits in any Zve64* extension.
RULE 8: Any trap taken by VMULH.VV in a Zve* extension MUST be a precise trap.

INSTRUCTION: VMULH.VX
FORMAT: vmulh.vx vd, vs2, rs1, vm
RULE 1: VMULH.VX must multiply each element of source vector register vs2 by the signed integer scalar in rs1, treating both operands as SEW-bit signed values.
RULE 2: VMULH.VX must compute a 2 × SEW-bit product for each element and must write the most-significant SEW bits of that product to the corresponding element of destination vector register vd.
RULE 3: VMULH.VX must apply the vector mask vm so that for each element whose mask bit is clear the destination element is left unchanged, and for each element whose mask bit is set the high-SEW-bit result is written to vd.
RULE 4: VMULH.VX must perform a simple fractional signed multiply without any scaling, rounding, or saturation of the result.
RULE 5: VMULH.VX belongs to the mulh class and therefore must write the high word of the product to the destination register rather than the low word.
RULE 6: VMULH.VX computes the product of each vector element and the scalar operand and writes the high word of each product to the corresponding destination vector element.
RULE 7: If a trap condition occurs while executing VMULH.VX, then a precise trap is taken: mcause is written with the corresponding exception code, mepc is set to the address of the VMULH.VX instruction, and the destination register is not updated.

INSTRUCTION: VMULHSU.VV
FORMAT: vmulhsu.vv vd, vs2, vs1, vm
RULE 1: If VMULHSU.VV is executed, then for each element i the destination register vd[i] contains the upper SEW bits (bits [2·SEW‑1:SEW]) of the 2·SEW‑bit product of vs2[i] interpreted as a signed integer and vs1[i] interpreted as an unsigned integer.
RULE 2: VMULHSU.VV writes the most-significant SEW-bit half of each 2 × SEW-bit product into the corresponding element of the destination vector register vd.
RULE 3: VMULHSU.VV does not apply any scaling, rounding, or saturation to the multiplication result; it returns the high bits directly.
RULE 4: VMULHSU.VV never signals overflow because the operation returns the high half of the product without overflow detection.
RULE 5: VMULHSU.VV returns the high word of the product of each pair of vector elements.
RULE 6: If VMULHSU.VV is executed with a 64‑bit element width in any Zve64* configuration, then it raises an illegal‑instruction exception (mcause = 2).
RULE 7: Any trap taken by VMULHSU.VV is a precise trap.

INSTRUCTION: VMULHSU.VX
FORMAT: vmulhsu.vx vd, vs2, rs1, vm
RULE 1: If a VMULHSU.VX instruction is executed, then for each element i the destination vector element vd[i] contains the upper SEW bits of the 2 × SEW‑bit product of the signed SEW‑bit value in vs2[i] and the unsigned SEW‑bit value in scalar register rs1.
RULE 2: VMULHSU.VX writes the upper SEW-bit half of each 2*SEW-bit product into the corresponding element of the destination vector register vd.
RULE 3: VMULHSU.VX performs a simple fractional multiply with no option to scale, round, or saturate the result.
RULE 4: VMULHSU.VX never raises an overflow condition because overflow cannot occur in this operation.
RULE 5: In Zve64* extensions, the VMULHSU.VX instruction is not defined for element width EEW = 64.
RULE 6: If any Zve*‑defined trap condition (such as an illegal‑instruction fault, misaligned memory access, or other exception) occurs while executing VMULHSU.VX, then the processor raises the corresponding exception, sets mcause to the appropriate trap code, writes the faulting PC to mepc, and transfers control to the trap handler according to the Zve* extensions’ trap model.

INSTRUCTION: VMULHU.VV
FORMAT: vmulhu.vv vd, vs2, vs1, vm
RULE 1: VMULHU.VV multiplies each SEW-bit element of source vector vs2 with the corresponding SEW-bit element of source vector vs1 as unsigned integers, generates a 2 × SEW-bit product, and writes the high SEW-bit portion of each product to the corresponding element of destination vector vd.
RULE 2: The value written by VMULHU.VV for each element is the most-significant SEW bits of the 2 × SEW-bit product of the unsigned multiplication.
RULE 3: VMULHU.VV performs a simple fractional multiply and does not apply any scaling, rounding, or saturation to the result.
RULE 4: VMULHU.VV never produces an overflow condition, and therefore no overflow exception or flag is generated by the instruction.
RULE 5: If vmulhu.vv vd, vs2, vs1, vm is executed, then for each active element i the destination register vd[i] contains the high half of the product of the corresponding elements vs2[i] × vs1[i], and any element of vd that is inactive under the mask vm retains its previous value.
RULE 6: In the Zve64x, Zve64f, and Zve64d extensions, VMULHU.VV MUST NOT be available for EEW = 64.
RULE 7: Any trap generated by VMULHU.VV is a precise trap.

INSTRUCTION: VMULHU.VX
FORMAT: vmulhu.vx vd, vs2, rs1, vm
RULE 1: If VMULHU.VX does not compute the unsigned SEW‑bit × SEW‑bit product for each source element and the scalar, then the destination register’s elements will not contain the upper SEW bits of the 2 × SEW‑bit product (i.e., the expected high‑half result will be incorrect).
RULE 2: VMULHU.VX must write the upper SEW-bit half of each 2 × SEW-bit product into the corresponding element of the destination vector register.
RULE 3: VMULHU.VX provides no option to scale, round, or saturate the multiplication result and returns the high bits directly.
RULE 4: VMULHU.VX must never raise an overflow exception because overflow is not possible for this unsigned high-multiply operation.
RULE 5: VMULHU.VX returns the high word of the product of each element of the source vector and the scalar integer operand.
RULE 6: In the Zve64x extension, VMULHU.VX is not available when the element width (EEW) is 64 bits.
RULE 7: In the Zve64f extension, VMULHU.VX is not available when the element width (EEW) is 64 bits.
RULE 8: In the Zve64d extension, VMULHU.VX is available for element widths of 8, 16, and 32 bits.
RULE 9: In the Zve32x extension, VMULHU.VX is supported for element widths of 8, 16, and 32 bits.

INSTRUCTION: VMV1R.V
FORMAT: vmv1r.v vd, vs2
RULE 1: VMV1R.V copies all VLEN bits from source vector register vs2 to destination vector register vd, behaving as if EEW equals SEW, EMUL equals NREG, and effective length evl equals EMUL × VLEN/SEW with NREG set to 1.
RULE 2: The usual property that no elements are written when vstart ≥ vl does not apply to VMV1R.V.
RULE 3: VMV1R.V is not reserved when vstart exceeds the largest element index for the current vtype setting.
RULE 4: VMV1R.V is reserved if vstart ≥ evl.
RULE 5: When vd is equal to vs2, VMV1R.V does not change any vector register state.
RULE 6: For VMV1R.V the immediate field simm[2:0] must be 0, representing NREG = 1.
RULE 7: Any encoding of VMV1R.V with simm[2:0] ≠ 0 is reserved.
RULE 8: If VMV1R.V is executed with vm ≠ 1, then the instruction raises an illegal‑instruction exception (mcause set to the illegal‑instruction code).

INSTRUCTION: VMV4R.V
FORMAT: vmv4r.v vd, vs2
RULE 1: VMV4R.V copies the entire contents of each whole vector register, i.e., all VLEN bits, and can copy whole vector register groups.
RULE 2: If the nr field of a VMV4R.V instruction is not equal to 4, then executing the instruction raises an illegal‑instruction exception.
RULE 3: VMV4R.V operates as if EEW = SEW, EMUL = NREG, and its effective length evl is calculated as EMUL × VLEN / SEW.
RULE 4: VMV4R.V does not observe the usual property that no elements are written when vstart ≥ vl.
RULE 5: VMV4R.V does not observe the usual property that the instruction is reserved when vstart exceeds the largest element index for the current vtype setting.
RULE 6: VMV4R.V is reserved if vstart ≥ evl.
RULE 7: VMV4R.V does not modify any vector register state when the destination register vd is the same as the source register vs2.
RULE 8: VMV4R.V requires that the low three bits of the immediate field (simm[2:0]) equal NREG-1, therefore for VMV4R.V simm[2:0] must be 3.
RULE 9: VMV4R.V must use an NREG value of 1, 2, 4, or 8, and any encoding with simm[4:0] other than 0, 1, 3, or 7 is reserved.
RULE 10: VMV4R.V requires source and destination vector register numbers to be aligned to the vector register group size, and any encoding with misaligned register numbers is reserved.

INSTRUCTION: VMV8R.V
FORMAT: vmv8r.v vd, vs2
RULE 1: VMV8R.V copies all VLEN bits of each of eight consecutive source vector registers to the corresponding eight consecutive destination vector registers.
RULE 2: VMV8R.V operates with element width equal to SEW, an EMUL field equal to 8, and an effective vector length evl equal to EMUL multiplied by VLEN divided by SEW.
RULE 3: VMV8R.V does not modify any architectural state when the destination register number equals the source register number.
RULE 4: VMV8R.V requires that the source and destination register numbers be aligned on an eight-register boundary appropriate for the register group size; encodings that violate this alignment are reserved.
RULE 5: VMV8R.V encodings whose low three bits of the immediate field (simm[2:0]) do not equal 7 (i.e., NREG-1) are reserved.

INSTRUCTION: VMV.S.X
FORMAT: vmv.s.x vd, rs1
RULE 1: VMV.S.X copies the scalar integer register to element 0 of the destination vector register.
RULE 2: If SEW < XLEN, VMV.S.X copies the least-significant bits of the scalar integer register to element 0 and ignores the upper XLEN-SEW bits.
RULE 3: If SEW > XLEN, VMV.S.X sign-extends the scalar integer register value to SEW bits before writing to element 0.
RULE 4: For each element with index greater than 0 and less than VLEN/SEW, VMV.S.X treats the element as a tail element according to the current tail-agnostic or tail-undisturbed policy.
RULE 5: If vstart ≥ vl, VMV.S.X performs no operation and does not update the destination register.
RULE 6: When vl = 0, VMV.S.X does not update any elements in the destination vector register group regardless of the value of vstart.

INSTRUCTION: VMV.V.I
FORMAT: vmv.v.i vd, simm5
RULE 1: The VMV.V.I instruction must write the specified immediate value to each element of the destination vector register.
RULE 2: When the immediate value in a VMV.V.I instruction is zero, the destination vector register must contain zero in every element after execution.

INSTRUCTION: VMV.X.S
FORMAT: vmv.x.s rd, vs2
RULE 1: VMV.X.S transfers a single scalar value between an integer x register and element 0 of a vector register while ignoring LMUL and vector register groups.
RULE 2: VMV.X.S copies the SEW-wide element at index 0 of the source vector register into the destination integer register.
RULE 3: If SEW > XLEN, VMV.X.S transfers only the least-significant XLEN bits of the source element and discards the upper SEW-XLEN bits.
RULE 4: If SEW < XLEN, VMV.X.S sign-extends the source element to XLEN bits before writing it to the integer register.
RULE 5: VMV.X.S performs its copy operation even when vl = 0.

INSTRUCTION: VMXNOR.MM
FORMAT: vmxnor.mm vd, vs2, vs1
RULE 1: VMXNOR.MM computes each mask element as the logical NOT of the exclusive-OR of the corresponding mask elements of the two source registers, storing the result in the destination mask register.
RULE 2: VMXNOR.MM permits the destination mask register to be the same register as either source mask register.
RULE 3: VMXNOR.MM operates on a single vector register irrespective of the vlmul field setting in vtype.
RULE 4: VMXNOR.MM does not change the architectural register vlmul.
RULE 5: During execution of VMXNOR.MM, mask elements with indices less than vstart remain unchanged and vstart is reset to zero after the instruction finishes.
RULE 6: If a VMXNOR.MM instruction is encoded with vm=0, then it raises an illegal‑instruction exception; otherwise the instruction is always unmasked, so no elements are inactive and the destination register contains the element‑wise XNOR of the source registers for every vector element.
RULE 7: If a mask element index i is ≥ vl for a VMXNOR.MM instruction, then after execution the destination mask register’s element i is written (its value is the result of the operation, not the original value).
RULE 8: The pseudoinstruction vmset.m vd is defined as an alias for VMXNOR.MM vd, vd, vd, which sets all mask bits in vd to 1.

INSTRUCTION: VMXOR.MM
FORMAT: vmxor.mm vd, vs2, vs1
RULE 1: The VMXOR.MM instruction performs a bitwise exclusive-OR of the two source mask registers and writes the result to the destination mask register.
RULE 2: The VMXOR.MM instruction always uses the same register for its destination operand and its first source operand.
RULE 3: VMXOR.MM computes each mask element as the exclusive-or of the corresponding mask elements of the two source mask registers, storing the result in the destination mask register (vd.mask[i] = vs2.mask[i] XOR vs1.mask[i]).
RULE 4: VMXOR.MM operates on mask registers, each mask element being a single bit, and therefore the instruction always reads and writes a single vector register irrespective of the vlmul setting in vtype.
RULE 5: VMXOR.MM does not modify the architectural value of the vlmul field in vtype.
RULE 6: VMXOR.MM permits the destination mask register to be the same physical register as either source mask register.
RULE 7: For VMXOR.MM, mask elements whose indices are less than the current value of vstart are left unchanged by the instruction.
RULE 8: After completing execution of VMXOR.MM, the architectural register vstart is reset to zero.
RULE 9: If VMXOR.MM is executed, then all vector elements are treated as active regardless of any mask register, and the destination mask register contains the element‑wise XOR of the two source mask registers for every element.
RULE 10: VMXOR.MM updates mask elements with indices greater than or equal to the current vector length (vl) according to the tail-agnostic policy, meaning the tail elements are written regardless of their previous values.
RULE 11: When VMXOR.MM is used with both source registers identical to the destination register (vd, vd, vd), the resulting destination mask register is cleared, i.e., all mask bits become zero.

INSTRUCTION: VNCLIP.WI
FORMAT: vnclip.wi vd, vs2, zimm5, vm
RULE 1: VNCLIP.WI sets the vxsat bit in the vxsat register if any destination element is saturated.

INSTRUCTION: VNCLIP.WV
FORMAT: vnclip.wv vd, vs2, vs1, vm
RULE 1: VNCLIP.WV selects the rounding mode from the vxrm CSR for all element computations.
RULE 2: VNCLIP.WV sets the vxsat bit in the vxsat register if any destination element is saturated by the operation.

INSTRUCTION: VNCLIP.WX
FORMAT: vnclip.wx vd, vs2, rs1, vm
RULE 1: VNCLIP.WX computes each destination element vd[i] as the clipped result of rounding the signed fixed-point value obtained from source element vs2[i] after it is right-shifted by a shift amount taken from integer register rs1.
RULE 2: VNCLIP.WX uses the low lg₂(2 × SEW) bits of integer register rs1 as the right-shift amount that provides the scaling for each source element.
RULE 3: VNCLIP.WX narrows each source element to a destination element whose width is half that of the source element, discarding any bits beyond the destination element width after clipping.
RULE 4: VNCLIP.WX rounds each scaled source element according to the rounding mode specified in the vxrm CSR, rounding around the least-significant bit of the destination element.
RULE 5: VNCLIP.WX performs rounding before applying saturation to the destination element.
RULE 6: VNCLIP.WX treats the rounded, shifted source value as a signed integer and saturates it to the representable range of the signed destination element if overflow would occur.
RULE 7: VNCLIP.WX sets the vxsat bit in the vxsat register when any destination element is saturated as a result of the operation.

INSTRUCTION: VNCLIPU.WI
FORMAT: vnclipu.wi vd, vs2, zimm5, vm
RULE 1: VNCLIPU.WI uses a zero-extended 5-bit immediate uimm and takes the low lg2(2*SEW) bits of that immediate as the right-shift amount applied to each source element for scaling.
RULE 2: VNCLIPU.WI, when performing a SEW = 64-bit to SEW = 32-bit narrowing operation, uses the low 6 bits of the immediate as the shift amount.
RULE 3: VNCLIPU.WI obtains the rounding mode from the vxrm CSR and applies rounding around the least-significant bit of the destination element before any saturation is performed.
RULE 4: VNCLIPU.WI treats the result after rounding and shifting as an unsigned integer and, if that value exceeds the maximum unsigned value representable in the destination element width, saturates the result to that maximum.
RULE 5: VNCLIPU.WI sets the vxsat bit in the vxsat CSR if any destination element is saturated.

INSTRUCTION: VNCLIPU.WV
FORMAT: vnclipu.wv vd, vs2, vs1, vm
RULE 1: VNCLIPU.WV uses the low lg2(2 × SEW) bits of each vs1 element to determine the right-shift amount applied to the corresponding vs2 element.
RULE 2: For a SEW of 64 bits narrowing to a SEW of 32 bits, VNCLIPU.WV uses the low 6 bits of each vs1 element as the shift amount.
RULE 3: VNCLIPU.WV rounds the shifted value using the rounding mode encoded in the vxrm CSR, rounding around the least-significant bit of the destination element.
RULE 4: VNCLIPU.WV performs rounding before any saturation is applied.
RULE 5: VNCLIPU.WV treats the rounded, shifted source value as an unsigned integer for the purpose of clipping to the destination element width.
RULE 6: VNCLIPU.WV saturates a destination element to the maximum unsigned value representable in the destination element if the unsigned result would overflow that width.
RULE 7: VNCLIPU.WV writes each destination element as the clipped unsigned result of the rounding and possible saturation operation.
RULE 8: VNCLIPU.WV sets the vxsat flag in the vxsat register if any destination element is saturated.
RULE 9: VNCLIPU.WV implements the element-wise operation vd[i] = clip(roundoff_unsigned(vs2[i], vs1[i])) for all vector elements i.

INSTRUCTION: VNCLIPU.WX
FORMAT: vnclipu.wx vd, vs2, rs1, vm
RULE 1: VNCLIPU.WX rounds each source element vs2[i] to an unsigned integer using the rounding mode specified in the vxrm CSR, with rounding performed around the least-significant bit of the destination element and before any saturation.
RULE 2: VNCLIPU.WX sets the vxsat bit in the vxsat register if any element of the destination vector register group is saturated during the operation.

INSTRUCTION: VNMSAC.VV
FORMAT: vnmsac.vv vd, vs1, vs2, vm
RULE 1: VNMSAC.VV must operate only on elements up to the current active vector length.
RULE 2: For each active element i, VNMSAC.VV must compute the low half of the product of vs1[i] and vs2[i], negate that low-half product, and add the result to the original value of vd[i].
RULE 3: VNMSAC.VV must write the computed value back to vd[i], thereby overwriting the original contents of the destination register (destructive behavior).
RULE 4: VNMSAC.VV must use only the low half of the 2×XLEN product of vs1[i] and vs2[i] in the computation and must not use the high half.
RULE 5: If the mask operand vm is present, VNMSAC.VV must update only those elements whose corresponding mask bit is set and must leave unchanged the elements whose mask bit is clear.

INSTRUCTION: VNMSAC.VX
FORMAT: vnmsac.vx vd, rs1, vs2, vm
RULE 1: VNMSAC.VX computes each element i as the negated product of the scalar integer in rs1 and the i-th element of vs2 added to the original i-th element of vd, storing the result into vd[i].
RULE 2: VNMSAC.VX uses only the low half of the product of x[rs1] and vs2[i] when performing the addition with the original vd[i].

INSTRUCTION: VNMSUB.VV
FORMAT: vnmsub.vv vd, vs1, vs2, vm
RULE 1: For each element i selected by the mask vm, VNMSUB.VV MUST write to vd[i] the value obtained by negating the low half of the product of vs1[i] and vd[i] and then adding vs2[i].
RULE 2: For each element i not selected by the mask vm, VNMSUB.VV MUST leave vd[i] unchanged.

INSTRUCTION: VNMSUB.VX
FORMAT: vnmsub.vx vd, rs1, vs2, vm
RULE 1: VNMSUB.VX is a destructive instruction that overwrites the destination vector register with the computed result for each active element.
RULE 2: VNMSUB.VX computes for each element i up to the active vector length the value vd[i] = -(x[rs1] * vd[i]) + vs2[i] using the scalar integer from integer register rs1, the original destination element, and the i-th element of source vector register vs2.
RULE 3: VNMSUB.VX uses only the low-order half of the integer product x[rs1] * vd[i] when negating the product before adding the corresponding vs2 element.
RULE 4: When a mask operand vm is supplied, VNMSUB.VX updates only those elements whose mask bit is true and leaves the other destination elements unchanged.
RULE 5: If no mask operand is present, VNMSUB.VX updates all elements up to the current active vector length.

INSTRUCTION: VNSRA.WI
FORMAT: vnsra.wi vd, vs2, zimm5, vm
RULE 1: VNSRA.WI extracts a half-width field from each 2*SEW-wide element of the source vector vs2 and produces a SEW-wide result in the destination vector vd.
RULE 2: VNSRA.WI uses a zero-extended 5-bit immediate uimm encoded in the instruction as the source of the shift amount.
RULE 3: VNSRA.WI determines the effective shift amount by selecting the low lg2(2*SEW) bits of the zero-extended immediate value.
RULE 4: When SEW equals 64 bits, VNSRA.WI selects the low 6 bits of uimm as the effective shift amount.
RULE 5: VNSRA.WI right-shifts the extracted half-width field arithmetically by the effective shift amount and then sign-extends the shifted value to fill a SEW-wide destination element.

INSTRUCTION: VNSRA.WV
FORMAT: vnsra.wv vd, vs2, vs1, vm
RULE 1: VNSRA.WV must convert a double-width source vector register group specified by vs2 whose EEW/EMUL equals 2 × SEW / 2 × LMUL into a result vector register group whose element width and group multiplier are the current SEW and LMUL settings.
RULE 2: If a second source vector register group vs1 is present for VNSRA.WV, the element width of vs1 must equal the element width of the result (EEW = SEW).
RULE 3: In the vector-vector form of VNSRA.WV (vnop.wv vd, vs2, vs1, vm), each result element vd[i] must be the arithmetic right-shift of the double-width element vs2[i] by the shift amount contained in the corresponding element vs1[i].
RULE 4: VNSRA.WV must produce a single-width result vector register group vd whose element width equals the current SEW setting.
RULE 5: VNSRA.WV produces destination elements whose width (SEW) is exactly one half of the width of the corresponding source elements.
RULE 6: If VNSRA.WV does not extract the smaller field from each wider source element before shifting, then the destination vector register contains the source elements shifted by the full-width values instead of the correctly extracted field, producing an incorrect result.
RULE 7: VNSRA.WV sign-extends the result of the arithmetic right shift to fill the destination element width.
RULE 8: VNSRA.WV uses only the low lg₂(2 × SEW) bits of each shift-amount element, discarding any higher-order bits.

INSTRUCTION: VNSRA.WX
FORMAT: vnsra.wx vd, vs2, rs1, vm
RULE 1: If the element width of the source vector register group vs2 is not exactly twice the destination element width SEW, then the instruction raises an illegal‑instruction exception; otherwise each element of vs2 is extracted as a signed 2 × SEW‑bit value and used for the narrowing operation.
RULE 2: VNSRA.WX takes the shift amount from scalar register rs1 and uses only the low lg2(2 × SEW) bits of that value as the arithmetic right-shift count.
RULE 3: For a narrowing from 64-bit source elements to 32-bit destination elements, VNSRA.WX uses the low 6 bits of rs1 as the shift amount.
RULE 4: VNSRA.WX performs a sign-extending arithmetic right shift of each selected 2 × SEW element of vs2 by the shift amount and writes the low SEW bits of the shifted result into the corresponding element of destination register group vd.
RULE 5: VNSRA.WX respects the vector mask vm such that elements for which the mask bit is clear are not written to vd and retain their prior values.

INSTRUCTION: VNSRL.WI
FORMAT: vnsrl.wi vd, vs2, zimm5, vm
RULE 1: If a VNSRL.WI instruction’s destination vector register group overlaps the source vector register group and the overlap does not satisfy any of the three conditions in RULE 1, RULE 2, or RULE 3, then executing the instruction raises an illegal‑instruction exception (mcause set to the illegal‑instruction code).
RULE 2: Any VNSRL.WI instruction encoding that specifies a vector register group using a register other than the lowest-numbered register of that group is reserved.
RULE 3: When source and destination registers of VNSRL.WI overlap and have different EEW, the instruction executes with mask-agnostic and tail-agnostic behavior regardless of the vta and vma bits in vtype.
RULE 4: VNSRL.WI performs a logical right shift on each element of the source vector register group vs2 using a shift amount derived from the immediate operand.
RULE 5: VNSRL.WI zero-extends the result of the logical right shift and writes the lower SEW bits into the corresponding elements of the destination vector register group vd.
RULE 6: If the source element width is not exactly twice the destination element width (SEW) for VNSRL.WI, then the instruction raises an illegal‑instruction exception.
RULE 7: VNSRL.WI uses the low lg2(2*SEW) bits of the zero-extended 5-bit immediate operand uimm as the shift amount.
RULE 8: When the source element width is 64 bits and the destination element width is 32 bits, VNSRL.WI uses the low 6 bits of the immediate as the shift amount.
RULE 9: VNSRL.WI applies the vector mask vm to predicate each element operation in the same manner as other vector instructions.

INSTRUCTION: VNSRL.WV
FORMAT: vnsrl.wv vd, vs2, vs1, vm
RULE 1: VNSRL.WV is the zero-extending (logical) variant of the narrowing right-shift operation.
RULE 2: VNSRL.WV uses the low lg2(2 * SEW) bits of each shift-amount element from vs1 as the effective shift amount for the corresponding element of vs2.
RULE 3: VNSRL.WV performs a logical right shift of each 2 * SEW element of the source vector register group vs2 by the effective shift amount, inserting zeros into the vacated high bits.
RULE 4: VNSRL.WV extracts the low SEW bits of the shifted 2 * SEW result and zero-extends them to form a SEW-wide destination element.
RULE 5: VNSRL.WV writes the zero-extended SEW result to the corresponding element of destination vector register group vd for each element whose mask bit in vm is true.
RULE 6: VNSRL.WV leaves the destination element unchanged for each element whose mask bit in vm is false.
RULE 7: If VNSRL.WV is executed and the standard vector‑operation checks (e.g., supported element width, valid vtype settings, sufficient vector length) are satisfied, then the instruction completes normally with no additional exception; if any of those standard checks fail, then the same exception defined for vector operations (such as an illegal‑instruction or vector‑length exception) is raised.

INSTRUCTION: VNSRL.WX
FORMAT: vnsrl.wx vd, vs2, rs1, vm
RULE 1: If VNSRL.WX does not perform a logical right shift on each source element before extracting the smaller field, then the destination vector register contains values that differ from the zero‑extended lower bits of the correctly shifted source elements.
RULE 2: VNSRL.WX obtains the shift amount from the scalar integer register rs1.
RULE 3: VNSRL.WX uses only the low lg2(2*SEW) bits of the value in rs1 as the shift amount, ignoring higher bits.
RULE 4: When SEW is 64 bits and the destination element width is 32 bits, VNSRL.WX uses the low 6 bits of rs1 as the shift amount.
RULE 5: If after executing VNSRL.WX any element of the destination vector register group vd does not equal the result of applying the VNSRL.WX operation to the corresponding element of the source vector register group vs2, then the architectural state shows an incorrect value in that vd element.
RULE 6: VNSRL.WX respects the optional mask register vm, leaving destination elements unchanged for which the corresponding mask bit is clear.
RULE 7: If rs1 is the zero register x0, VNSRL.WX halves the integer width of each source element, converting it to a destination element of half the source width.

INSTRUCTION: VREDAND.VS
FORMAT: vredand.vs vd, vs2, vs1, vm
RULE 1: All operands and the result of VREDAND.VS must have identical SEW width.
RULE 2: VREDAND.VS uses element 0 of source vector register vs1 as the scalar operand for the reduction.
RULE 3: VREDAND.VS considers only the elements of vs2 whose corresponding mask bits are active in the current vector mask vm when performing the reduction.
RULE 4: VREDAND.VS computes the logical AND of the scalar operand vs1[0] with each active element of vs2 and reduces these values to a single element.
RULE 5: VREDAND.VS writes the single reduction result to element 0 of destination vector register vd.

INSTRUCTION: VREDMAX.VS
FORMAT: vredmax.vs vd, vs2, vs1, vm
RULE 1: VREDMAX.VS requires that the selected element width (SEW) of the source scalar register vs1, the source vector register vs2, and the destination register vd are identical.
RULE 2: VREDMAX.VS writes the maximum of the scalar element vs1[0] and all active elements of vector register vs2 into element 0 of the destination register vd.
RULE 3: VREDMAX.VS defines only element 0 of the destination register vd after execution; the contents of all other elements of vd are unspecified.
RULE 4: VREDMAX.VS considers an element of vs2 active if the corresponding mask bit in vm is true, and only those active elements participate in the maximum computation.
RULE 5: VREDMAX.VS ignores elements of vs2 whose corresponding mask bit in vm is false when computing the maximum.

INSTRUCTION: VREDMAXU.VS
FORMAT: vredmaxu.vs vd, vs2, vs1, vm
RULE 1: If the SEW of vd, vs2, or the element vs1[0] are not identical, then VREDMAXU.VS raises an illegal‑instruction exception (mcause = 2).
RULE 2: VREDMAXU.VS computes vd[0] as the unsigned maximum of the scalar element vs1[0] and every element of vs2.
RULE 3: VREDMAXU.VS MUST store the computed maximum in vd[0] using the same SEW width as the source operands.

INSTRUCTION: VREDMIN.VS
FORMAT: vredmin.vs vd, vs2, vs1, vm
RULE 1: VREDMIN.VS requires that the selected element width (SEW) of the source registers vs1 and vs2 and the destination register vd are identical.
RULE 2: If VREDMIN.VS executes, then the destination register contains the minimum of all active elements of vs2 together with the scalar element vs1[0].
RULE 3: VREDMIN.VS writes the reduction result to element 0 of the destination vector register vd.
RULE 4: VREDMIN.VS computes vd[0] as the minimum of the scalar element vs1[0] and each active element of vs2 selected by the mask vm.
RULE 5: VREDMIN.VS considers an element of vs2 active only if the corresponding mask bit in vm is set.
RULE 6: VREDMIN.VS does not modify any elements of vd other than element 0.

INSTRUCTION: VREDMINU.VS
FORMAT: vredminu.vs vd, vs2, vs1, vm
RULE 1: If the source operands or the destination element of VREDMINU.VS do not all have the same standard element width (SEW), then the instruction raises an illegal‑instruction exception (mcause is set to the illegal‑instruction code).
RULE 2: VREDMINU.VS follows the overflow behavior of single-width reduction instructions, whereby any arithmetic overflow wraps around.
RULE 3: VREDMINU.VS computes vd[0] as the unsigned minimum of the scalar element vs1[0] and each active element of vs2.
RULE 4: VREDMINU.VS treats an element of vs2 as active if the corresponding mask bit in vm is set, and excludes elements with a cleared mask bit from the reduction.

INSTRUCTION: VREDOR.VS
FORMAT: vredor.vs vd, vs2, vs1, vm
RULE 1: VREDOR.VS computes vd[0] as the bitwise OR of vs1[0] and all active elements of vs2 selected by the mask vm.
RULE 2: VREDOR.VS uses the mask vm to determine which elements of vs2 are active, and only those active elements contribute to the OR reduction.
RULE 3: VREDOR.VS requires that vd, vs2, vs1, and the result all have the same standard element width (SEW) as defined for single-width reduction instructions.
RULE 4: VREDOR.VS writes the reduction result only to element 0 of the destination vector register vd and leaves all other elements of vd unchanged.

INSTRUCTION: VREDSUM.VS
FORMAT: vredsum.vs vd, vs2, vs1, vm
RULE 1: VREDSUM.VS requires that the standard element width (SEW) of the destination register vd, source register vs1, and source register vs2 are identical.
RULE 2: VREDSUM.VS treats the element vs1[0] as the initial accumulator value for the reduction.
RULE 3: VREDSUM.VS adds each active element of vs2 to the accumulator, where an element of vs2 is active if the corresponding mask bit in vm is true.
RULE 4: VREDSUM.VS performs the additions using integer arithmetic defined for the instruction's SEW, and any overflow wraps around modulo 2^SEW.
RULE 5: After all active elements of vs2 have been added, VREDSUM.VS writes the final accumulator value to element 0 of the destination register vd.
RULE 6: VREDSUM.VS does not modify any element of vd other than element 0.

INSTRUCTION: VREDXOR.VS
FORMAT: vredxor.vs vd, vs2, vs1, vm
RULE 1: VREDXOR.VS MUST use a selected element width (SEW) that is identical for both source vector registers and the destination vector register, and the result element width is that same SEW.
RULE 2: VREDXOR.VS sets the element zero of the destination vector register (vd[0]) to the bitwise XOR of the element zero of the first source vector register (vs1[0]) with the XOR of all active elements of the second source vector register (vs2).

INSTRUCTION: VREM.VV
FORMAT: vrem.vv vd, vs2, vs1, vm
RULE 1: VREM.VV must compute the signed remainder of each corresponding element of the source vector vs2 divided by the corresponding element of the source vector vs1 and place the result in the matching element of the destination vector vd.
RULE 2: The signed remainder produced by VREM.VV for each element must be identical to the result of the scalar signed remainder operation applied to the same operand values.
RULE 3: The equivalence between the result of VREM.VV and the scalar signed remainder operation must hold for extreme input cases.

INSTRUCTION: VREM.VX
FORMAT: vrem.vx vd, vs2, rs1, vm
RULE 1: VREM.VX reads a signed scalar integer from integer register rs1 and a vector of signed integers from vector register vs2, computes the signed remainder of each element of vs2 divided by the scalar value, and writes the resulting signed integers to the corresponding elements of destination vector register vd.
RULE 2: VREM.VX must produce, for every element, a signed remainder value that is identical to the result of the scalar signed remainder operation defined by the base RISC-V ISA for the same dividend and divisor, even when the operands are extreme edge-case values.

INSTRUCTION: VREMU.VV
FORMAT: vremu.vv vd, vs2, vs1, vm
RULE 1: VREMU.VV is a vector-vector instruction that computes the element-wise unsigned integer remainder of each element of source vector register vs2 divided by the corresponding element of source vector register vs1.
RULE 2: VREMU.VV writes each computed remainder element into the corresponding element of destination vector register vd only for elements whose mask bit in vm is active.
RULE 3: VREMU.VV does not modify the corresponding element of vd for any element whose mask bit in vm is inactive.
RULE 4: VREMU.VV produces the same remainder result as the scalar unsigned remainder operation for every possible input value, including extreme cases.

INSTRUCTION: VREMU.VX
FORMAT: vremu.vx vd, vs2, rs1, vm
RULE 1: VREMU.VX must compute for each element i of the source vector register vs2 the unsigned remainder of vs2[i] divided by the scalar integer in rs1 and must write that result to element i of the destination vector register vd.
RULE 2: VREMU.VX must apply the vector mask register vm so that if the mask bit for element i is clear, the instruction must leave vd[i] unchanged.
RULE 3: The unsigned remainder result produced by VREMU.VX for any element i must be identical to the result that would be produced by the scalar unsigned remainder instruction applied to the same operand values.
RULE 4: For extreme input values, including division by zero and overflow cases, VREMU.VX must produce the same architectural result or exception as the scalar unsigned remainder instruction would produce for those values.
RULE 5: Any permissions, exceptions, or architectural side-effects that apply to the scalar unsigned remainder instruction must also apply to each element operation performed by VREMU.VX.

INSTRUCTION: VREV8.V
FORMAT: vrev8.v vd, vs2, vm
RULE 1: VREV8.V reverses the order of bytes within each element of the source vector register and writes the resulting elements to the destination vector register.
