RULE 2: For each element, the byte at position i (where 0 ≤ i < SEW/8) in the source element becomes the byte at position (SEW/8-1-i) in the corresponding destination element when VREV8.V is executed.
RULE 3: When VREV8.V is executed with a mask, elements for which the mask bit is clear are left unchanged in the destination vector register.
RULE 4: Elements with an index greater than or equal to the current vector length (VL) are not written by VREV8.V and retain their previous values.
RULE 5: VREV8.V is defined for any standard element width (SEW) that is a multiple of eight bits, and when SEW equals eight bits the instruction leaves each element unchanged because there is only a single byte.
RULE 6: VREV8.V reverses the order of bytes within each element of the source vector and writes the resulting elements to the destination vector.
RULE 7: VREV8.V performs a byte-reversal (endian swap) on each element of the source vector register vs2 and writes the resulting element to the destination vector register vd.
RULE 8: For each element index i from vstart to the current vector length minus one, VREV8.V reads the SEW-wide element from vs2, reverses the order of its bytes, and writes the SEW-wide result to vd at the same index.
RULE 9: Within each element, VREV8.V assigns output bits k..k+7 the value of input bits (j-1)..j for k = 0, 8, …, SEW-8 while j is initialized to SEW and decremented by 8 after each assignment.
RULE 10: VREV8.V does not modify any architectural state other than the destination vector register vd.

INSTRUCTION: VRGATHER.VI
FORMAT: vrgather.vi vd, vs2, zimm5, vm
RULE 1: VRGATHER.VI zero-extends the immediate uimm to XLEN bits and treats the resulting value as an unsigned integer index.
RULE 2: VRGATHER.VI does not truncate the zero-extended uimm index to SEW bits when XLEN is greater than SEW.
RULE 3: For each element i, VRGATHER.VI writes vd[i] equal to 0 if the unsigned uimm index is greater than or equal to VLMAX; otherwise it writes vd[i] equal to the element vs2[uimm].
RULE 4: If a mask register vm is supplied and a mask bit for an element is 0, then the destination element is not required to be updated; it may remain unchanged (mask‑undisturbed) or contain any value (mask‑agnostic).
RULE 5: VRGATHER.VI can read the source vector element at any index less than VLMAX regardless of the current vector length vl.
RULE 6: VRGATHER.VI writes results only to destination elements i such that i is less than the current vector length vl; elements with i ≥ vl are handled according to the current tail policy.
RULE 7: VRGATHER.VI applies the current tail policy to destination elements beyond vl.
RULE 8: VRGATHER.VI encoding is reserved if the destination register group vd overlaps with the source register group vs2.

INSTRUCTION: VRGATHER.VV
FORMAT: vrgather.vv vd, vs2, vs1, vm
RULE 1: VRGATHER.VV MUST interpret each index element from the second source vector register group as an unsigned integer.
RULE 2: VRGATHER.VV MUST be able to read a source element at any index that is less than VLMAX regardless of the current active vector length (vl).
RULE 3: VRGATHER.VV MUST write result elements for all element positions i where i < vl, and for positions i ≥ vl the architectural effect MUST follow the currently configured tail policy.
RULE 4: When a mask is supplied, VRGATHER.VV MUST update only elements whose mask bit is true and MUST leave inactive elements undisturbed or set them according to the configured mask-undisturbed or mask-agnostic policy.
RULE 5: For each element i, VRGATHER.VV MUST write zero to the destination element if the index value vs1[i] is greater than or equal to VLMAX; otherwise it MUST write the element from the first source vector register group located at index vs1[i].
RULE 6: VRGATHER.VV MUST use the same element width (SEW) and vector grouping (LMUL) for both the data source vector register group and the index source vector register group.
RULE 7: If SEW is 8 bits and VRGATHER.VV references an index value less than 0 or greater than 255, then the destination vector register contains an undefined (unpredictable) result.
RULE 8: VRGATHER.VV MUST return zero for any destination element whose corresponding index value is greater than or equal to VLMAX.
RULE 9: VRGATHER.VV MUST have a destination vector register group that does not overlap any source vector register group; encodings that cause overlap are reserved.
RULE 10: In an execution of VRGATHER.VV v11, v1, v10, v0.t the instruction expands elements from source vector v1 into destination vector v11 according to the indices supplied by vector v10, and writes each element only if the corresponding mask bit in v0.t is true.

INSTRUCTION: VRGATHER.VX
FORMAT: vrgather.vx vd, vs2, rs1, vm
RULE 1: VRGATHER.VX treats the index value from the scalar register (or immediate) as an unsigned XLEN-bit integer and does not truncate the index when XLEN is greater than SEW.
RULE 2: VRGATHER.VX writes a zero to destination element i when the unsigned index is greater than or equal to VLMAX; otherwise it writes the element from the source vector at that index.
RULE 3: VRGATHER.VX reads a single element from the source vector at the computed index and writes that value to each destination element whose mask bit is active.
RULE 4: VRGATHER.VX leaves destination elements unchanged for which the mask bit is false, following the mask-agnostic policy.
RULE 5: VRGATHER.VX writes results only to the first vl elements of the destination vector and handles elements beyond vl according to the current tail policy.
RULE 6: VRGATHER.VX encoding is reserved when the destination vector register group overlaps any source vector register group.

INSTRUCTION: VRGATHEREI16.VV
FORMAT: vrgatherei16.vv vd, vs2, vs1, vm
RULE 1: VRGATHEREI16.VV computes each destination element vd[i] as 0 if the corresponding index element vs1[i] is greater than or equal to VLMAX, otherwise as the element vs2[vs1[i]].
RULE 2: VRGATHEREI16.VV treats index values in vs1 as unsigned integers.
RULE 3: VRGATHEREI16.VV writes at most vl elements to the destination register group vd; elements beyond vl are handled according to the current tail policy.
RULE 4: If a mask bit is zero for a given element during execution of VRGATHEREI16.VV, then the architectural result for that element must either remain equal to its pre‑instruction value (mask‑undisturbed) or be equal to the value that would be produced by the same operation without applying the mask (mask‑agnostic); any other value in the destination element indicates a violation.
RULE 5: VRGATHEREI16.VV must not have the destination register group vd overlap with either source register group vs2 or vs1, and any such overlap makes the encoding reserved.

INSTRUCTION: VROL.VV
FORMAT: vrol.vv vd, vs2, vs1, vm
RULE 1: VROL.VV MUST perform a bitwise left rotation on each element of the source vector vs2.
RULE 2: VROL.VV MUST use the corresponding element of vs1 as the rotate amount for each element of vs2.
RULE 3: VROL.VV MUST mask the rotate amount with (SEW-1), using only the low log2(SEW) bits of each vs1 element and ignoring all higher bits.
RULE 4: For each element index i from vstart to vl-1, VROL.VV MUST set the destination element vd[i] to the value of vs2[i] rotated left by (vs1[i] & (SEW-1)).
RULE 5: VROL.VV MUST NOT modify destination elements for indices i outside the active vector range [vstart, vl).
RULE 6: If VROL.VV does not retire with a successful completion status after performing the element‑wise rotations, then an exception is raised (mcause is set to the corresponding exception code) and the destination register does not contain the expected rotated result.

INSTRUCTION: VROL.VX
FORMAT: vrol.vx vd, vs2, rs1, vm
RULE 1: VROL.VX must perform a bitwise left rotation on each element of the source vector register vs2.
RULE 2: VROL.VX must obtain the rotate amount from integer register rs1 and apply the same amount to all vector elements.
RULE 3: VROL.VX must mask the value read from rs1 with the low log2(SEW) bits, ignoring all higher-order bits of rs1.
RULE 4: VROL.VX must, for each element index i from vstart to vl-1, write to destination vector register vd element i the result of left-rotating the corresponding element of vs2 by the masked rotate amount.
RULE 5: VROL.VX must leave unchanged any element of vd whose index is outside the active range vstart to vl-1.
RULE 6: VROL.VX must rotate bits within each element modulo the element width SEW, so that bits shifted out of the most-significant position reappear at the least-significant position.
RULE 7: VROL.VX must retire successfully without raising an architectural exception when all active elements have been processed.

INSTRUCTION: VROR.VI
FORMAT: vror.vi vd, vs2, zimm6, vm
RULE 1: A VROR.VI instruction executed with an immediate rotate amount equal to the modular negation of a desired left-rotate amount produces the same architectural result as a rotate-left immediate operation would have produced, because no VROL.VI immediate form is defined.

INSTRUCTION: VROR.VV
FORMAT: vror.vv vd, vs2, vs1, vm
RULE 1: VROR.VV performs a bitwise right rotation on each element of the source vector register vs2.
RULE 2: VROR.VV takes the rotation amount for each element from the corresponding element of the source vector register vs1.
RULE 3: VROR.VV uses only the low log2(SEW) bits of the rotation-amount value from vs1 and ignores all higher bits.

INSTRUCTION: VROR.VX
FORMAT: vror.vx vd, vs2, rs1, vm
RULE 1: VROR.VX must rotate each element of the source vector register vs2 right by a rotate amount derived from the low log2(SEW) bits of the integer register rs1.
RULE 2: VROR.VX must ignore all bits of rs1 above the low log2(SEW) bits when determining the rotate amount.
RULE 3: VROR.VX must iterate over element indices i from the current vstart up to vl-1 inclusive.
RULE 4: For each element i, VROR.VX must write to the destination vector register vd the result of right-rotating the i-th element of vs2 by the masked rotate amount.
RULE 5: VROR.VX must use an element width equal to the selected SEW (EEW = SEW) for all element operations.
RULE 6: After processing all elements in the active range, VROR.VX must retire with success (RETIRE_SUCCESS) and must not raise an exception.

INSTRUCTION: VRSUB.VI
FORMAT: vrsub.vi vd, vs2, simm5, vm
RULE 1: VRSUB.VI must treat the immediate operand as a scalar value that is used as the left operand of the subtraction for every element of the operation.
RULE 2: VRSUB.VI must compute for each element index i within the current vector length the result imm − vs2[i] and write that result to the destination element vd[i] when the mask bit for that element is true.
RULE 3: VRSUB.VI must leave the destination element vd[i] unchanged for any element index i within the current vector length when the mask bit for that element is false.
RULE 4: VRSUB.VI must perform the element-wise computation for all element indices i from 0 up to the current vector length minus one.
RULE 5: VRSUB.VI must use the vector-scalar reverse-subtract immediate form, meaning the operation subtracts each source vector element from the immediate scalar.
RULE 6: VRSUB.VI must, in the absence of a mask register, execute the subtraction for all elements of the current vector length.

INSTRUCTION: VS1R.V
FORMAT: vs1r.v vs3, (rs1)
RULE 1: VS1R.V transfers exactly one vector register (NFIELDS = 1).
RULE 2: VS1R.V computes its effective vector length evl as VLEN divided by EEW and uses this evl regardless of the current vtype configuration and the current vl setting.
RULE 3: The usual property that no elements are written when vstart ≥ vl does not apply to VS1R.V.
RULE 4: The usual property that the instruction is reserved when vstart exceeds the largest element index for the current vtype does not apply to VS1R.V.
RULE 5: VS1R.V stores the lowest-numbered vector register at the lowest-numbered memory addresses beginning at the address in rs1.

INSTRUCTION: VS2R.V
FORMAT: vs2r.v vs3, (rs1)
RULE 1: VS2R.V encoding is reserved when the nf field encodes a number of registers that is not a power of two.
RULE 2: VS2R.V encoding is reserved when the starting vector register number is not aligned to the vector register group size defined by NFIELDS.
RULE 3: VS2R.V encoding is reserved for NFIELDS values other than 1, 2, 4, or 8.
RULE 4: VS2R.V computes its effective vector length as evl = NFIELDS × VLEN ÷ EEW independent of the current vtype and vl settings.
RULE 5: VS2R.V does not suppress element stores when vstart ≥ vl, so stores may occur even if vstart is greater than or equal to vl.
RULE 6: VS2R.V encoding is reserved when the architectural vstart is greater than or equal to the computed evl.
RULE 7: VS2R.V uses the scalar register specified as rs1 to supply the base address for the memory store.
RULE 8: VS2R.V may raise a misaligned address exception if the base address is not naturally aligned to the larger of EEW/8 bytes and SEWMIN/8 bytes.
RULE 9: VS2R.V operates as an unmasked unit-stride store of elements whose effective element width is 8 bits.
RULE 10: VS2R.V stores the lowest-numbered vector register at the lowest memory address and stores successive vector registers contiguously in increasing address order.

INSTRUCTION: VS8R.V
FORMAT: vs8r.v vs3, (rs1)
RULE 1: VS8R.V stores the contents of the eight vector registers v8 through v15 to memory.
RULE 2: VS8R.V writes the contents of vector register v8 to the lowest-addressed memory location and writes the contents of registers v9 through v15 to consecutive higher addresses in ascending register order.
RULE 3: VS8R.V uses the scalar integer register specified as rs1 as the base address for the memory store.
RULE 4: VS8R.V does not suppress stores when vstart ≥ vl; instead it is reserved when vstart ≥ VLEN.
RULE 5: VS8R.V is reserved if the eight registers are not aligned as a vector-register group.
RULE 6: VS8R.V writes the register contents to memory exactly as they appear in the registers because the in-register byte layout is identical to the in-memory byte layout, making the stored data independent of the EEW value.
RULE 7: VS8R.V transfers eight whole vector registers as indicated by its nf field.

INSTRUCTION: VSADD.VI
FORMAT: vsadd.vi vd, vs2, simm5, vm
RULE 1: VSADD.VI adds a signed immediate operand to each signed integer element of the source vector vs2 and writes the resulting saturated values to the destination vector vd.
RULE 2: VSADD.VI produces each element of the destination vector vd with the same element width as the corresponding elements of the source vector vs2.
RULE 3: VSADD.VI saturates on overflow by replacing any element result that exceeds the representable range of the destination element type with the nearest representable signed integer value.
RULE 4: VSADD.VI sets the vxsat bit in the vector status register when any element addition overflows the destination element type.

INSTRUCTION: VSADD.VV
FORMAT: vsadd.vv vd, vs2, vs1, vm
RULE 1: VSADD.VV adds the signed integer value of each element in source vector register vs2 to the signed integer value of the corresponding element in source vector register vs1 and writes the result to the corresponding element of destination vector register vd.
RULE 2: VSADD.VV saturates an element result to the nearest representable value when the signed addition of the corresponding elements of vs2 and vs1 would overflow the destination element type.
RULE 3: VSADD.VV sets the vxsat status flag bit whenever any element result is saturated due to overflow.

INSTRUCTION: VSADD.VX
FORMAT: vsadd.vx vd, vs2, rs1, vm
RULE 1: VSADD.VX MUST perform a signed integer addition of each element in vs2 with the scalar value in rs1 to produce an element result.
RULE 2: VSADD.VX MUST write each element result into the corresponding element of the destination vector register vd for elements whose mask bit is true, or for all elements when no mask is present.
RULE 3: VSADD.VX MUST leave each destination element unchanged for elements whose mask bit is false when a mask register vm is present.

INSTRUCTION: VSADDU.VI
FORMAT: vsaddu.vi vd, vs2, simm5, vm
RULE 1: VSADDU.VI MUST add, for each element, the unsigned integer in the corresponding element of vs2 to the sign-extended immediate imm and write the result to the corresponding element of vd.
RULE 2: If the result of the element-wise addition performed by VSADDU.VI would overflow the destination element, VSADDU.VI MUST replace the result with the closest representable unsigned value for that element and set the vxsat bit.

INSTRUCTION: VSADDU.VV
FORMAT: vsaddu.vv vd, vs2, vs1, vm
RULE 1: VSADDU.VV performs element-wise addition of unsigned integer elements from vs2 and vs1 and writes the results to the corresponding elements of vd.
RULE 2: VSADDU.VV saturates each element result to the largest representable unsigned integer value when the addition would overflow the element's width.
RULE 3: VSADDU.VV sets the vxsat status bit whenever any element result is saturated due to overflow.
RULE 4: VSADDU.VV writes an element of vd only if the corresponding mask bit in vm is true; otherwise the element of vd remains unchanged.

INSTRUCTION: VSADDU.VX
FORMAT: vsaddu.vx vd, vs2, rs1, vm
RULE 1: VSADDU.VX must perform an element-wise unsigned integer addition of each element of source vector register vs2 with the unsigned integer value in integer register rs1, storing each result in the corresponding element of destination vector register vd.
RULE 2: VSADDU.VX must saturate any element result that would exceed the maximum unsigned integer representable for the element width to that maximum value.
RULE 3: VSADDU.VX must set the vxsat status flag when any element result is saturated due to overflow.

INSTRUCTION: VSBC.VVM
FORMAT: vsbc.vvm vd, vs2, vs1, v0
RULE 1: For each element index i, VSBC.VVM computes vd[i] equal to vs2[i] minus vs1[i] minus v0.mask[i].
RULE 2: VSBC.VVM writes the computed element results to the destination vector register vd.
RULE 3: Despite being encoded as a masked instruction, VSBC.VVM operates on all element positions and writes back all body elements regardless of the mask bits.
RULE 4: The VSBC.VVM encoding is reserved when the destination vector register vd is register v0.

INSTRUCTION: VSBC.VXM
FORMAT: vsbc.vxm vd, vs2, rs1, v0
RULE 1: VSBC.VXM MUST compute each element i of the active vector length as vd[i] = vs2[i] - x[rs1] - v0.mask[i], where x[rs1] is the scalar value read from integer register rs1 and v0.mask[i] provides the borrow-in bit.
RULE 2: VSBC.VXM with vm set to 1 (unmasked form) is reserved.
RULE 3: VSBC.VXM MUST write the result to every element i of the destination vector register vd regardless of the mask bits.
RULE 4: VSBC.VXM encoding is reserved when the destination vector register is v0.
RULE 5: VSBC.VXM MUST obtain the borrow-in (carry-in) bits exclusively from the implicit mask register v0.

INSTRUCTION: VSE8.V
FORMAT: vse8.v vs3, (rs1), vm
RULE 1: VSE8.V stores a vector of 8-bit elements from the source register group vs3 to memory starting at the address contained in integer register rs1.
RULE 2: VSE8.V may be masked using the mask register vm, and the mask encoding used by VSE8.V is the same as that used by other vector unit-stride operations.
RULE 3: VSE8.V with a mask register and the .t qualifier must store each 8-bit element from the specified source vector register to successive memory addresses beginning at the address held in the base integer register, and it must write an element only when the corresponding mask bit is true, leaving the destination memory unchanged for elements whose mask bit is false.
RULE 4: VSE8.V without a mask operand must perform an unmasked store that writes every 8-bit element from the source vector register to successive memory locations starting at the base address, regardless of any mask state.
RULE 5: VSE8.V without a mask operand must write a zero byte to each destination memory location when the corresponding element in the source vector register is zero.

INSTRUCTION: VSE16.V
FORMAT: vse16.v vs3, (rs1), vm
RULE 1: VSE16.V stores each 16-bit element of the source vector register vs3 to memory using unit-stride addressing starting at the address held in integer register rs1.
RULE 2: VSE16.V computes the memory address for the i-th element as rs1 plus i multiplied by 2 bytes, where i is the element index within the active vector length.
RULE 3: VSE16.V uses the mask register specified by the vm field to control element-wise storage, storing an element only when the corresponding mask bit is true and leaving memory unchanged when the mask bit is false.
RULE 4: VSE16.V reads the mask encoding vm, which may be a mask register such as v0.t or may be omitted, and the presence or absence of a mask determines whether masked store semantics are applied.
RULE 5: VSE16.V does not modify any integer or floating-point registers; it only writes to memory and may read the mask register.

INSTRUCTION: VSE32.V
FORMAT: vse32.v vs3, (rs1), vm
RULE 1: VSE32.V stores a vector of 32-bit elements from the specified source vector register to consecutive memory locations beginning at the address held in the specified base integer register.
RULE 2: In the form "vse32.v v8, (a2)", VSE32.V stores the 32-bit element vector contained in vector register v8 to the memory address referenced by integer register a2.
RULE 3: VSE32.V stores each 32-bit element of the source vector register group specified by vs3 to memory addresses beginning at the address contained in rs1.
RULE 4: For element index i, VSE32.V computes the target address as rs1 plus i multiplied by 4 bytes, thereby using a unit-stride of one element.
RULE 5: If the mask operand vm is present and is v0.t, VSE32.V stores only those elements whose corresponding mask bit is true and does not modify memory for elements whose mask bit is false.
RULE 6: If the mask operand vm is omitted, VSE32.V stores all elements of vs3 without applying any mask.
RULE 7: The operand vs3 is interpreted as a vector register group that provides the 32-bit elements to be written by VSE32.V.
RULE 8: The operand rs1 provides the base address from which VSE32.V begins the unit-stride store.

INSTRUCTION: VSE64.V
FORMAT: vse64.v vs3, (rs1), vm
RULE 1: VSE64.V treats the vector source operand vs3 as the register group that provides the 64-bit elements to be stored.
RULE 2: VSE64.V uses the scalar register rs1 as the base address for the first 64-bit element to be written to memory.
RULE 3: VSE64.V stores each 64-bit element from vs3 to consecutive memory locations with a unit stride of eight bytes, starting at the address held in rs1.
RULE 4: VSE64.V respects the mask encoding vm; when vm is specified as v0.t, only elements whose corresponding mask bit is true are written to memory, and elements with a false mask bit are not stored.
RULE 5: VSE64.V treats a missing vm field as an unconditional store, effectively as if all mask bits are true.

INSTRUCTION: VSETIVLI
FORMAT: vsetivli rd, uimm, vtypei
RULE 1: VSETIVLI must update the vtype CSR to the value encoded in its vtypei operand.
RULE 2: VSETIVLI must compute a new vector length (vl) from the unsigned immediate operand (uimm) according to the vector length calculation algorithm and store that result in the vl CSR.
RULE 3: If VSETIVLI sets the vill bit (bit XLEN-1) of the vtype register to 1, the resulting vtype value is illegal.
RULE 4: If any bit from XLEN‑2 down to 8 in the vtype value written by VSETIVLI is non‑zero, then the processor raises an illegal‑instruction exception (mcause = 2).
RULE 5: VSETIVLI sets bit 7 (vma) of the vtype register to indicate the vector mask-agnostic attribute.
RULE 6: VSETIVLI sets bit 6 (vta) of the vtype register to indicate the vector tail-agnostic attribute.
RULE 7: VSETIVLI sets bits 5 down to 3 (vsew[2:0]) of the vtype register to specify the selected element width (SEW) setting.
RULE 8: VSETIVLI sets bits 2 down to 0 (vlmul[2:0]) of the vtype register to specify the vector register group multiplier (LMUL) setting.

INSTRUCTION: VSETVL
FORMAT: vsetvl rd, rs1, rs2
RULE 1: VSETVL MUST write the signed vlmul field in vtype and the architectural LMUL value is defined as LMUL = 2^vlmul[2:0].
RULE 2: After reset, the vector extension must be in a state such that the values of vtype and vl can be read and subsequently restored by a single VSETVL instruction.
RULE 3: Executing a VSETVL instruction resets the architectural register vstart to zero.
RULE 4: VSETVL writes the value supplied in source register rs2 to the vtype CSR.
RULE 5: VSETVL sets the vl CSR to the hardware-determined vector length that results from the given AVL and the newly written vtype.
RULE 6: VSETVL writes the value of the vl CSR, after it has been determined, into destination register rd.
RULE 7: VSETVL updates both the vtype CSR and the vl CSR as part of its execution.
RULE 8: VSETVL causes an illegal-instruction exception if the vill bit (bit XLEN-1) of the vtype value supplied in rs2 is 1.
RULE 9: VSETVL sets the vector-mask-agnostic attribute to the value of bit 7 (vma) of the vtype value supplied in rs2.
RULE 10: VSETVL sets the vector-tail-agnostic attribute to the value of bit 6 (vta) of the vtype value supplied in rs2.
RULE 11: VSETVL selects the element width (SEW) for subsequent vector operations according to the vsew[2:0] field (bits 5-3) of the vtype value supplied in rs2.
RULE 12: VSETVL selects the vector register-group multiplier (LMUL) for subsequent vector operations according to the vlmul[2:0] field (bits 2-0) of the vtype value supplied in rs2.
RULE 13: When the vtype value supplied in rs2 is neither illegal nor reserved, VSETVL writes that value into the architectural vtype CSR.
RULE 14: VSETVL determines the new vector length (VL) using the same algorithm as VSETVLI, applying that algorithm to the vtype value taken from rs2.
RULE 15: If any unsupported value is present in the vtype argument of VSETVL, the vill bit in the resulting vtype MUST be set.
RULE 16: If any unsupported value is present in the vtype argument of VSETVL, all remaining bits in the resulting vtype MUST be cleared to zero.
RULE 17: If any unsupported value is present in the vtype argument of VSETVL, the vl register MUST be written with zero.
RULE 18: Implementations of VSETVL MUST consider every bit of the vtype argument when determining support, and an unsupported value in any bit position MUST result in the vill bit being set.
RULE 19: When executing VSETVL with rs1 not x0, the instruction must treat AVL as the unsigned integer value held in the integer register specified by rs1 and must write the resulting vector length to the integer register specified by rd.
RULE 20: When executing VSETVL with rs1 = x0 and rd ≠ x0, the instruction must treat AVL as the maximum unsigned integer value (~0) and must write the resulting VLMAX to both the vector length register and the integer register specified by rd.
RULE 21: When executing VSETVL with rs1 = x0 and rd = x0, the instruction must treat AVL as the current vector length value read from the vl CSR and must write the resulting vector length back to the vl CSR without writing to any integer register.
RULE 22: VSETVL must set vl to an integer multiple of the element-group size when the operand whose vl is being set is a vector of element groups.
RULE 23: If VSETVL is executed with a vl value that is not an integer multiple of the element‑group size for a vector operand consisting of element groups, then the instruction raises an illegal‑instruction exception (mcause = 2).
RULE 24: VSETVL must set vl to either VLMAX or to a positive integer multiple of EGSMAX when element-group instructions are present and the supplied AVL value is greater than VLMAX.
RULE 25: VSETVL must select an appropriate SEW when EEW is encoded statically in the instruction or when an instruction has multiple operands containing vectors of element groups with differing EEW.
RULE 26: VSETVL must not be affected by the Zvkt extension.

INSTRUCTION: VSETVLI
FORMAT: vsetvli rd, rs1, vtypei
RULE 1: A VSETVLI instruction sets the LMUL field in the architectural vtype register to 2^{vlmul[2:0]}, where vlmul[2:0] is the signed vlmul field encoded in the instruction.
RULE 2: If VSETVLI is executed with the ta flag set, then any subsequent vector instruction that writes fewer elements than the maximum vector length may leave the destination register’s tail elements with unspecified values—the architectural state does not guarantee any particular value for those bits.
RULE 3: When the tu flag is specified on VSETVLI, subsequent vector instructions leave tail elements undisturbed.
RULE 4: When the ma flag is specified on VSETVLI, subsequent vector instructions treat mask bits as agnostic.
RULE 5: When the mu flag is specified on VSETVLI, subsequent vector instructions leave mask bits undisturbed.
RULE 6: VSETVLI must set the vtype CSR to the value supplied by its vtypei operand.
RULE 7: VSETVLI must compute the vector length (vl) from the AVL supplied by its rs1 operand and the newly written vtype CSR, and must set the vl CSR to that computed value.
RULE 8: VSETVLI must write the resulting value of the vl CSR into the destination register rd.
RULE 9: The immediate fields of VSETVLI provide bits for the vtype CSR as follows: bit XLEN-1 (vill), bits XLEN-2:8, bit 7 (vma), bit 6 (vta), bits 5:3 (vsew[2:0]), and bits 2:0 (vlmul[2:0]).
RULE 10: If the vill bit (XLEN-1) supplied by the immediate fields of VSETVLI is 1, the resulting vtype value MUST be considered illegal.
RULE 11: If any of the bits XLEN-2 through 8 supplied by the immediate fields of VSETVLI are non-zero, the resulting vtype value is reserved.
RULE 12: VSETVLI MUST set the vma bit (bit 7) of the vtype CSR to the value specified by its immediate field.
RULE 13: VSETVLI MUST set the vta bit (bit 6) of the vtype CSR to the value specified by its immediate field.
RULE 14: VSETVLI MUST set the vsew[2:0] bits (bits 5:3) of the vtype CSR according to the SEW mnemonic operand, where e8 selects SEW = 8 bits, e16 selects SEW = 16 bits, e32 selects SEW = 32 bits, and e64 selects SEW = 64 bits.
RULE 15: VSETVLI MUST set the vlmul[2:0] bits (bits 2:0) of the vtype CSR according to the LMUL mnemonic operand, where mf8 selects LMUL = 1/8, mf4 selects LMUL = 1/4, mf2 selects LMUL = 1/2, m1 selects LMUL = 1, m2 selects LMUL = 2, m4 selects LMUL = 4, and m8 selects LMUL = 8.
RULE 16: VSETVLI MUST update the architectural vtype CSR to the value represented by its immediate fields (SEW, LMUL, vta, vma).
RULE 17: VSETVLI must treat the AVL as the unsigned integer value contained in the integer register specified by rs1 when rs1 is not x0.
RULE 18: VSETVLI must write the newly computed vl value to the integer register specified by rd when rs1 is not x0.
RULE 19: VSETVLI must use the maximum unsigned integer value (~0) as the AVL when rs1 is x0 and rd is not x0.
RULE 20: VSETVLI must write the resulting VLMAX to both the vl register and the integer register specified by rd when rs1 is x0 and rd is not x0.
RULE 21: VSETVLI must use the current vl register value as the AVL when both rs1 and rd are x0.
RULE 22: VSETVLI must write the resulting vl value to the vl register but not to any integer register when both rs1 and rd are x0.
RULE 23: VSETVLI with rs1 equal to x0 and rd equal to x0 allows the vtype register to be changed while maintaining the current vl, provided VLMAX is not reduced.
RULE 24: The current vl value can be read from the vl CSR after executing VSETVLI.
RULE 25: Implementations may assume that vl is not changed by VSETVLI with rs1 equal to x0 and rd equal to x0 when VLMAX is not reduced.
RULE 26: VSETVLI a3, a0, e16, m4, ta, ma sets the vector element width to 16 bits and the vector length multiplier to 4, establishing a 16-bit integer vector type.
RULE 27: VSETVLI a3, a0, e16, m4, ta, ma writes the computed VL value, which is the number of elements that can be processed for this iteration, into destination register a3.
RULE 28: VSETVLI x0, x0, e32, m8, ta, ma sets the vector element width to 32 bits and the vector length multiplier to 8, establishing a 32-bit integer vector type.

INSTRUCTION: VSEXT.VF2
FORMAT: vsext.vf2 vd, vs2, vm
RULE 1: VSEXT.VF2 sign-extends each element of the source vector whose element width (EEW) is half of the destination element width (SEW) to produce SEW-sized elements in the destination vector.
RULE 2: VSEXT.VF2 requires the source element width (EEW) to be exactly one-half of the selected SEW; any other EEW is not a supported width for this instruction.
RULE 3: If VSEXT.VF2 is executed and the source vector’s EMUL ≠ LMUL / 2, then the instruction raises an illegal‑instruction exception.
RULE 4: VSEXT.VF2 produces a destination vector whose element width (EEW) equals the selected SEW and whose EMUL equals the current LMUL.

INSTRUCTION: VSEXT.VF4
FORMAT: vsext.vf4 vd, vs2, vm
RULE 1: VSEXT.VF4 must sign-extend each source vector element whose element width is one-fourth of the destination element width to produce a destination element of size SEW.
RULE 2: VSEXT.VF4 requires that the source element width EEW equals SEW divided by 4 and that this EEW is a supported width.
RULE 3: VSEXT.VF4 defines the destination element width EEW to be equal to SEW.
RULE 4: If the source EEW for VSEXT.VF4 is not a supported width, the encoding of VSEXT.VF4 is reserved.
RULE 5: If the source EMUL calculated for VSEXT.VF4 would be below the minimum legal LMUL, the encoding of VSEXT.VF4 is reserved.
RULE 6: When a mask operand vm is supplied to VSEXT.VF4, the sign-extension operation is performed only for elements whose mask bit is true, and destination elements whose mask bit is false are not written.
RULE 7: An instruction whose vs1 field value is binary 00101 must be decoded as the VSEXT.VF4 instruction.

INSTRUCTION: VSEXT.VF8
FORMAT: vsext.vf8 vd, vs2, vm
RULE 1: VSEXT.VF8 must sign-extend each source element whose element width is one-eighth of the destination element width, producing destination elements of size SEW.
RULE 2: If the source EEW for VSEXT.VF8 is not a supported width or the computed source EMUL is less than the minimum legal LMUL, then the VSEXT.VF8 encoding is reserved.
RULE 3: The destination operand element width for VSEXT.VF8 equals SEW.
RULE 4: If the effective LMUL (EMUL) of the destination operand for VSEXT.VF8 does not equal the instruction’s LMUL field, then an illegal‑instruction exception is raised.
RULE 5: VSEXT.VF8 performs sign-extension of a source vector integer operand whose EEW is less than SEW, filling the destination vector with SEW-sized elements.
RULE 6: VSEXT.VF8 implements the sign-extend variant of the vector integer extension instructions, not the zero-extend variant.

INSTRUCTION: VSHA2CH.VV
FORMAT: vsha2ch.vv vd, vs2, vs1
RULE 1: VSHA2CH.VV causes an illegal-instruction exception RETIRE_FAIL when the product LMUL × VLEN is less than the element group width EGW.
RULE 2: VSHA2CH.VV is reserved when the selected SEW is not 32 for the zvknha encoding.
RULE 3: VSHA2CH.VV is reserved when the selected SEW is neither 32 nor 64 for the zvknhb encoding.
RULE 4: VSHA2CH.VV is reserved when the destination register group overlaps any source register group.

INSTRUCTION: VSHA2CL.VV
FORMAT: vsha2cl.vv vd, vs2, vs1
RULE 1: VSHA2CL.VV in a Zvknha implementation is reserved when the selected SEW is any value other than 32.
RULE 2: VSHA2CL.VV in a Zvknhb implementation is reserved when the selected SEW is any value other than 32 or 64.
RULE 3: VSHA2CL.VV encoding is reserved when the destination register group overlaps with either source register group vs1 or vs2.
RULE 4: VSHA2CL.VV writes 4 × SEW bits to destination register vd as the concatenation {a @ b @ e @ f} after processing each element group.
RULE 5: When SEW equals 32, VSHA2CL.VV performs two rounds of SHA-256 compression.
RULE 6: When SEW equals 64, VSHA2CL.VV performs two rounds of SHA-512 compression.
RULE 7: VSHA2CL.VV raises an illegal-instruction exception with RETIRE_FAIL when LMUL × VLEN is less than EGW.
RULE 8: After completing both rounds for element i, VSHA2CL.VV stores the concatenation {a @ b @ e @ f} into vd for that element.
RULE 9: When the execution completes without encountering the LMUL × VLEN < EGW condition, VSHA2CL.VV retires successfully.

INSTRUCTION: VSHA2MS.VV
FORMAT: vsha2ms.vv vd, vs2, vs1
RULE 1: VSHA2MS.VV operates on SHA-256 when the selected SEW is 32 bits and operates on SHA-512 when the selected SEW is 64 bits.
RULE 2: If VSHA2MS.VV processes an element group, then the group must contain exactly four SEW‑sized elements, and the destination register will contain a 128‑bit result when SEW = 32 and a 256‑bit result when SEW = 64; if the group does not consist of four SEW‑sized elements, the destination register receives an unpredictable value.
RULE 3: If VSHA2MS.VV processes a number of element groups other than vl / 4, then the destination vector register elements beyond the first (vl / 4) × 4 positions will not retain their original source values.
RULE 4: VSHA2MS.VV encodings in the zvknha extension are reserved when the selected element width (SEW) is not 32.
RULE 5: VSHA2MS.VV encodings in the zvknhb extension are reserved when the selected element width (SEW) is neither 32 nor 64.
RULE 6: VSHA2MS.VV encodings are reserved when the destination register group (vd) overlaps any register in the source groups vs1 or vs2.
RULE 7: VSHA2MS.VV must raise an illegal-instruction exception and cause retire failure if the product of LMUL and VLEN is less than the element group width (EGW).
RULE 8: VSHA2MS.VV processes element groups indexed from floor(vstart/EGS) up to floor(vl/EGS)-1 for each active element group.
RULE 9: VSHA2MS.VV computes W[16] as sig1(W[14]) plus W[9] plus sig0(W[1]) plus W[0] using the SEW-dependent definitions of sig0 and sig1.
RULE 10: VSHA2MS.VV computes W[17] as sig1(W[15]) plus W[10] plus sig0(W[2]) plus W[1] using the SEW-dependent definitions of sig0 and sig1.
RULE 11: VSHA2MS.VV computes W[18] as sig1(W[16]) plus W[11] plus sig0(W[3]) plus W[2] using the SEW-dependent definitions of sig0 and sig1.
RULE 12: VSHA2MS.VV computes W[19] as sig1(W[17]) plus W[12] plus sig0(W[4]) plus W[3] using the SEW-dependent definitions of sig0 and sig1.
RULE 13: After computing W[16] through W[19], VSHA2MS.VV writes four SEW-sized elements to vd containing the concatenation of W[19], W[18], W[17], and W[16] for the current element group.
RULE 14: When SEW is 32, VSHA2MS.VV defines sig0(x) as ROTR(x,7) XOR ROTR(x,18) XOR SHR(x,3) and sig1(x) as ROTR(x,17) XOR ROTR(x,19) XOR SHR(x,10).
RULE 15: When SEW is 64, VSHA2MS.VV defines sig0(x) as ROTR(x,1) XOR ROTR(x,8) XOR SHR(x,7) and sig1(x) as ROTR(x,19) XOR ROTR(x,61) XOR SHR(x,6).
RULE 16: VSHA2MS.VV defines ROTR(x,n) as (x right-shifted by n) OR (x left-shifted by (SEW-n)), where the shifts are performed on SEW-bit values.
RULE 17: VSHA2MS.VV defines SHR(x,n) as the logical right shift of the SEW-bit value x by n bits.

INSTRUCTION: VSLIDE1DOWN.VX
FORMAT: vslide1down.vx vd, vs2, rs1, vm
RULE 1: For each element index i where i < vstart, VSLIDE1DOWN.VX must leave vd[i] unchanged.
RULE 2: For each element index i with vstart ≤ i < vl-1, VSLIDE1DOWN.VX must set vd[i] to the value of vs2[i+1] if the corresponding mask bit v0.mask[i] is enabled.
RULE 3: For element index i = vl-1, VSLIDE1DOWN.VX must write the scalar integer register x[rs1] (after XLEN/SEW conversion) to vd[vl-1] if the mask bit v0.mask[vl-1] is enabled.
RULE 4: If the mask bit for element index vl-1 is not enabled, VSLIDE1DOWN.VX must not modify vd[vl-1] and must follow the current mask-agnostic/undisturbed policy.
RULE 5: For each element index i where vl ≤ i < VLMAX, VSLIDE1DOWN.VX must handle the element according to the current tail policy.
RULE 6: When XLEN < SEW, VSLIDE1DOWN.VX must sign-extend the value from x[rs1] to SEW bits before writing it to the destination element.
RULE 7: When XLEN > SEW, VSLIDE1DOWN.VX must copy only the least-significant SEW bits of x[rs1] to the destination element and ignore the remaining high bits.
RULE 8: VSLIDE1DOWN.VX must not access memory during its execution.
RULE 9: VSLIDE1DOWN.VX must not alter any vector register other than the destination register group vd.

INSTRUCTION: VSLIDE1UP.VX
FORMAT: vslide1up.vx vd, vs2, rs1, vm
RULE 1: VSLIDE1UP.VX must place the scalar integer register rs1 value into element 0 of the destination vector register group vd when element 0 is active, otherwise element 0 is updated according to the current mask-agnostic/undisturbed policy.
RULE 2: VSLIDE1UP.VX sign-extends the rs1 value to SEW bits before writing to vd[0] when XLEN < SEW, and copies the least-significant SEW bits of rs1 to vd[0] while ignoring the high XLEN-SEW bits when XLEN > SEW.
RULE 3: For each active element i satisfying 0 ≤ i < vl-1, VSLIDE1UP.VX copies the source vector element vs2[i] into the destination element vd[i+1], implementing a one-element upward shift for the remaining active elements.
RULE 4: VSLIDE1UP.VX does not update any destination elements with index i ≥ vl; such elements are handled according to the current tail policy.
RULE 5: VSLIDE1UP.VX requires that the destination vector register group vd does not overlap the source vector register group vs2, and if overlap occurs the encoding of VSLIDE1UP.VX is reserved.

INSTRUCTION: VSLIDEDOWN.VI
FORMAT: vslidedown.vi vd, vs2, zimm5, vm
RULE 1: VSLIDEDOWN.VI writes at most the number of destination elements indicated by the current vl register.
RULE 2: VSLIDEDOWN.VI treats destination elements with index greater than or equal to vl according to the current tail policy.
RULE 3: VSLIDEDOWN.VI forms the source start index OFFSET by zero-extending the 5-bit immediate to XLEN bits, and does not truncate OFFSET to SEW bits when XLEN is larger than SEW.
RULE 4: For VSLIDEDOWN.VI, when vstart is less than vl and an element index i satisfies 0 ≤ i+OFFSET < VLMAX, the source element src[i] is taken from vs2 element i+OFFSET.
RULE 5: For VSLIDEDOWN.VI, when vstart is less than vl and an element index i satisfies i+OFFSET ≥ VLMAX, the source element src[i] is zero.
RULE 6: For VSLIDEDOWN.VI, when vstart is less than vl and an element index i satisfies 0 ≤ i < vstart, the destination element vd[i] remains unchanged.
RULE 7: For VSLIDEDOWN.VI, when vstart is less than vl and an element index i satisfies vstart ≤ i < vl, the destination element vd[i] is written with src[i] only if the corresponding mask bit v0.mask[i] is enabled.
RULE 8: For VSLIDEDOWN.VI, when vstart is less than vl and an element index i satisfies vl ≤ i < VLMAX, the destination element follows the current tail policy.

INSTRUCTION: VSLIDEDOWN.VX
FORMAT: vslidedown.vx vd, vs2, rs1, vm
RULE 1: VSLIDEDOWN.VX computes each destination element i as vd[i] = vs2[i + OFFSET] where OFFSET is the unsigned integer value read from integer register rs1.
RULE 2: VSLIDEDOWN.VX uses the full XLEN-wide unsigned value in rs1 as OFFSET and does not truncate OFFSET to SEW bits even when XLEN > SEW.
RULE 3: VSLIDEDOWN.VX limits the number of destination elements that may be written to the current value of vl; elements with indices i ≥ vl are not written directly by the instruction.
RULE 4: For each element i with vstart ≤ i < vl and 0 ≤ i + OFFSET < VLMAX, VSLIDEDOWN.VX sets src[i] = vs2[i + OFFSET].
RULE 5: For each element i with vstart ≤ i < vl and i + OFFSET ≥ VLMAX, VSLIDEDOWN.VX sets src[i] = 0.
RULE 6: For each element i where 0 ≤ i < vstart, VSLIDEDOWN.VX leaves vd[i] unchanged.
RULE 7: For each element i where vstart ≤ i < vl and the mask bit v0.mask[i] is enabled, VSLIDEDOWN.VX writes vd[i] = src[i]; if the mask bit is disabled, vd[i] remains unchanged.
RULE 8: For each element i where vl ≤ i < VLMAX, VSLIDEDOWN.VX processes vd[i] according to the current tail policy.
RULE 9: If vstart ≥ vl, VSLIDEDOWN.VX performs no element writes to vd and applies only the tail policy to elements i ≥ vl.

INSTRUCTION: VSLIDEUP.VI
FORMAT: vslideup.vi vd, vs2, zimm5, vm
RULE 1: The immediate operand uimm for VSLIDEUP.VI is a 5-bit unsigned value that is zero-extended to XLEN bits.
RULE 2: For VSLIDEUP.VI, if XLEN is greater than SEW, the OFFSET derived from uimm is not truncated to SEW bits.
RULE 3: For VSLIDEUP.VI the architectural value of vl determines the maximum number of destination elements that may be written.
RULE 4: For VSLIDEUP.VI destination elements with indices from OFFSET through vl-1 are written only if the corresponding mask bit is enabled and OFFSET < vl.
RULE 5: For VSLIDEUP.VI when vstart < vl, each element i satisfying 0 ≤ i < min(vl, max(vstart, OFFSET)) is written as vd[i] = vs2[i-OFFSET] provided the mask for element i is enabled.
RULE 6: For VSLIDEUP.VI when vstart < vl, each element i satisfying max(vstart, OFFSET) ≤ i < vl remains unchanged.
RULE 7: If an element index i satisfies vl ≤ i < VLMAX during VSLIDEUP.VI, then the resulting value of destination element i is undefined (any value is permitted) and no exception is raised.
RULE 8: The destination vector register group used by VSLIDEUP.VI must not overlap the source vector register group; overlapping encodings are reserved.
RULE 9: For VSLIDEUP.VI if the mask bit for a destination element is disabled, that element is not written and retains its previous value.

INSTRUCTION: VSLIDEUP.VX
FORMAT: vslideup.vx vd, vs2, rs1, vm
RULE 1: VSLIDEUP.VX uses the unsigned integer value in the integer register specified by rs1 as the start index OFFSET for the destination.
RULE 2: If XLEN is greater than SEW, VSLIDEUP.VX does not truncate the OFFSET value to SEW bits.
RULE 3: VSLIDEUP.VX writes at most the number of elements specified by the current vl value.
RULE 4: VSLIDEUP.VX writes destination elements with indices from OFFSET up to vl-1 when those elements are unmasked and OFFSET is less than vl.
RULE 5: When vstart < vl, VSLIDEUP.VX sets each destination element with index i satisfying 0 ≤ i < min(vl, max(vstart, OFFSET)) to the value of the source element at index i-OFFSET if the mask bit for element i is enabled.
RULE 6: For indices i satisfying max(vstart, OFFSET) ≤ i < vl, VSLIDEUP.VX leaves the destination element unchanged.
RULE 7: For indices i satisfying vl ≤ i < VLMAX, VSLIDEUP.VX applies the tail-policy to the destination element.
RULE 8: The destination vector register group for VSLIDEUP.VX must not overlap the source vector register group; overlapping encodings are reserved.

INSTRUCTION: VSLL.VI
FORMAT: vsll.vi vd, vs2, zimm5, vm
RULE 1: VSLL.VI shifts each element of the vector register group identified by the vs2 operand left by a number of bit positions and writes the shifted result to the vector destination register group identified by the vd operand.
RULE 2: VSLL.VI obtains the shift-amount value from the 5-bit immediate field uimm, which is zero-extended before use.
RULE 3: VSLL.VI uses only the low lg2(SEW) bits of the zero-extended immediate value to control the element shift amount, where SEW is the configured element width.

INSTRUCTION: VSLL.VV
FORMAT: vsll.vv vd, vs2, vs1, vm
RULE 1: VSLL.VV uses only the low lg2(SEW) bits of each shift-amount value to determine the amount by which the corresponding element of vs2 is shifted.
RULE 2: VSLL.VV performs a logical shift left on each element of vs2 by the element-wise shift amount taken from vs1 and writes the resulting elements to the destination vector register group vd.
RULE 3: VSLL.VV does not perform sign-extension or zero-extension of the shifted data; the shift is purely logical.

INSTRUCTION: VSLL.VX
FORMAT: vsll.vx vd, vs2, rs1, vm
RULE 1: VSLL.VX obtains the shift-amount value from the scalar integer register identified by the rs1 field.
RULE 2: VSLL.VX uses only the low lg2(SEW) bits of the value read from rs1 as the effective shift amount for the logical left shift.
RULE 3: VSLL.VX logically shifts each element of the source vector vs2 left by the effective shift amount and writes the resulting elements to the destination vector register group specified by vd.

INSTRUCTION: VSM3C.VI
FORMAT: vsm3c.vi vd, vs2, zimm5
RULE 1: If VSM3C.VI is executed with source registers holding a valid SM3 state and message block, then the destination register contains the SM3 compression result for those inputs.
RULE 2: If VSM3C.VI is executed on a vector element group that is not exactly eight 32‑bit elements (i.e., not 256 bits), then an illegal‑instruction exception is raised.
RULE 3: If VSM3C.VI does not process exactly vl divided by the element‑group size (EGS) element groups, then the destination vector register will have fewer than vl elements updated—the elements beyond the processed groups retain their original values.
RULE 4: VSM3C.VI is reserved when the selected element width (SEW) is not 32.
RULE 5: VSM3C.VI is reserved when the destination register group (vd) overlaps the source register group (vs2).
RULE 6: VSM3C.VI causes an illegal-instruction exception with RETIRE_FAIL if LMUL × VLEN is less than the element group width (EGW) of 256.
RULE 7: VSM3C.VI byte-swaps each 32-bit input word from big-endian to little-endian before any SM3 compression computation.
RULE 8: VSM3C.VI uses the 5-bit unsigned immediate rnds (values 0-31) to select the pair of SM3 rounds numbered 2 × rnds and 2 × rnds + 1.
RULE 9: If VSM3C.VI uses a T_j value other than 0x79CC4519 when j ≤ 15, or other than 0x7A879D8A when j > 15, then the destination register holds a value that does not match the correct SM3 compression output.
RULE 10: If VSM3C.VI is executed with a round index j ≤ 15, the instruction’s output must be the result of applying the GG1 Boolean function to its inputs; if j > 15, the output must be the result of applying the GG2 Boolean function to its inputs.
RULE 11: If VSM3C.VI does not compute the intermediate values ss1, ss2, tt1, tt2 and update the state words by applying the rotations and the functions FF_j, GG_j, P_0, and ROL32 exactly as defined in the pseudo‑code, then the architectural registers holding the state words will contain values that differ from the reference pseudo‑code results.
RULE 12: VSM3C.VI writes the eight resulting 32-bit state words to vd after byte-swapping each word from little-endian back to big-endian.
RULE 13: VSM3C.VI must process each element group i from eg_start to eg_len − 1, where eg_len = vl/EGS and eg_start = vstart/EGS.

INSTRUCTION: VSM3ME.VV
FORMAT: vsm3me.vv vd, vs2, vs1
RULE 1: VSM3ME.VV must raise an illegal-instruction exception and retire with failure when LMUL multiplied by VLEN is less than the effective group width of 256 bits.
RULE 2: VSM3ME.VV must retire successfully when LMUL multiplied by VLEN is greater than or equal to the effective group width of 256 bits.
RULE 3: VSM3ME.VV encoding is reserved when the selected standard element width (SEW) is any value other than 32 bits.
RULE 4: VSM3ME.VV encoding is reserved when the destination register group overlaps the source register group vs2.

INSTRUCTION: VSM4K.VI
FORMAT: vsm4k.vi vd, vs2, zimm5
RULE 1: If VSM4K.VI is executed on an element group that is not 128 bits (i.e., not exactly four 32‑bit elements), then it raises an illegal‑instruction exception.
RULE 2: If VSM4K.VI processes a number of element groups different from vl / EGS, then the destination register’s value does not match the value that would be produced by processing exactly vl / EGS groups.
RULE 3: If vl is not a multiple of 4, then executing VSM4K.VI raises an illegal‑instruction exception.
RULE 4: If vstart is not a multiple of 4, then executing VSM4K.VI raises an illegal‑instruction exception.

INSTRUCTION: VSM4R.VS
FORMAT: vsm4r.vs vd, vs2
RULE 1: VSM4R.VS requires the scalar element group operand to have EMUL equal to ceil(128 / VLEN).
RULE 2: If the destination register group vd overlaps the source register group containing vs2, then executing VSM4R.VS raises an illegal‑instruction exception (mcause = 2).
RULE 3: If the value in vl is not an integer multiple of 4 when executing VSM4R.VS, then the instruction raises an illegal‑instruction exception (mcause = 2).
RULE 4: If vstart is not a multiple of 4 when VSM4R.VS is executed, the instruction raises an illegal‑instruction exception (mcause = 2).
RULE 5: If vl or vstart is not an integer multiple of 4 for VSM4R.VS, the instruction encoding is reserved.
RULE 6: VSM4R.VS requires SEW to be 32; any other SEW value makes the VSM4R.VS encoding reserved.
RULE 7: For VSM4R.VS, LMUL multiplied by VLEN must be at least 128, otherwise an illegal-instruction exception is raised even when vl = 0.
RULE 8: For implementations with VLEN ≥ 128, VSM4R.VS does not require its scalar element group operand to be aligned to LMUL.
RULE 9: If the selected element width (SEW) for VSM4R.VS is any value other than 32, the encoding of VSM4R.VS is reserved.
RULE 10: If the destination register group (vd) overlaps the source register group (vs2) for the .vs form of VSM4R.VS, the encoding of VSM4R.VS is reserved.
RULE 11: If LMUL × VLEN is less than the effective group width (EGW) of 128, VSM4R.VS raises an illegal-instruction exception and retires with failure.
RULE 12: For each element index i, VSM4R.VS computes B = x2 ^ x3 ^ x4 ^ rk1, applies the SM4 sub-word transformation to B producing S, and then computes x5 = sm4_round(x1, S).
RULE 13: For each element index i, VSM4R.VS computes B = x3 ^ x4 ^ x5 ^ rk2, applies the SM4 sub-word transformation to B producing S, and then computes x6 = sm4_round(x2, S).
RULE 14: If VSM4R.VS is executed, then the destination register x7 must contain SM4Round(x3, SM4SubWord(x4 ⊕ x5 ⊕ x6 ⊕ rk3)); otherwise the architectural state is incorrect (x7 holds a different value).
RULE 15: After computing x4, x5, x6, and x7 for element index i, VSM4R.VS writes the concatenated 128-bit value (x7 @ x6 @ x5 @ x4) back to element i of the destination register group vd.

INSTRUCTION: VSM4R.VV
FORMAT: vsm4r.vv vd, vs2
RULE 1: VSM4R.VV must raise an illegal-instruction exception and cause RETIRE_FAIL when LMUL multiplied by VLEN is less than 128 bits.
RULE 2: For each element-group index i processed by VSM4R.VV, the instruction writes the 128-bit vector composed of x7, x6, x5, and x4 back to Vd element group i.
RULE 3: After VSM4R.VV has processed all element groups, the instruction must retire successfully (RETIRE_SUCCESS).
RULE 4: In the VSM4R.VV .vv form, the round keys are consumed from Vs2 in the natural order rk0, rk1, rk2, rk3, which distinguishes encryption from decryption.

INSTRUCTION: VSM.V
FORMAT: vsm.v vs3, (rs1)
RULE 1: VSM.V stores a byte vector of length ceil(vl/8) elements to the memory address specified by rs1, using an element width of 8 bits (EEW=8).
RULE 2: VSM.V operates with the same semantics as an unmasked byte store (EEW=8) for the computed effective vector length.
RULE 3: VSM.V interprets the architectural vstart value as a byte count, so vstart advances by one for each byte element processed.
RULE 4: VSM.V respects the vill field of vtype, and execution of VSM.V is illegal when vtype.vill is set.
RULE 5: For VSM.V the effective vector length is evl = ceil(vl/8), corresponding to EMUL = 1.

INSTRUCTION: VSMUL.VV
FORMAT: vsmul.vv vd, vs2, vs1, vm
RULE 1: VSMUL.VV sets the vxsat flag if any element's result is saturated during the operation.
RULE 2: VSMUL.VV MUST NOT be included for EEW = 64 in any Zve64* extension.
RULE 3: VSMUL.VV MUST be available for EEW = 8, 16, and 32 in every Zve* extension.
RULE 4: If a VSMUL.VV execution encounters any trap condition, then a precise trap is taken: the instruction does not retire, PC is set to the trap handler address, and mcause contains the corresponding exception code.

INSTRUCTION: VSMUL.VX
FORMAT: vsmul.vx vd, vs2, rs1, vm
RULE 1: If VSMUL.VX is executed, then each SEW‑width element of the destination vector register contains the signed product of the corresponding element of vs2 and the signed value in x[rs1].
RULE 2: When VSMUL.VX executes, each element i computes a signed product of vs2[i] and the signed scalar x[rs1] using a double‑width (2 × SEW) intermediate result.
RULE 3: VSMUL.VX shifts each 2 × SEW intermediate product right by SEW-1 bits.
RULE 4: VSMUL.VX saturates the rounded value so that the final result fits into SEW bits.
RULE 5: VSMUL.VX sets the vxsat bit when saturation occurs for any element.
RULE 6: VSMUL.VX writes each destination element vd[i] as clip(roundoff_signed(vs2[i] * x[rs1], SEW-1)), which is the saturated, rounded result of the signed multiplication.
RULE 7: VSMUL.VX is supported by every Zve* extension except that it is not included when the effective element width (EEW) is 64 in a Zve64* implementation.
RULE 8: VSMUL.VX executes with precise traps on all Zve* extensions.

INSTRUCTION: VSOXEI8.V
FORMAT: vsoxei8.v vs3, (rs1), vs2, vm
RULE 1: If VSOXEI8.V executes, then the source vector’s elements are written to memory at addresses base + index × (SEW/8) in increasing index order, and each stored element occupies exactly SEW bits (i.e., SEW/8 bytes).
RULE 2: VSOXEI8.V must use an 8-bit element width for the index as defined by the statically encoded EEW of 8 bits, independent of the data element width.
RULE 3: If VSOXEI8.V does not store its elements in program order, then after the instruction the memory bytes at the base address plus successive element offsets will not contain the corresponding elements of the source vector in the original order (i.e., the stored values appear out of order).

INSTRUCTION: VSOXEI16.V
FORMAT: vsoxei16.v vs3, (rs1), vs2, vm
RULE 1: VSOXEI16.V stores each element of vector register vs3 to the memory address obtained by adding the 16-bit index element from the corresponding element of vector register vs2 to the base address held in integer register rs1.
RULE 2: VSOXEI16.V interprets the statically encoded EEW field as the element width of the index (16 bits) rather than as the width of the stored data.

INSTRUCTION: VSOXEI32.V
FORMAT: vsoxei32.v vs3, (rs1), vs2, vm
RULE 1: VSOXEI32.V stores each element of the source vector register group vs2 to memory using an address formed by adding a 32-bit index element taken from the index vector vs3 to the base address held in rs1, and the store of each element must use the current SEW as the data width.
RULE 2: VSOXEI32.V performs its stores as ordered operations, so the stores must become globally visible in program order relative to other memory accesses.

INSTRUCTION: VSOXEI64.V
FORMAT: vsoxei64.v vs3, (rs1), vs2, vm
RULE 1: VSOXEI64.V must perform an ordered store of 64-bit elements whose data come from the vector register vs3 to memory addresses formed by adding the base address in integer register rs1 to the byte-offsets taken from the corresponding elements of the index vector vs2.

INSTRUCTION: VSRA.VI
FORMAT: vsra.vi vd, vs2, zimm5, vm
RULE 1: VSRA.VI MUST perform an arithmetic (sign-extending) right shift on each element of the source vector register group.
RULE 2: VSRA.VI MUST obtain its shift amount from a zero-extended 5-bit immediate field (uimm).
RULE 3: VSRA.VI MUST use only the low lg2(SEW) bits of the shift-amount value to determine the effective shift amount.
RULE 4: VSRA.VI MUST shift each element of the source vector right by the effective shift amount and must sign-extend the vacated bits.

INSTRUCTION: VSRA.VV
FORMAT: vsra.vv vd, vs2, vs1, vm
RULE 1: VSRA.VV MUST perform an arithmetic right shift with sign-extension on each element of the source vector.
RULE 2: VSRA.VV MUST obtain the shift-amount values from the vector register group specified by vs1.
RULE 3: VSRA.VV MUST use only the low lg2(SEW) bits of each shift-amount element to determine the shift amount applied to the corresponding element.

INSTRUCTION: VSRA.VX
FORMAT: vsra.vx vd, vs2, rs1, vm
RULE 1: VSRA.VX performs a sign-extending arithmetic right shift on each element of the source vector register group vs2.
RULE 2: VSRA.VX obtains the shift-amount value from the scalar integer register rs1.
RULE 3: VSRA.VX uses only the low lg2(SEW) bits of the rs1 value to control the shift amount, and ignores all higher bits.
RULE 4: VSRA.VX writes the shifted result of each element into the corresponding element of the destination vector register group vd.

INSTRUCTION: VSRL.VI
FORMAT: vsrl.vi vd, vs2, zimm5, vm
RULE 1: VSRL.VI performs a logical right shift on each element of the source vector register group vs2, zero-extending the vacated bits, and writes the result to the destination vector register group vd.
RULE 2: VSRL.VI obtains its shift-amount value from a zero-extended 5-bit immediate field named uimm.
RULE 3: VSRL.VI masks the shift-amount value by using only the low lg2(SEW) bits of the zero-extended immediate to determine the actual shift amount for each element.
RULE 4: VSRL.VI applies the vector mask vm such that the shift operation is performed only on elements whose mask bit is true, while elements with a false mask bit are left unchanged.
RULE 5: VSRL.VI writes the shifted element results to the vector register group specified by vd.

INSTRUCTION: VSRL.VV
FORMAT: vsrl.vv vd, vs2, vs1, vm
RULE 1: VSRL.VV obtains each element's shift amount from the corresponding element of the vs1 vector register group.
RULE 2: VSRL.VV uses only the low lg2(SEW) bits of each shift-amount element to control the shift amount for that element.
RULE 3: VSRL.VV performs a logical right shift on each element and fills the vacated bit positions with zeros (zero-extension).

INSTRUCTION: VSRL.VX
FORMAT: vsrl.vx vd, vs2, rs1, vm
RULE 1: VSRL.VX performs a zero-extending logical right shift on each element of the source vector register group specified by vs2.
RULE 2: VSRL.VX writes the result of the shift to the destination vector register group specified by vd.
RULE 3: VSRL.VX uses only the low lg2(SEW) bits of the value read from rs1 to determine the shift amount applied to each element.
RULE 4: VSRL.VX treats the shift operation as logical, inserting zeros into the most-significant bits vacated by the shift.

INSTRUCTION: VSSE8.V
FORMAT: vsse8.v vs3, (rs1), rs2, vm
RULE 1: VSSE8.V supports negative stride values.
RULE 2: VSSE8.V supports a stride value of zero.
RULE 3: If rs2 is not x0 and the integer register x[rs2] contains zero, VSSE8.V must perform one memory access for each active element, though the accesses remain unordered.
RULE 4: If a VSSE8.V instruction accesses a memory region that is not idempotent under RVWMO and the implementation omits any of the instruction’s defined memory accesses, then the architectural state will not reflect those accesses (e.g., the destination register or memory locations will contain incorrect values), whereas omission is permitted only for accesses to idempotent memory regions where no architectural change would be observable.
RULE 5: If a VSSE8.V instruction accesses a non‑idempotent memory region and omits any element store, then the omitted memory byte retains its pre‑instruction value (the store does not occur).

INSTRUCTION: VSSE16.V
FORMAT: vsse16.v vs3, (rs1), rs2, vm
RULE 1: VSSE16.V supports negative stride values and also a stride value of zero.
RULE 2: If VSSE16.V writes to a memory address that other agents can observe (i.e., not an idempotent region as defined by RVWMO), then a load performed by another agent from that address after the instruction must return the value written by VSSE16.V.

INSTRUCTION: VSSE32.V
FORMAT: vsse32.v vs3, (rs1), rs2, vm
RULE 1: VSSE32.V supports negative stride values and a stride value of zero.
RULE 2: VSSE32.V implementations must appear to perform each memory access mandated by the instruction semantics.

INSTRUCTION: VSSE64.V
FORMAT: vsse64.v vs3, (rs1), rs2, vm
RULE 1: VSSE64.V supports negative and zero strides.
RULE 2: If VSSE64.V is executed with rs2 ≠ x0 and the stride register x[rs2] contains zero, then the implementation must issue one memory write for each active element (all writes target the same address), so the target memory location ends up holding the last element’s value and the total number of memory writes equals the number of active elements.
RULE 3: If a VSSE64.V instruction does not perform a memory write for any of its element addresses, then the corresponding memory location retains its original value (observable by a subsequent load), indicating the required memory access was omitted.
RULE 4: If a VSSE64.V instruction accesses a non‑idempotent memory region, then every element store must be performed; the device mapped to that region must observe the full sequence of writes (e.g., its status register reflects the expected number of stores).

INSTRUCTION: VSSEG3E32.V
FORMAT: vsseg3e32.v vs3, (rs1), vm
RULE 1: VSSEG3E32.V stores a packed vector consisting of three 4-byte segments to memory.
RULE 2: VSSEG3E32.V writes the packed segments to memory beginning at the address held in rs1.

INSTRUCTION: VSSRA.VI
FORMAT: vssra.vi vd, vs2, zimm5, vm
RULE 1: VSSRA.VI uses a zero-extended 5-bit immediate (uimm) as the shift-amount operand.
RULE 2: VSSRA.VI determines the effective shift amount by taking the low lg2(SEW) bits of the immediate uimm and ignoring any higher bits.
RULE 3: For each element i of the source vector register group vs2 that is enabled by the vector mask vm, VSSRA.VI computes vd[i] as the result of a sign-extending arithmetic right shift of vs2[i] by the effective shift amount, with the bits shifted out rounded according to the current rounding mode vxrm.
RULE 4: VSSRA.VI updates only those destination elements vd[i] for which the corresponding mask bit vm[i] is true; elements with vm[i] false are left unchanged.
RULE 5: The rounding of the bits shifted out by VSSRA.VI follows the rounding mode specified by the vector rounding mode register vxrm.
RULE 6: The sign-extending arithmetic right shift performed by VSSRA.VI replicates the sign bit of the original element into the vacated high-order bits.

INSTRUCTION: VSSRA.VV
FORMAT: vssra.vv vd, vs2, vs1, vm
RULE 1: VSSRA.VV performs a scaling right arithmetic shift that sign-extends the shifted value.
RULE 2: VSSRA.VV takes the shift-amount value for each element from the corresponding element of the vector register group specified by vs1.
RULE 3: VSSRA.VV uses only the low lg2(SEW) bits of each element of the vs1 shift-amount value to determine the shift amount for that element.
RULE 4: VSSRA.VV rounds the bits shifted out of each element according to the current vxrm rounding mode.
RULE 5: VSSRA.VV computes vd[i] = roundoff_signed(vs2[i], vs1[i]) for each element i, where roundoff_signed denotes a sign-extending arithmetic right shift of vs2[i] by the low lg2(SEW) bits of vs1[i] with rounding applied as defined by vxrm.

INSTRUCTION: VSSRL.VI
FORMAT: vssrl.vi vd, vs2, zimm5, vm
RULE 1: VSSRL.VI vd, vs2, uimm, vm must compute each destination element as vd[i] = roundoff_unsigned(vs2[i], uimm).
RULE 2: VSSRL.VI must shift each source element right by a shift amount equal to the low lg2(SEW) bits of the zero-extended 5-bit immediate uimm.
RULE 3: VSSRL.VI must produce a zero-extending logical shift right result for each element.
RULE 4: VSSRL.VI must apply element-wise masking as indicated by the vm field, leaving masked-off destination elements unchanged.
RULE 5: VSSRL.VI must obtain the shift amount from a zero-extended 5-bit immediate field named uimm.

INSTRUCTION: VSSRL.VV
FORMAT: vssrl.vv vd, vs2, vs1, vm
RULE 1: VSSRL.VV must shift each element of the source vector register group specified by vs2 right by a per-element amount taken from the corresponding element of the vector register group specified by vs1 and must round the shifted-out bits according to the current rounding mode (vxrm).
RULE 2: VSSRL.VV must produce zero-extended results, i.e., the high bits of each destination element are filled with zeros after the shift-right logical operation.
RULE 3: VSSRL.VV must obtain the shift-amount value for each element from the vector register group identified by the vs1 operand.
RULE 4: VSSRL.VV must use only the low lg2(SEW) bits of each shift-amount element to determine the effective shift amount for the corresponding element.
RULE 5: VSSRL.VV must write each resulting element to the destination vector register group vd, and the write must be masked by the optional mask vm so that elements for which vm is false remain unchanged.
RULE 6: For every element index i, VSSRL.VV must set vd[i] equal to roundoff_unsigned(vs2[i], vs1[i]), where roundoff_unsigned implements a logical right shift of vs2[i] by the effective shift amount derived from vs1[i] and applies rounding according to vxrm.

INSTRUCTION: VSSRL.VX
FORMAT: vssrl.vx vd, vs2, rs1, vm
RULE 1: VSSRL.VX writes each element of the destination vector register group vd with the result of the scaling shift right logical operation.
RULE 2: VSSRL.VX obtains the shift-amount value from scalar integer register rs1 and uses only the low lg2(SEW) bits of that value to control the shift amount.
RULE 3: VSSRL.VX performs a logical right shift on each element of vs2 by the amount specified by the low lg2(SEW) bits of rs1, producing a zero-extended unsigned intermediate result.
RULE 4: VSSRL.VX rounds the bits shifted out of each element according to the current vxrm rounding mode.
RULE 5: VSSRL.VX stores into each element vd[i] the value resulting from applying the unsigned rounding operation defined by the current vxrm setting to the logically right-shifted value of vs2[i] using the shift amount x[rs1].

INSTRUCTION: VSSSEG2E32.V
FORMAT: vssseg2e32.v vs3, (rs1), rs2, vm
RULE 1: VSSSEG2E32.V must support a byte-stride offset provided by rs2 that can be negative, zero, or positive.
RULE 2: For each element index i where 0 ≤ i < VL, VSSSEG2E32.V stores the element of the second segment (vector register v3[i]) to the memory address computed as rs1 + i * rs2 + 4 bytes.
RULE 3: For segment s of VSSSEG2E32.V where s is 0 or 1, the store address for element i is rs1 + i * rs2 + s * 4 bytes.

INSTRUCTION: VSSUB.VV
FORMAT: vssub.vv vd, vs2, vs1, vm
RULE 1: VSSUB.VV subtracts the signed integer element in source vector register vs2 from the signed integer element in source vector register vs1 for each element position and writes the resulting element to the corresponding position of destination vector register vd.
RULE 2: VSSUB.VV replaces the result of an element subtraction with the nearest representable signed integer value when the true difference exceeds the destination element's signed integer range.
RULE 3: VSSUB.VV sets the architectural vxsat flag when any element subtraction overflows and is saturated to the closest representable value.

INSTRUCTION: VSSUB.VX
FORMAT: vssub.vx vd, vs2, rs1, vm
RULE 1: VSSUB.VX reads the signed integer value from scalar register rs1 and treats it as the scalar operand for the subtraction.
RULE 2: For each element index i where the corresponding mask bit in vm is true, VSSUB.VX computes the signed subtraction result = vs2[i] − rs1.
RULE 3: For each element index i where the corresponding mask bit in vm is false, VSSUB.VX does not modify the destination element vd[i].
RULE 4: If the signed subtraction result for an element would exceed the representable range of the destination element type, VSSUB.VX saturates that element to the nearest representable value of that type.
RULE 5: When any element saturation occurs, VSSUB.VX sets the vxsat status bit.
RULE 6: If no element overflow occurs, VSSUB.VX leaves the vxsat status bit unchanged.

INSTRUCTION: VSSUBU.VV
FORMAT: vssubu.vv vd, vs2, vs1, vm
RULE 1: VSSUBU.VV performs a saturating subtraction of unsigned integer elements, subtracting each element of source vector register vs1 from the corresponding element of source vector register vs2 and writing the element result to destination vector register vd.
RULE 2: If an elementwise unsigned subtraction performed by VSSUBU.VV would produce a value outside the representable range of the destination element type, VSSUBU.VV replaces that element result with the nearest representable value and sets the vxsat bit in the vector status register.

INSTRUCTION: VSSUBU.VX
FORMAT: vssubu.vx vd, vs2, rs1, vm
RULE 1: VSSUBU.VX MUST subtract the unsigned integer scalar in integer register rs1 from each unsigned integer element of source vector register vs2 and write the unsigned integer results to destination vector register vd.
RULE 2: VSSUBU.VX MUST replace an element result with the closest representable unsigned integer value when the subtraction would overflow the destination element width.
RULE 3: VSSUBU.VX MUST set the vxsat flag when any element result is replaced because of overflow.
RULE 4: VSSUBU.VX MUST NOT set the vxsat flag when no element result overflows the destination element width.

INSTRUCTION: VSUXEI8.V
FORMAT: vsuxei8.v vs3, (rs1), vs2, vm
RULE 1: VSUXEI8.V stores 8-bit elements, where the element width equals the standard element width (SEW) of 8 bits.
RULE 2: VSUXEI8.V uses the scalar register rs1 as the base address for all memory stores.
RULE 3: VSUXEI8.V uses the vector register vs2 to provide a byte offset for each element's store address.
RULE 4: VSUXEI8.V uses the vector mask register vm to determine which elements are active for storing.
RULE 5: For each element i where vm[i] is true, VSUXEI8.V stores the 8-bit element from vs3[i] to the memory address computed as rs1 plus the byte offset vs2[i].
RULE 6: For each element i where vm[i] is false, VSUXEI8.V does not perform a store to memory for that element.

INSTRUCTION: VSUXEI16.V
FORMAT: vsuxei16.v vs3, (rs1), vs2, vm
RULE 1: VSUXEI16.V performs an unordered 16-bit indexed store of data elements whose size is the current SEW.
RULE 2: In VSUXEI16.V the suffix `ei` specifies that the statically encoded EEW applies to the index elements rather than to the stored data elements.

INSTRUCTION: VSUXEI32.V
FORMAT: vsuxei32.v vs3, (rs1), vs2, vm
RULE 1: VSUXEI32.V stores each element of vector register group vs3 to a memory address computed by adding the scalar register rs1 base address to a byte-offset taken from the corresponding element of vector register group vs2.
RULE 2: If a VSUXEI32.V instruction is decoded with an EEW field not equal to 32 bits, then the core raises an illegal‑instruction exception (mcause = 2).
RULE 3: VSUXEI32.V scatters elements from a source vector to memory locations whose addresses are computed by adding a base address to offset values supplied by an index vector, which must be appropriately scaled before the instruction executes.
RULE 4: In the form VSUXEI32.V v8, (a2), v16, v0.t, the vector register v8 provides the elements that are stored to memory.
RULE 5: In the form VSUXEI32.V v8, (a2), v16, v0.t, the vector register v16 supplies the offset values that are added to the base address to form the destination address for each element.
RULE 6: In the form VSUXEI32.V v8, (a2), v16, v0.t, the mask register v0.t causes VSUXEI32.V to store an element only when the corresponding mask bit is true.
RULE 7: VSUXEI32.V leaves memory unchanged for elements whose corresponding mask bit in the mask register is false.

INSTRUCTION: VSUXEI64.V
FORMAT: vsuxei64.v vs3, (rs1), vs2, vm
RULE 1: VSUXEI64.V is a vector indexed-unordered store instruction that writes SEW data to memory.
RULE 2: If VSUXEI64.V is executed, then for each element i where the mask vm[i] = 1, memory at address rs1 + (vs2[i] * 8) contains the 64‑bit value from vs3[i]; elements with vm[i] = 0 cause no store.
RULE 3: VSUXEI64.V stores 64-bit elements from vs3 to memory addresses computed by adding the base address in rs1 to each byte-offset element in vs2.
RULE 4: VSUXEI64.V uses the statically-encoded EEW field to define the element width of the index vector vs2, not the width of the stored data elements.
RULE 5: VSUXEI64.V applies the mask vm such that only elements with a true mask bit are written to memory, while elements with a false mask bit are not stored.
RULE 6: If a program expects the individual element stores of a VSUXEI64.V instruction to occur in program order, then the architecture provides no ordering guarantee and the memory locations may contain the stored values in any permutation.
RULE 7: The same opcode may be accepted as an alias written as VSXEI64.V, and executing that alias must produce the identical architectural effect as VSUXEI64.V.

INSTRUCTION: VSUXSEG2EI32.V
FORMAT: vsuxseg2ei32.v vs3, (rs1), vs2, vm
RULE 1: VSUXSEG2EI32.V stores a 32-bit word from the first element of the destination data register group (v2[i]) to the memory address obtained by adding the scalar base address in rs1 to the byte offset contained in the corresponding element of the index register group (v5[i]).
RULE 2: VSUXSEG2EI32.V stores a 32-bit word from the second element of the destination data register group (v3[i]) to the memory address obtained by adding the scalar base address in rs1 to the byte offset contained in the corresponding element of the index register group (v5[i]) plus 4 bytes.
RULE 3: VSUXSEG2EI32.V computes each target address by adding the scalar base address in rs1 to the byte offset given by the corresponding element of the index vector register group and, for subsequent fields within a segment, adds an additional offset equal to the field index multiplied by the element width (32 bits).
RULE 4: If the EMUL value of the index vector register group for VSUXSEG2EI32.V is not equal to (EEW / SEW) × LMUL (where EEW is the element width encoded in the instruction), then an illegal‑instruction exception is raised.
RULE 5: If EMUL × 2 exceeds 8 for VSUXSEG2EI32.V, then executing the instruction raises an illegal‑instruction exception (mcause = 2).
RULE 6: VSUXSEG2EI32.V permits the destination data register groups to overlap the source index register group because the overlap constraint that applies to indexed segment loads does not apply to stores.

INSTRUCTION: VWADD.VV
FORMAT: vwadd.vv vd, vs2, vs1, vm
RULE 1: VWADD.VV adds each pair of corresponding SEW-wide elements from source vector registers vs2 and vs1 after sign-extending both operands to 2 × SEW bits, producing a 2 × SEW-wide signed sum for each element.
RULE 2: VWADD.VV writes each 2 × SEW-wide sum result to the corresponding element of the destination vector register vd.
RULE 3: VWADD.VV uses the mask specified by the vm field to control element-wise activation of the addition operation.

INSTRUCTION: VWADD.VX
FORMAT: vwadd.vx vd, vs2, rs1, vm
RULE 1: vwadd.vx sign-extends each SEW-wide element of the source vector register vs2 and the SEW-wide scalar element in rs1 to double-width (2*SEW) before performing the addition.
RULE 2: For every element position where the mask vm is true, vwadd.vx adds the sign-extended vs2 element to the sign-extended rs1 element, producing a double-width result.
RULE 3: vwadd.vx writes the double-width result of each active element to the corresponding element of the destination vector register vd.
RULE 4: vwadd.vx leaves the elements of vd unchanged for element positions where the mask vm is false.
RULE 5: The result width produced by vwadd.vx is exactly twice the standard element width of the source operands (2*SEW).
RULE 6: When the scalar register rs1 is x0, vwadd.vx sign-extends the vs2 elements to double-width, adds zero, and therefore stores a double-width copy of the sign-extended vs2 elements in vd.

INSTRUCTION: VWADD.WX
FORMAT: vwadd.wx vd, vs2, rs1, vm
RULE 1: VWADD.WX adds a signed SEW-width scalar from integer register rs1 to each SEW-width element of the vector source vs2 and stores the 2*SEW-width results in the destination vector vd.
RULE 2: The destination element width of VWADD.WX is defined to be twice the standard element width (2*SEW).
RULE 3: VWADD.WX treats both the scalar operand and the vector source elements as signed integers during the addition.
